# Frontend UI Conventions

## Color tokens

- **Always use `slate`, never `gray`** — they are not interchangeable. `gray` is warmer/less blue-tinted and creates visible mismatch on adjacent surfaces in the dark theme.

**Three-level background depth system** — use these levels consistently, never flatten them:

| Level | Usage | Class |
|---|---|---|
| Deepest | Grid cells, page canvas | `bg-canvas` |
| Mid | Column headers, swimlane labels, card surfaces, panels | `bg-surface` |
| Elevated | Dropdowns, modals, popovers | `bg-surface` with `shadow-xl` |

- Borders: `border-line-subtle` for grid lines (subtle), `border-line` for panels, `border-line-strong` for interactive elements
- Text: `text-fg` for primary content, `text-fg-secondary` for secondary, `text-fg-muted` for muted/stats (not on the card face or the row-chip cluster, next bullet)
- **Dark `fg-muted` is below AA as text on `bg-surface` (3.07:1) and `fg-faint` far below (1.93:1; 2.56 light).** Informative text on the card face and in custom-field chips uses `text-fg-tertiary` (5.71 dark / 7.58 light) as its quietest tone, never `fg-muted` or `fg-faint` (#1862, guarded in `onTintTokens.test.ts`). The tokens themselves are unchanged; the app-wide question (retune dark `fg-muted` or keep promoting per use, plus informative `fg-faint` uses) is #2030. Elsewhere, prefer `text-fg-tertiary` for new informative text on `bg-surface`. Existing `text-fg-muted` prescriptions in this file stand until #2030 decides the token: do not migrate them piecemeal in an unrelated change, and never copy them onto the card face, its sibling surfaces (card peek, archived cards panel, swimlane field popover and edit row) or the swimlane row-chip cluster (chips, the `+N`/`All fields` trigger, the admin-only padlock), which follow the same floor.

- **Light `--warning` / `--success` are the -700 step (#2029).** The -600 step was 3.56 / 3.30:1 on white. Now `--warning` orange-700 `194 65 12` is 5.18:1 on `bg-surface`, 4.95 on `bg-sunken`, 4.73 on `bg-canvas` and `bg-surface-hover`; `--success` green-700 `21 128 61` 5.02 / 4.79 / 4.58 / 4.58. Dark is unchanged (orange-400 / green-400). Every `text-warning` / `text-success` on those surfaces clears AA, so no card-face or toast exemption remains. Consequences, measured: the `bg-<hue>/N` fills darken with the token, so on-tint text on them drops slightly (light, worst over canvas/sunken/surface/surface-active; on `bg-surface-active` the light warning/success `/20` fill with on-tint text is 4.51:1, a margin of about 0.01, recomputed by audit, so do not darken either token or the fill further): `/20` warning 5.26 → 5.01, success 5.27 → 5.00 (health floor re-pinned at 5.00); `/15` 5.59 → 5.39 and 5.56 → 5.36; `/30` warning 4.65 → 4.30 on canvas, so do not put `text-warning-on-tint` on `bg-warning/30` over `bg-canvas`. The aging ramp's stale fill moved to its own `--stale-tint` (the old orange) because on `bg-warning/15` at `opacity-85` the light stale badge (`warning-on-tint` text) fell to 4.44:1 (the card title holds 9.71); ramp ratios are unchanged. Known pre-existing defect, not caused here: the active Analytics tab's Beta badge (`bg-warning/30 text-warning-on-tint` on the `bg-primary` tab) is 1.30:1 light / 2.71 dark before #2029 and 1.17 light after (tracked as GitLab #2236; the Beta badge's warning `/20` fill with on-tint text over the hovered tab's `bg-surface-active` measures 4.505:1, a hair above AA).
- **`success-on-tint` and `info-on-tint` as toast text (#2060).** Measured from `index.css` with the WCAG formula (pinned in `onTintTokens.test.ts`). On `bg-surface`: `success-on-tint` 10.42:1 dark / 7.14:1 light, `info-on-tint` 8.11 / 8.72. On `bg-primary/15` over `bg-surface` (the `info` toast shell): `success-on-tint` 9.11 / 5.78, `info-on-tint` 7.09 / 7.07. All clear AA (4.5:1); no new token. `warning-on-tint` (8.67 / 7.31) and `danger-on-tint` (7.71 / 8.31) on `surface` are recorded in § System signals departure 2.

## Section header actions

When a section header (e.g. "My Boards", "Groups") carries action buttons:
- Place them right-aligned in a `flex items-center gap-2` group
- Primary action (e.g. "+ New board"): primary button variant
- Secondary action (e.g. "Import"): secondary button variant — **not** `text-info`; that color is reserved for active filter/selection states
- Do **not** use a full-width dashed bottom button as the primary creation affordance when the section already has content — that pattern reads as an empty state

## Buttons

Three variants — use no others:

| Variant | Classes |
|---|---|
| Primary | `bg-button-primary hover:bg-button-primary-hover text-on-primary` |
| Secondary | bare text (`text-fg-secondary`) + `hover:text-fg hover:bg-surface-hover` |
| Icon-only | `hover:bg-surface-hover` with icon content |

- Consistent sizing: `px-3 py-1.5 text-sm rounded` for most buttons
- Focus state: `focus:outline-none focus:ring-2 focus:ring-primary-emphasis` (use `focus:ring-danger-emphasis` for danger buttons) — always use `focus:` not `focus-visible:` for consistency
- Danger variant (destructive actions): `bg-danger-bg hover:bg-danger-bg-hover text-on-danger`
- **Warning variant (reversible but consequential actions, e.g. Archive; also the GitLab sign-in button) (#2169):** `bg-warning-bg hover:bg-warning-bg-hover text-on-warning font-medium`. Never `text-fg` on `bg-warning-bg`: with the old amber-600 fill it was 2.58:1 in dark mode, and it is 3.56 light / 4.07 dark on amber-700. `--warning-bg` is amber-700 `180 83 9` in both themes and `--warning-bg-hover` amber-800 `146 64 14`; white text is 5.02:1 at rest and 7.09:1 on hover (amber-600 gave 3.19 at rest). Focus ring `focus:ring-warning-emphasis`, or `focus:ring-primary-emphasis` on the sign-in buttons, which match their siblings. In dark mode the fill is 2.91:1 against `bg-surface`, about the same as the danger fill (3.03); the label carries the control. Pinned in `onTintTokens.test.ts`, which also fails on any other text color on a `bg-warning-bg` fill in the four files that use one as a button.
- Disabled state: `disabled:opacity-40 disabled:cursor-not-allowed` — never `disabled:opacity-50`
- Primary and danger buttons always include `font-medium`
- Border radius: always `rounded` — never `rounded-lg` on buttons (sole exception: the file dropzone, see § Multi-step import wizards and file uploads)
- **Every icon-only button carries an `aria-label`, the focus ring above and `type="button"` (#1737).** `title` alone is not a reliable accessible name (Firefox and VoiceOver can skip it); keep `title` as the tooltip and add `aria-label`, naming the target in a repeated row (`Remove direct board role for <name>`, `Remove choice <name>`, `Decrease weight`). A glyph such as `✕`, `✎`, `−` or `+` is never a name. Audit with a multi-line-aware pass: a single-line `grep '<button'` misses a JSX button whose attributes span lines.

**Why `--button-primary` is a separate token from `--primary`:** CTAs use `blue-900` (`#1e3a8a`, 10.36:1 on white) in light mode for comfortable AAA contrast, while `--primary` stays `blue-600` for brand-tint surfaces (avatars, active tabs, toggle tracks, saved-filter tabs). Do not consolidate these back into a single token — issue #855 intentionally split them so primary CTAs read as strong actions without desaturating the app's blue accents. Dark mode assigns both tokens the same blue-600 value; the split only matters on light backgrounds. Never use `bg-primary` on a button-shaped element, and never use `text-fg` on a `bg-button-primary` fill — `text-on-primary` is the only permitted text color on primary CTAs.

- **Every per-row text-button action in an admin table must carry the full button compliance set — `rounded focus:outline-none focus:ring-2 focus:ring-primary-emphasis` (or `focus:ring-danger-emphasis` for a destructive action) — even though it renders as bare text, not a filled button.** The Admin → Users per-row actions cell (`AdminPage.tsx`) is the reference: as of #1291 only the two highest-frequency actions — Deactivate/Reactivate and Make/Demote admin — stay as inline bare-text buttons, with `focus:ring-danger-emphasis` reserved for Deactivate and Demote admin (the two that remove access). Every remaining low-frequency action (Grant/Revoke all-content, Force reset, Clear lockout, Restart onboarding tour) moved into that row's `OverflowMenu` — see § Admin table row overflow menu below. A bare-text action is still a real button and needs a visible focus indicator and consistent corner radius like any other (#1280).

## Admin table row overflow menu (#1291)

When an admin table row (e.g. Admin → Users) accumulates enough conditional per-row actions that a no-wrap `flex` cell starts depending on the table's `overflow-x-auto` wrapper to absorb the width, fold the low-frequency actions into an `OverflowMenu` rather than letting the row keep growing — the same fold used for the column kebab (§ Column kebab menu below), applied to a table row instead of a column header.

- **Keep inline only the actions used most often** — for Admin → Users that's Deactivate/Reactivate and Make/Demote admin. Everything else (Grant/Revoke all-content, Force reset, Clear lockout, Restart onboarding tour) is a support action reached far less often and belongs in the menu.
- **Trigger:** `OverflowMenu` with `ariaLabel={`More actions for ${displayName}`}` — unlike the column kebab, the admin-table trigger is **not** hover-revealed (`opacity-0 group-hover:opacity-100`); admin tables are keyboard/mouse-scannable lists, not a hover-dense canvas, so the trigger stays visible at rest like the other row buttons.
- **Build the items array inline in the row render** (not `useMemo`, since a table row isn't its own component the way `ColumnHeader` is) — the array is small (at most 4 items) and the row already re-renders on every users-list update.
- `OverflowMenu` itself renders nothing when a row's items array is empty — don't add a separate conditional to hide the trigger.

## Sidebar and tab navigation

- **A page-level sidebar's section buttons carry the focus ring and mark the active item (#439).** Add `focus:outline-none focus:ring-2 focus:ring-primary-emphasis` and `aria-current={active ? "page" : undefined}` (`type="button"`). The active fill (`bg-primary`) is a visual-only cue; `aria-current` is what tells a screen reader which section is showing. Applies to page-level sidebars only — adopters: the `AdminPage` and `SettingsPage` sidebars.
- **Tab strips inside a modal, panel or drawer follow their own ARIA pattern, not this one.** A strip that switches panels in place is a tab list (`role="tab"` + `aria-selected`, as in `CardDetail`); a strip of independent toggles uses `aria-pressed` (as in `BoardActivityDrawer`). `BoardSettingsModal`'s tab buttons predate this note and use `aria-current="true"`; leave them until that modal's tabs are reworked rather than mixing patterns piecemeal.

## Feature parity between self-service and admin-initiated equivalents

**Invite by email (#731, #1444).** `EmailInviteForm` is shared by the site, group and board surfaces; a new surface passes `surface`, `roleOptions` and `showLinkDivider` and must not fork the copy. Its pickers pass `SingleSelectDropdown`'s `ariaLabel` (`"Role"`, `"Expires"`) because the trigger's visible text is only the selected value.

**Board invite links (#439).** The UI noun for a board's shareable link is **"invite link"**, never "share link": the board already has a public, sign-in-free Share link in Settings → Sharing, and the two must never be confused. Invite links live in Settings → Members (the "New link" button sits on the "Pending invites" heading row), never in the Sharing tab. A board invite link **always expires** (1, 7 or 30 days, default 7) — the create form never offers "Never". Since #1727 the same holds for group invite links, and neither form offers the Admin role (a shareable link is a bearer credential); the group *email* invite form still offers Admin. Rows created before #1727 can still show "Never" and Admin, so the list keeps rendering both. When the site's `registration_mode` is not `open`, the create form says so (`New users can't sign up from an invite link on this site. Only people who already have an account can join with it.`), because a shareable link never creates an account on an invite-only or closed site.

**Inline revoke in an admin table row (#439).** In a `<table>` (Admin → Board Invites), the inline Confirm/Cancel prompt renders as an inserted `<tr><td colSpan={columnCount} className="px-4 pb-2.5">` directly below its row, never inside the Actions cell (which would stretch one column). The trigger's `aria-label` names both the item and its container (`Revoke invite {prefix} for {board}`), since many rows share the "Revoke" label.

When a feature has both a self-service control (Settings) and an admin-initiated equivalent (Admin panel) — e.g. "Restart onboarding tour" in `SettingsPage.tsx` and the matching per-user action in `AdminPage.tsx` — both use **identical action copy** and **the same success/error feedback pattern** (inline `text-success`/`text-danger` message, same auto-clear timing). Do not let the two surfaces drift into different wording or different feedback mechanisms for what is, from the affected user's perspective, the exact same state change (#1280).

## Inputs and textareas

- `bg-surface border border-line rounded px-3 py-1.5 text-sm text-fg-secondary`
- Focus ring: `focus:outline-none focus:ring-2 focus:ring-primary-emphasis focus:border-transparent`
- Placeholder: `placeholder-fg-muted`
- **Inputs in a row with `SingleSelectDropdown` triggers** (rule builders such as `LaneFilterPopover`, #2205/#2226) use `px-2 py-1 text-sm`, matching the trigger, rather than `px-3 py-1.5`, so the row's controls share one height. Standalone form inputs keep `px-3 py-1.5`.

## Dropdown menus

All dropdowns — `SelectDropdown` or hand-rolled — must follow this style:

**Trigger button**
- `bg-surface border rounded px-2 py-1 text-sm outline-none flex items-center gap-1 transition`
- Default state: `border-line-strong text-fg-secondary hover:border-line-emphasis`
- Open / active-filter state: `border-primary-soft text-info`

**Menu panel**
- `bg-surface border border-line-strong rounded-lg shadow-lg py-1`

**Menu items**
- `w-full text-left px-3 py-1.5 text-sm transition hover:bg-surface-hover`
- Default text: `text-fg-secondary`; selected/active: `text-info`

**Separators** (3D engraved effect — every adjacent item pair, all dropdowns):
```tsx
{i > 0 && (
  <div role="separator" className="mx-4">
    <div className="h-px bg-sunken" />
    <div className="h-px bg-surface-active/50" />
  </div>
)}
```
- Place before each item where `i > 0`; no manual `separatorBefore` prop needed
- `SelectDropdown` handles this automatically; hand-rolled dropdowns (e.g. FilterBar) must add it manually

**Disabled dropdowns must include an explanation** — when rendering `<SelectDropdown disabled={true}>`, always pass `disabledReason="..."`. The component renders it as a `title` attribute and `aria-label` on the trigger. A silently greyed-out control with no explanation is inaccessible and a dead end for all users.

**`SelectDropdown` supports external label and helper wiring (#1430).** Pass `id` so `<label htmlFor>` binds to the `role=combobox` button trigger, and `ariaDescribedBy={helperId}` to tie always-rendered helper text (`text-xs text-fg-muted`, with a stable `id`) to it. Use `ariaLabel` only when there is no visible label, because `aria-label` overrides the label association.

**Active-count badge on a "reveals sub-controls" trigger (#964).** A `CheckboxDropdown`/`SingleSelectDropdown` trigger may carry an active-count badge (`bg-primary-emphasis/20 text-info px-2 py-0.5 text-xs rounded-full`, `aria-hidden="true"`, count folded into the trigger's `aria-label` as `"{label}, {N} active"`) when the trigger's job is to reveal a variable set of sub-controls into the toolbar row rather than being itself a single filter. Precedent: `"+ Filter"` in `FilterBar` (`frontend/src/components/Board/FilterBar.tsx`), which replaced four always-visible facet dropdowns with one collapsed trigger; the badge counts facets that have an active *value*, independent of which facets are currently expanded — that "expanded" signal already has its own presentation (the trigger's own `border-primary-soft text-info` active state), so the badge stays a distinct, decoupled signal rather than duplicating it. Do not add this badge to a trigger that is itself a single filter (e.g. the Assignee/Label/Priority dropdowns it replaces) — those communicate their own active state via the existing border-color treatment and don't need a second indicator.

**A badge-carrying "reveals sub-controls" trigger must pin its own visible label, not let `selected` drive it.** `CheckboxDropdown`'s default `displayLabel` logic renders a generic `"{label}: {selected labels}"` summary once anything is selected — correct for a trigger that *is* a filter (Assignee, Label, Priority), wrong for one whose `selected` instead tracks which *other* controls are expanded (`"+ Filter"`), where it would produce `"+ Filter: Assignee, Label"` — a third, text-based signal on top of the border-color "expanded" state and the badge's "has a value" count, and one that can diverge from the trigger's `aria-label` (always built from the static `label`), a WCAG 2.5.3 Label-in-Name mismatch. Pass `hideSelectionSummary` on any such trigger to keep its visible text pinned to `label`. `badge={0}` and `badge={undefined}` both suppress the badge and its `aria-label` fold-in identically — the component gates both on the same truthy check, so a caller never needs to remember to pass `count || undefined`.

## Modals and dialogs

- **An inline confirm that replaces its trigger takes focus on open (#863 ux-review).** `useConfirmFocusReturn` covers the close; on open, focus moves into the confirm: its input when it has one (a password), else its primary button. The prompt text sits in a polite live region.
- **`aria-disabled` needs a handler guard (#863 ux-review).** A control kept focusable with `aria-disabled` (in-flight "Sending…", an inert Done) must ignore activation in its handler, because `aria-disabled` does not stop clicks or Enter. A result region is cleared when a new run starts, so an identical result is announced again.
- **Never fake a re-authentication (#863 ux-review).** A "Sign in again" or "Continue with SSO" control must perform a real re-auth round trip. If the surface cannot start one, say what the user should do in text and offer no button; a page reload is not a re-verify.
- **A scrollable code block is keyboard-reachable (#863 ux-review).** A `<pre>` with `overflow-x-auto` gets `tabIndex={0}`, `role="region"`, an `aria-label`, and the standard focus ring, so keyboard users can scroll it and assistive tech names it.
- **A confirm whose answer is a choice, not a yes (#2054).** The Confirm/Cancel word pair below is for a prompt with one outcome. When the user must pick between two outcomes, name each one on its own button, with the group layout of the turn-on confirm: `role="group"` labeled by a polite-live prompt, the consequence of each action in its own paragraph tied to that button with `aria-describedby`, and right-aligned Cancel (Secondary), the outcome that can break something (Danger) and the outcome that can't (Primary, which takes focus on open). Each consequence paragraph leads with its button's exact label. Every action button goes `aria-disabled` with a handler guard while the request runs, and only the clicked button swaps to the progress label, so focus stays put on failure. Reference: the rotate confirm in `WebhookDetail`.
- **Enter-to-submit and Escape ignore IME composition (#2167).** While an IME composition is active, Enter commits the candidate and Escape cancels it, so neither may submit a form or close a modal. `useEscapeStack` ignores a composing Escape itself; every text-input `onKeyDown` that submits on Enter must test `!isImeComposing(e)` from `src/utils/imeComposition.ts` (it also covers Safari's `keyCode === 229`). Test with `fireEvent.keyDown(input, { key: "Enter", isComposing: true })`.
- **Escape is swallowed while an inline confirm's request runs (#2054).** Consumed, silently and with no effect: the button holding focus already shows the request running, and closing the confirm would read as a cancel and hide a failure. Opening another confirm is refused for the same reason. Reference: `escapeConfirm` and `onConfirmChange` in `BoardSettingsWebhooksTab`.
- **A request whose response is the only copy of a secret holds every way out (#2054).** The server has already replaced the secret, and nothing will ever say the new one went unseen, so Close, the backdrop, Escape, a tab switch and Back all refuse while it runs, rather than prompting: there is nothing on screen yet to ask about. Escape on a running confirm follows the rule above; every other refusal, including Escape on a create, which has no confirm to swallow it, has to be noticed from the control that was refused, so it is a `role="alert"` banner at the top of the tab, scrolled into view, and keyed so that each refusal is announced again; a status slot at the bottom of the tab would leave a dead control. Focus stays where it is. A failure that isn't a plain refusal (no response, or a 5xx) says the change may have gone through and refetches, so the screen shows what the server has before anyone retries. A failed rotate also refetches on a 4xx, because that can be the webhook having been deleted elsewhere. Actions on one view are tracked per action, never in one shared in-flight slot: another action finishing must not re-arm a request still running. Reference: `guard` and `issuingSecret` in `BoardSettingsWebhooksTab`.
- **Confirm on a restoring action (#863, Part 8 departure 23).** The rule below confirms only the transition that removes access. Turning a webhook back on is confirmed anyway, because it resumes sending data off the server, can skip events (`cursor_expired`) or change the owner (`creator_lost_access`, which also asks for the current password, or a fresh SSO sign-in for an SSO-only account). The confirm says which of those happens. For an SSO-only account the take-over sends no password and the server checks for a recent provider sign-in; the UI says so and, when it is not recent, tells the user to sign in again with the provider rather than offering a button that cannot do it. Reference: `WebhookDetail`.
- Backdrop: `fixed inset-0 bg-backdrop/60 z-50`
- Panel: `bg-surface border border-line rounded-lg shadow-xl`
- Consistent padding: `p-6` for content, `pb-4` for header
- Close button: icon-only variant, top-right corner
- Footer layout: `flex items-center justify-end gap-3` — always `gap-3`, never `gap-2`
- **The backdrop dismisses only on a press that began on it (#2095).** A press that starts inside the panel and is released on the backdrop (a color picker drag, a text selection) fires its click on the backdrop, their common ancestor. `ModalWrapper` records the press origin on `mouseDown` and its click handler requires it. A test that clicks a `ModalWrapper` backdrop must `fireEvent.mouseDown` it first; `userEvent.click` already does.
- **Fixed-height tabbed modals** — when a modal contains tabs with variable content height, give the panel a fixed height (`h-[85vh] max-h-[640px] min-h-0`) rather than only a max-height. This prevents layout jumping between tabs. The scrollable content region uses `overflow-y-auto flex-1` and the panel uses `flex flex-col`. Never use `max-h` alone on a tabbed modal panel.
- **A scroll region inside a popover or dialog must be reachable and visible (#1455).** (a) Make it keyboard-focusable: `tabIndex={0}`, `role="region"`, an `aria-label`, and `focus:outline-none focus:ring-2 focus:ring-inset focus:ring-primary-emphasis`. When it overflows on open, focus it rather than the panel, since a focused ancestor scrolls the page instead. (b) Show an overflow cue: a `h-8 bg-gradient-to-t from-surface to-transparent pointer-events-none` fade pinned to its bottom, rendered only while `scrollTop + clientHeight < scrollHeight`. Never rely on the native scrollbar, which macOS hides. **The fade must never hide a focused control (#2180):** the region sets `scroll-pb-<n>` equal to the fade height (`scroll-pb-8` for the `h-8` fade), and the card detail body, whose fades are `h-6` top and `h-10` bottom, sets `scroll-pt-6 scroll-pb-10`; without it the browser scrolls a focused control to the region's edge, under the fade (Tab, arrow-key `focus()` and `scrollIntoView` all honor scroll-padding). A sticky fade inside padding adds the padding too (see #2179 below). Cover each new region with a Vitest class assertion that pins its `scroll-pb` class. Playwright short-viewport cases in `e2e/overflow-fade-focus.spec.ts` cover EditSwimlaneModal, CardDetail, EditColumnModal, CustomFieldQuickEditPopover, CollapsedFlyout, AnchoredMenuPanel (an assignee facet), SingleSelectDropdown (`portalMenu`) and MultiSelectDropdown (#2186); each was confirmed red with its `scroll-pb` class removed. Add one for a new host, using `walkClearOfFades`, which fails unless a fade was mounted at least once (so a case cannot pass because the list did not overflow) and stops once the fade unmounts at the end of the list. `SelectDropdown` has no browser case yet: its four role options only overflow a viewport near 150px tall, and reaching one needs board or group settings fixtures. The earlier "failed on every run" result came from a helper that treated an unmounted fade as a failure, so it is not evidence the case is impossible; its `scroll-pb-8` is pinned by Vitest only until someone adds the case. (c) A popover that closes on outside scroll with a capture-phase listener must ignore scrolls whose target is inside the panel, or scrolling its own list dismisses it. **Named exception to (a):** `MultiSelectDropdown`'s list is not a Tab stop, because it follows the ARIA combobox pattern: focus stays in the search combobox, whose arrow keys move `aria-activedescendant` and scroll the active option into view, and Tab there closes the menu. A second Tab stop on the list would break that keyboard model, not complete it. A popover whose list is reachable only by scrolling has no such alternative and is not covered by this exception.
- **Anchored `fixed` popovers are sized to content and placed from a measurement (#1455).** Cap the scroll region at the visible viewport with `visibleMaxHeight()` (see the visible-viewport rule below), not at a fixed rem height or a `calc(100vh - ...)`. Measure `offsetHeight` in `useLayoutEffect` and place the panel below the trigger, else above it, else pinned to the viewport's bottom edge. **Keep the side chosen at open for as long as the panel still fits there (#1457)**: a filter that shrinks the list must not flip an upward menu below the trigger, and an upward menu keeps its bottom edge at the trigger. Choose the side again only when the list grows past that edge — never slide a panel over its own trigger. Never estimate the height from an item count. Keep it `visibility: hidden` until placed, and dismiss on window `resize` and on any scroll outside the panel (capture phase, ignoring scrolls inside it per rule (c) above). **Move focus only after placement**, in an effect keyed on `top !== null`, never `useEffect(..., [])`: the hidden first commit's passive effects flush before the placement re-render, and browsers ignore `focus()` on a hidden element. jsdom does not, so assert on the panel's visibility at the moment `focus()` runs (see `swimlaneCustomFields.test.tsx`), not with `toHaveFocus()`. Reuse `useAnchoredPlacement` (placement with the side lock, hidden-until-placed `top`, and the `onResize`/`onOutsideScroll` dismissals) and `useOverflowFade` (the bottom-fade flag) from `src/hooks/` rather than re-deriving them; a popup that is not a React child (the Tiptap mention list in `RichTextEditor`) uses `placeFixedElement` from the same module, re-placed from a `ResizeObserver` because its list renders asynchronously. **Named exception to rule (a) (Tab stop):** a roving-focus `role="menu"` list (`SingleSelectDropdown`'s portal menu) is reached with Arrow/Home/End, so its scroll region is not a Tab stop; rule (b), the overflow cue, still applies. Named exceptions to (a): `AnchoredMenuPanel` (#1482; its content is Tab-reachable checkboxes and buttons that scroll into view on focus, so the region is not a second Tab stop. A portaled panel sits at the end of `<body>`, so Tab off its last control, or Shift+Tab off its first, closes the menu and refocuses the trigger (forward Tab then moves on to the next toolbar control; Shift+Tab stays on the trigger), keeping the order trigger -> panel -> next control (a FilterBar facet menu stays mounted on that close, so its trigger survives to take focus); Escape also returns focus to the trigger), MultiSelectDropdown and SelectDropdown. Both are focus-staying comboboxes driven by aria-activedescendant. Any such component MUST scroll the active option into view on every activeIndex change, or a keyboard user can move the highlight out of the capped list. **Named exception to the resize and scroll dismissal:** the custom color popover (`ColorSwatchPicker`, #2095) skips both while one of its own inputs has focus, because a phone keyboard opening resizes the viewport and scrolls the page exactly as the user starts to type, which would throw the draft away. **Named exception to rule (a) (Tab stop):** the same popover's scroll region takes no `tabIndex`, because every control inside it is a Tab stop and focus scrolls it; rule (b), the overflow cue, still applies. **Scroll padding under a sticky overflow fade includes the container's bottom padding (#2179).** A `sticky bottom-0` fade pins to the inside of the scroll container's padding, but `scroll-padding` is measured from the scrollport edge, so set `scroll-pb` to the fade height plus the container's bottom padding (the custom color popover's `h-8` fade plus `p-3` is `scroll-pb-11`), or focus lands under the fade. **Named exception to the scroll dismissal:** the mention list re-anchors to the live caret on an outside scroll instead of closing, because ProseMirror and the browser scroll the caret into view as the query is typed, which happens exactly when the caret is near an edge, so a scroll dismissal would close the list mid-word. It still ends on `resize`, through Tiptap's own exit path (a `{ exit: true }` meta transaction on its suggestion `PluginKey`, as Escape does), never by only hiding the element, which would leave the suggestion capturing Enter and the arrow keys. Adopters: `ColorSwatchPicker`'s custom color popover (#2095, non-portaled, so it stays inside a host modal's focus trap), `SwimlaneFieldsPopover`, `MultiSelectDropdown`, `SelectDropdown` (#1480: always portaled and anchored, so a role/option select in a scrolling modal, the Members tab last row included, no longer clips), `SingleSelectDropdown`'s `portalMenu` (#1478: it holds user-data choice lists, so the Edit Swimlane dropdown field no longer clips; the Row 2 toolbar pickers share the path), `CheckboxDropdown` (#1482: the board and Lens filter facets hold user-data lists such as assignees and labels, so the menu is a body portal capped at the viewport), `SavedFiltersDropdown` (#1482, same portal), the offboarding modal's transfer-owner search results in `AdminPage` (#1482: `mode="inline"`, because the board rows sit in an `overflow-hidden` list that clipped the in-flow menu), the three `MovementHistoryView` filters (#1482: `portalMenu`; the User list is board members and the view's panel is `overflow-hidden`), a menu inside the card sheet (#1482: `AnchoredMenuPanel` `mode="inline"`, `fixed` but kept in the card sheet's DOM, not a body portal, because the sheet's focus trap and click-outside dismissal treat a portal as outside the dialog; all three share `AnchoredMenuPanel`), `CustomFieldQuickEditPopover`, `CollapsedFlyout`, `ColumnPolicyPopover`, `HealthStatusPopover`, `CardContextMenu`, `OverflowMenu` (also a per-row menu in the admin user table, so a bottom-row kebab opens upward), the `BoardSettingsModal` member-search suggestions, `CardPeekPopover` (`mode: "side"`, no dismissals since mouse-leave closes it) and the `RichTextEditor` mention list. **Exempt, audited in #1457 because their content is bounded:** `SplitButton` (a fixed handful of menu items, used only in the Row 2 board and Lens toolbars near the top of the viewport); `Tooltip`, `RoleInfoTooltip` and `ConnectionStatus` (a line of text or a fixed `w-64` status panel, anchored in the header or toolbar); the `CardDetail` move popover (a fixed form of two selects, an inline error with at most a numbers line and the admin override link (#1772), and two buttons under the Move button in the panel header); and `ChoiceColorPicker` (`absolute`, not `fixed`: eight swatches plus a reset button inside the scrolling settings panel, which it scrolls into view); the `RichTextEditor` text-color popover (`absolute`, a fixed `w-40` grid of ten swatches in the editor toolbar; its Custom body widens to `w-max` (about 216px) and is shifted to stay inside the editor container, which is still bounded, so the exemption holds); `UserMenu` (a fixed handful of items under the header); the `BulkActionToolbar` Move / Assign / Priority menus (#1482: exempt because of their anchor, not because their content is bounded: the Assign menu lists board members, which is user data. The toolbar is `fixed bottom-4` on the viewport, not inside any scroll region. The Move and Assign menus open upward capped at `max-h-64 overflow-auto`, so only a viewport shorter than about 330px could clip them; the Priority menu has no cap, but it is a fixed list of four priorities); and the `Navbar` notifications panel (#1482: the header is not `position: fixed`, but it stays at the top of the viewport because the app shell is `h-screen overflow-hidden` (`App.tsx`), so it is not inside a scroll region either, and the panel is `absolute right-0 top-8` with its list capped at `max-h-80 overflow-y-auto`, so only a viewport shorter than about 400px could clip it; same anchor-plus-cap reason, not bounded content). **`MentionTextarea` `@`-mention list (#2092):** `position: fixed` and kept in the component's own DOM subtree (not a body portal, because the card sheet's focus trap and click-outside dismissal treat a portal as outside the dialog). It is placed from its measured natural height against the textarea's rect: below, else above, else the larger side with `max-height` capped to it so the list scrolls (`overflow-y-auto`, the active option scrolls into view). It never overlaps the textarea (the user is typing in it) and is never pinned; the side is kept while the list still fits there, and side and cap are recomputed on every re-measure. Focus never leaves the textarea (options `preventDefault` on mousedown). Like the Tiptap mention list it is exempt from the scroll dismissal (typing scrolls the caret into view); unlike it, it re-anchors on `resize` instead of closing, because a phone keyboard opening resizes the viewport as the user starts to type. It also follows the textarea every frame while open (a rect comparison), because the card sheet re-places itself with a slide after a resize or board scroll-end and fires no event the list could hear. It hides itself while its textarea is outside the viewport or outside its nearest scrollable ancestor (the sheet's scroll region). Escape closes it; a click elsewhere leaves it open and anchored to the textarea until the `@`-word ends. Placement measures the visible viewport (#2138), not `window.innerHeight`, so a phone's on-screen keyboard is excluded (see the visualViewport rule below). ARIA (#2137): the textarea is `role="combobox"` (`aria-autocomplete="list"`, `aria-haspopup="listbox"`, `aria-expanded`, and `aria-controls` / `aria-activedescendant` only while open) over a `role="listbox"` of `role="option" aria-selected` rows (divs, not buttons), with `useId`-derived ids so several instances never collide; no `aria-multiline` (ARIA 1.2 does not allow it on `combobox`, so the role drops the implicit multiline and that is accepted) and an accessible name (`ariaLabel`, else the placeholder); a `getByRole('textbox')` query no longer finds it. The `RichTextEditor` (TipTap) `MentionList` has the same list half (`role="listbox"`, `role="option"` divs, `aria-selected`, `useId` ids), and the combobox half (#2149) is set directly on `editor.view.dom` from the suggestion lifecycle while the list is open: `role="combobox"` replaces the contenteditable's `textbox` role (with `aria-autocomplete="list"`, `aria-haspopup="listbox"`, `aria-expanded`, which `textbox` does not support; only attributes ARIA 1.2 allows on `combobox`, so no `aria-multiline`) and an accessible name (the placeholder) if it has none; every attribute touched is snapshotted and restored, not only the role. The list root also `preventDefault`s mousedown so the blur-to-dismiss never fires from a click on its padding or scrollbar. `aria-controls` / `aria-activedescendant` are reported by `MentionList` via `onActiveChange` and follow the arrow keys, and all of it is removed (role restored) on exit, Escape and editor blur, which ends the suggestion. Focus stays in the editor. The role swap on a focused element is not yet verified with NVDA, JAWS or VoiceOver (#2158). Escape priorities are unchanged: 43 for `MentionTextarea`, 31 for the `RichTextEditor` mention popup (edit-mode cancel). Hover tooltips (`pointer-events-none absolute bottom-full`) are `Tooltip`-class and exempt. A new menu whose items come from user data is not exempt. **Named exception to this whole rule: the card detail sheet (#1914).** It is an `aria-modal` dialog, not a popover: it is placed again on open, swap, resize and board scroll-end, may change sides, and never dismisses on resize or scroll. It uses its own `useCardSheetPlacement` (see § Card detail sheet placement), not `useAnchoredPlacement`. The side lock and the resize/scroll dismissals above stay in force for every other anchored popover.
- **Anchored popovers place against the visible viewport's height, not `window.innerHeight` (#2138).** Companion to the anchored-popover rule above. On a phone the on-screen keyboard (and pinch zoom) shrinks `window.visualViewport` but not `innerHeight`, so a list placed below its field could sit under the keyboard. Every placement reads `getVisibleViewport()` from `src/hooks/useAnchoredPlacement.ts` (`visualViewport` `offsetTop` and `height`, falling back to the window where it is unavailable) and passes both to `computeAnchoredPlacement` (`viewportHeight`, `viewportTop`); never read `window.innerHeight` for a popover's available height. Two direct reads remain by design: `ChoiceColorPicker` (`absolute` inside the scrolling settings panel, already exempt per #1457) and `OnboardingTour` (not an anchored-popover adopter: a coach-mark tooltip with no text input). A `visualViewport` `resize` or `scroll` re-places and never dismisses: `useAnchoredPlacement` does this for every hook adopter, and a popup that is not a React child (`MentionTextarea`, the `RichTextEditor` mention list) subscribes with `subscribeVisualViewport(callback)`, which adds and removes both listeners together; clean it up beside the window listeners. This changes nothing about the existing window `resize` and outside-scroll dismissals, which stay as the rule above and its named exceptions define them (the Tiptap mention list still ends on window `resize`: the keyboard is already open when `@` is typed). **Height caps and horizontal clamps use the visible viewport too (#2161).** Cap an anchored popover's scroll region with `visibleMaxHeight(reserve, extra?)` from the same module (a px value from `getVisibleViewport().height`, e.g. `visibleMaxHeight(2 * POPOVER_VIEWPORT_MARGIN)` or `visibleMaxHeight(2 * POPOVER_VIEWPORT_MARGIN, "6rem")` for chrome inside the cap), never a `calc(100vh - ...)` / `100dvh` class or style: those units follow the layout viewport, which the keyboard does not shrink. It is read at render, and `useAnchoredPlacement` re-renders its caller on every `visualViewport` change, so the cap follows the keyboard. Clamp `left` with `clampLeftToVisible(left, width, margin?)`, never `window.innerWidth`. `right: window.innerWidth - rect.right` offsets for right-aligned `fixed` panels (`OverflowMenu`, `AnchoredMenuPanel` `align="right"`, `SplitButton`, `RoleInfoTooltip`, `ConnectionStatus`, `CardPeekPopover`'s `right`) stay in layout coordinates on purpose: that is what `fixed` positions against. Scope: this is for anchored popovers only; modals keep `max-h-[calc(100dvh-2rem)]` (`EditSwimlaneModal`, `ImportBoardModal`). The cap is a render-time snapshot, so a popup that does not call `useAnchoredPlacement` (the imperative `MentionTextarea` and `placeFixedElement` paths) must recompute it from `subscribeVisualViewport`; the `max-h-[calc(100vh-16px)]` / `max-w-[calc(100vw-16px)]` classes on the `MentionTextarea` list are only a ceiling for measuring its natural height, its inline cap owns the real limit. Pass the same `reserve` the placement margin uses (`POPOVER_VIEWPORT_MARGIN`). Remaining `100vw` width ceilings, not migrated (width ceiling only; the left position is already clamped to the visible box by `clampLeftToVisible`): `SelectDropdown`, `SingleSelectDropdown`, `AnchoredMenuPanel`, `ColumnPolicyPopover` and `HealthStatusPopover` cap their width with `calc(100vw - 16px)`; tracked as part of the horizontal clamp follow-up if pinch-zoom width becomes a problem. Remaining `innerWidth` reads by design: right-aligned `right:` offsets (above), the `CardDetail` move popover (exempt, fixed two-select form), `CustomFieldQuickEditPopover`'s flip-side test, and `OnboardingTour`. **A list anchored to a text input is never pinned (#2163).** A hook-placed list that fits neither above nor below its anchor normally falls to `pinned`, which can cover the input the user is typing in. When the anchor is a text input or caret, use `useInputAnchoredPlacement` (returns `{ top, maxHeight }`), pass `inputAnchored` to `AnchoredMenuPanel`, or pass `true` as the fourth argument of `placeFixedElement`: it takes the larger side, returns a `maxHeight` that fits it, and the list scrolls inside (apply `maxHeight: maxHeight ?? visibleMaxHeight(...)` to the panel; the imperative path sets `style.maxHeight` itself and its popup needs `overflow-y: auto`). The natural height is measured with that cap lifted, so a capped list does not flip sides as the cap changes. Non-input anchors (a button trigger, a menu, a chip) keep `pinned`; the default is unchanged. Input-anchored adopters: the `BoardSettingsModal` member-search suggestions, the `AdminPage` offboarding transfer-owner results (`AnchoredMenuPanel` `inputAnchored`), the `RichTextEditor` mention list and the `MentionTextarea` list (which does its own larger-side cap). Every other adopter anchors to a button, menu trigger or chip. The card detail sheet (`useCardSheetPlacement`) keeps `window.innerHeight` by design (#1914). Test each case with and without a stubbed `window.visualViewport` (jsdom has none; `stubVisualViewport` / `clearVisualViewport` in `src/test/visualViewportStub.ts`).
- **Inline confirmation for destructive toggles** — for settings toggles with immediate, broad-blast-radius effects (board-wide or instance-wide — e.g. enabling hard WIP enforcement, enabling maintenance mode), show an inline confirmation row before committing. The trigger is **blast radius and immediacy, not permanence**: a reversible change that instantly affects everyone still qualifies. **Confirm only the transition that removes access or functionality, never the one that restores it** — confirm turning maintenance mode on, never turning it off. On toggle click, keep the toggle **mounted and rendered** and add a text prompt + Confirm + Cancel **bare-text** buttons at `text-xs` scale directly below it (`text-danger hover:text-danger font-medium` for Confirm, `text-fg-tertiary hover:text-fg` for Cancel, both with `focus:ring-2` — reuse the member-removal and hard-WIP patterns in `BoardSettingsModal`). Do **not** swap the toggle row out for the confirm row, and do **not** use a filled button variant for Confirm — either one creates a third visual treatment of what should be a single pattern. Do **not** flip the toggle optimistically first: none of the reference instances do, and with the control never having changed there is nothing for Cancel to revert. Do not use a modal-within-modal or a danger-zone text input for toggle-level confirmations. This styling (including `font-medium` on Confirm) applies to **any** dedicated two-button inline Confirm/Cancel destructive row, not only toggles — e.g. the connected-account Disconnect confirm (#1314). The only sanctioned bare-text exemption from `font-medium` is the admin table's persistent row actions (§ Admin table row overflow menu); a transient confirm prompt is never that exemption.
- **The Members panel never shows a role that differs from the member's effective access without saying so (#1301).** A row for a member with all-content access (`has_all_content_access`, or the synthesized `site_admin` role) replaces the role picker with a non-editable `Full access` chip (key glyph + word, `bg-surface-hover text-fg-secondary`, `tabIndex=0`, `aria-disabled`, `title` and `aria-describedby` carrying "Full access to all boards. This account's board role doesn't apply."). When an explicit membership exists (`id !== null`), a `text-xs text-fg-tertiary` line under the name reads `Board role: <Role> — not in effect while this account has all-content access.`; a row with no membership shows the chip alone. No Moderator checkbox. Site admins also get a `Manage in Admin → Users` text link. The only write is a bare-text `Remove board role` action (only when `id !== null`, never for a membership the server refuses to change, whose row says why in visible text: `This membership is managed at the instance level and cannot be removed here.`) with the polite-wrapper inline confirm `Remove this board role? Full access is not affected.`; Escape at priority `57` dismisses it (returns `false` otherwise, and is swallowed while the removal is in flight; Confirm and Cancel use `aria-disabled`, not `disabled`); a failure is announced through the always-mounted polite region at the foot of the list with role-accurate copy and focus returns to the trigger, or to the row's chip once the row loses its explicit line. The flag comes from the server's `has_all_content_access`, which a member frame never carries, so an admin reader re-reads the board for a subject whose value is unknown.
- **The group Members panel applies the #1301 rule with differences (#2075).** A row with `has_all_content_access` (direct, inherited, owner or self) shows the same `Full access` chip (key glyph, `aria-disabled`) and, on non-inherited rows with a known role, a `Group role: <Role> — …` line. Differences from the board panel: a row whose stored role is `admin` keeps its picker (the role still counts toward the admin minimum and ownership transfer; its note says it does not limit access and still counts as a group admin), a non-admin-role row keeps a picker limited to its stored role and `Admin` (the only roles with an effect), with the chip beside it and an `aria-label` of `Group role for <name>`, so such a member can be promoted (#2165); a row with an unrecognized stored role has no picker; the visible label of the removal control is `Remove` (the aria-label reads `Remove group role for <name>` and the confirm `Remove this group role? Full access is not affected.`); there is no locked-membership line, polite-region failure announcement or focus-return to the chip on this panel yet (a refused removal shows the existing member error). The two chips are duplicated rather than shared; keep them identical.
- **Confirm/Cancel is the only sanctioned word pair for the inline destructive-confirm pattern.** Never use Yes/No, Revoke/Cancel, or a terse "Remove?" prompt. The prompt is a full sentence naming the target and consequence (`Remove <username> from this board?`, `Revoke this invite link? Anyone holding it will no longer be able to join.`), at `text-xs`, with `font-medium` on Confirm. `BoardSettingsModal` is the canonical reference; `InviteLinkPanel`, the `GroupDetail` member/label removes, `AdminPage` invite revoke, Admin → Board Invites revoke (#439) and `SettingsPage` token revoke (#1238), connected-account Disconnect (#1366) and `CardDetail`'s comment delete (#1365) conform. A page that registers a priority-0 Escape-to-navigate handler (e.g. `GroupDetail`, `AdminPage`, `SettingsPage`) must also register a higher-priority `useEscapeStack` handler (40) that cancels any open inline confirm, or Escape on the prompt leaves the page. When a row can show two confirms at once, disambiguate with `aria-label` per § Disambiguating repeated button labels.
- **An inline confirm's failure text is `Failed to <verb> <object>. Please try again.` (#1436).** One voice for every inline Confirm/Cancel failure: `Failed to remove member. Please try again.`, `Failed to delete comment. Please try again.`, `Failed to revoke invite. Please try again.`, `Failed to disconnect <Provider>. Please try again.` It is the majority form across the app; never `Could not …`, `Couldn't …` or a bare `Failed to <verb>.` with no next step. The verb and object repeat the Confirm's action (`Remove`, `Revoke`, `Disconnect`) so the failure reads as the answer to the prompt. A server `detail` replaces the generic string verbatim, and a refusal that names its own next step (`The name may already exist.`, `Only board admins can create labels.`) keeps that sentence instead of `Please try again.` The #2054 "may have gone through" wording for a no-response or 5xx on a secret-bearing request is a separate rule and stays. Render it per the #1421 rule below.
- **Dismissing an inline confirm returns focus to its trigger (#1367).** When an inline Confirm/Cancel prompt closes (Cancel, Escape, or a Confirm that completes and closes the prompt; a `CardDetail` comment delete that fails stays open instead, see the #1421 rule), keyboard focus must land on the control that opened it. The focused Cancel button unmounts, and most triggers are swapped out while the prompt is open, so without this focus falls to `body` and the user loses their place. Never hand-roll it with a captured element (the trigger is re-mounted, so the captured node is stale). Use `useConfirmFocusReturn(activeKey)` from `src/hooks/useConfirmFocusReturn.ts`: pass the key of the open confirm (`null` when none), attach `triggerRef(key)` to the trigger (a row id, or a constant string for a single prompt; a `Toggle` takes it as `buttonRef`; in a Save-attached confirm the trigger is Save). It restores focus only when focus would otherwise be lost, and is a no-op when the confirmed action removed the row. Every inline-confirm site uses it: the `BoardSettingsModal` member remove and hard-WIP confirms, `LensConnectionModal`, `InviteLinkPanel` (group and board lists), `GroupDetail` member/label removes, `AdminPage` invite revoke and maintenance mode, Admin → Board Invites revoke (`BoardInvitesTab`, #439), `SettingsPage` token revoke and Disconnect, `EmailSettingsSection` source switch, `BoardSettingsFieldsTab` "Change type" prompt (trigger is the type button), `BoardSettingsPoliciesSection` discard-switch prompt, `WebhookDetail` confirms, and `CardDetail` comment delete. A new inline confirm must add itself to this list and test Cancel (and Escape, where a handler exists) returning focus.
- **Every inline Confirm/Cancel prompt is announced through a persistent live region (#1435).** A wrapper that mounts together with its text (`role="status"` on the prompt itself) is not announced on open by NVDA/JAWS or some VoiceOver builds; text inserted into a region that was already in the DOM is. Next to `useConfirmFocusReturn`, call `useConfirmAnnouncement(message)` from `src/hooks/useConfirmAnnouncement.ts` with the prompt's full sentence while the confirm is open and `null` otherwise. It keeps one visually hidden `aria-live="polite" aria-atomic="true"` node (no `role`, marked `data-confirm-announcer`) on `document.body`, created when the first host mounts, and writes the prompt into it on open; closing clears it, so reopening announces again, and the latest open prompt owns it. The hook sits at the component's top level (it cannot live inside a render helper), so build the message from state there. The node is appended to the topmost open `aria-modal` dialog when there is one (screen readers that honor `aria-modal` may ignore a live region outside it), else to `document.body`, and the page-level one is created at app boot (`mountConfirmAnnouncer()` in `main.tsx`). Keep the prompt's own `role="status"` wrapper where it already has one: it still announces a failure inserted into it (the #1421 rule). **Known gap (ux-review, tracked in #2232):** a wrapper that contains the question text and buttons (the `CardDetail` comment delete, for one) can announce the prompt twice on screen readers that already announce a freshly mounted live region, and re-reads it on a failure because of `aria-atomic`; new prompts should instead keep the wrapper as an always-mounted slot for failure text only, with the question and buttons outside it. Adopted by every inline confirm listed in the #1367 rule above plus the `WebhookSecretReveal` close-without-storing prompt. Forms such as the `CardDetail` block form do not use the hook. Never hide or re-label the hook's node. In tests, `screen.getByText` skips it (`configure({ defaultIgnore })` in `src/test/setup.ts`); assert the announcement with `announced()` from `src/test/confirmAnnouncer.ts`. A new inline confirm must call the hook and add an `announced()` assertion beside its prompt test.
- **When an inline revoke removes its own trigger, focus moves to a stable landmark (#439).** `useConfirmFocusReturn` is a no-op when the confirmed action removed the trigger — a revoked row that leaves a filtered list, or a row that turns terminal and drops its Revoke button — so focus would fall to `body`. Move it explicitly, after the re-render, to the list's heading (`tabIndex={-1}`, `focus:outline-none`): "Pending invites" / "Invite links" in `InviteLinkPanel`, the "Board Invites" `h2` in `BoardInvitesTab`. The same applies to the panel's create form: opening it focuses **Name**, Cancel returns to **New link**, and a successful create focuses the reveal's **Copy**. A background refetch (a socket `reloadSignal` bump, including the echo of your own create) must keep the list mounted — show the spinner only while no rows are displayed — or it unmounts the focused control.
- **An inline Confirm/Cancel prompt is a polite live region and guards its own request (#1421).** Put `role="status" aria-live="polite" aria-atomic="true"` on the prompt's wrapper element (same trio as the existing status regions) so screen reader users are told it appeared, and render any failure text *inside* that same wrapper, on its own line (`<p className="text-xs text-danger mt-1">`) so it never overflows a single-line `whitespace-nowrap` row. Text inserted into an already-mounted region is announced reliably; the initial mount of the region together with its content may not be announced by every screen reader, so a prompt's own wrapper alone is not a reliable cue; the persistent announcer in the #1435 rule above is what announces it on open. Disable **both** Confirm and Cancel (`disabled:opacity-40`) while the request is in flight and early-return from the handler when one is already pending, so a double click sends one request. Wrap the request in `try/catch`: on failure keep the prompt open with a short `text-danger` message (`Failed to delete comment. Please try again.`, the "Failed to <verb> <object>. Please try again." voice) and re-enable the buttons, never leaving the UI stuck or letting a rejection go unhandled. Clear the error when the prompt is cancelled or reopened. Reference: `CardDetail` comment delete; `InviteLinkPanel` revoke (group and board lists, #1444) also follows it, and reports a `400` "already used" outcome — which closes the prompt — through an always-mounted `sr-only` `role="status"` region so it is still announced. `BoardSettingsModal` member remove carries only the live-region wrapper; it keeps its own #1373 design (shared error slot below the list, Cancel not disabled, prompt closed in `finally`).
- **Delete is offered only to who the server lets delete, through one predicate (#2034).** `canDeleteBoard` (`src/utils/canDeleteBoard.ts`) is the single gate for every board-delete control (Dashboard, board switcher, Board Settings Danger Zone): the owner, or a user with `can_access_all_content` (`site_admin` role on the board, `user.can_access_all_content` where only the user is known). `is_site_admin` and a board `admin` role do not grant it. Do not re-derive the check from `isAdmin` or `owner.id` in a new surface. A viewer who cannot delete still sees the Danger Zone, with no control and a note naming the owner. A failed delete reports inline (`role="alert"`, above the button) then raises a `danger` toast with `announce: false` (§ Toasts, inside-a-modal rule), keeps the modal open and the typed name, and on a 403 asks the host to refetch the board so a stale owner becomes the non-owner state. The alert renders outside the can-delete branch so it survives that flip, and focus moves to the non-owner note (`tabIndex={-1}`) because the focused button unmounts. A blocked Delete is `aria-disabled` with `aria-describedby` on the typed-name hint, and clicking it focuses the field. An empty board (no active or archived cards) keeps its single-click delete with no typed name; that exemption predates #2034 and is unchanged. Group delete follows the same rule (#2129): `canDeleteGroup` (`src/utils/canDeleteGroup.ts`, a wrapper over `canDeleteBoard`) gates the `GroupDetail` Danger zone, and a non-owner group admin sees the same `text-sm text-fg-tertiary` note naming the owner in place of the button. `GroupDetail` does not refetch on a 403, so the note carries no focus handling there; add the `tabIndex={-1}` focus effect if a refetch-on-403 is ever added.
- **Inline error messages render inside an always-mounted live region, and a confirm action keeps focus on failure (#1437).** Put `role="status" aria-live="polite" aria-atomic="true"` on the always-rendered container (e.g. the reserved `text-xs h-4` slot) and vary only its text; use `role="alert"` instead for a blocking failure. Never conditionally mount a bare `<span className="text-danger">` with no live region, because screen readers will not announce it. When a confirm-modal action button is inert in flight, use `aria-disabled` plus the handler's re-entry guard rather than `disabled` (a disabled button drops focus to `body`), swap its label to the progress form (`Deleting…`, `Archiving…`), and swallow Escape/backdrop dismissal while the request is pending; if `disabled` is unavoidable, restore focus to the primary action on failure. Reference: `CardDetail` delete/archive modal, checklist and attachment error slots.
- **A staged list reports per-item failures on the item and a count in the shared summary slot (#1299).** This is for staged lists (Board Settings "To be added"), where each item is re-sendable on its own; it is the opposite of the selection-based `BulkActionToolbar` in § Bulk operations (#1733), which keeps the whole selection and puts the reason in its summary. When one submit sends independent requests (e.g. Board Settings "Add N members to board"), use `Promise.allSettled`: remove the items that succeeded and keep the refused ones in place for retry. Render each refusal as `<p role="alert" className="text-xs text-danger mt-1 pl-8 break-words">` directly under its row, (`role="alert"`: it is conditionally mounted and blocks that row until fixed) with the person's name in an `sr-only` prefix (`{name}: {reason}`) and `aria-describedby` from the row's controls, showing the server `detail` when it is a non-empty string and a short fallback otherwise; clear a row's error when that row is edited or removed. Lock each row's controls that change what was sent (the role dropdown and Remove) while the requests are in flight, so a reason always describes the value that was sent. Typing in the search box never clears a failure summary; it goes away with the last refused row. The always-mounted summary slot (`text-xs min-h-4` with `role="status" aria-live="polite" aria-atomic="true"`) carries the outcome as counts ("Added N members. M could not be added.") and never repeats the server detail.
- **An action offered after a live-region message sits beside the region, never inside it (#1772).** Keep the `role="alert"` / `role="status"` element to the message text only; render the follow-up action (e.g. an admin override link) as its sibling with `aria-describedby` pointing at the region, so the announcement stays the message and the action is an ordinary Tab stop. While that action is in flight, mark it `aria-disabled` with a progress label ("Moving anyway…"), never `disabled`, so keyboard focus is not dropped. Reference: `CardDetail`'s Move popover.
- **In an explicit Save/Cancel composite editor, inline confirmation attaches to Save, not to the control.** The toggle rule above assumes the control commits on click. In a composite editor (§ Composite inline editors) nothing commits until Save, so confirming when a value is *selected* would force a second confirmation at Save time and leaves an incoherent state where the user "confirmed" something they can still Cancel. Instead, diff the last-saved value against the pending draft inside the submit handler and show the confirm row only when the blast-radius transition is actually present — still only the direction that removes or re-routes functionality, never the one that restores it. Reference: `EmailSettingsSection.tsx`'s `env → database` switch, which reuses the maintenance-mode confirm's exact bare-text styling with a different trigger point.
- **Escape priority inside the card detail panel** — any dropdown, picker, or popover rendered inside `CardDetail` must register `useEscapeStack` at a priority **above 30**. **Never use `useDropdownEscape`** there: it registers at 25, below the panel's own close handler at 30, so Escape would dismiss the entire panel instead of the control. Allocated so far: `30` panel close · `31` rich-text edit-mode cancel (`RichTextEditor`, first Escape cancels the edit, second closes the panel) · `32` card-face custom-field quick-edit popover (`CustomFieldQuickEditPopover`, #1856; see below) · `33` color picker in the `RichTextEditor` toolbar · `34` inline new-label input (`CardDetail`, #1972; returns `false` when not adding) · `35` archive/delete confirm and the inline comment-delete prompt (#1365) · `36` move popover (swallows Escape while its admin override is in flight, #1772) · `37` relation picker and the bulk-add checklist dialog (`CardDetail`, #1376/#1891; both return `false` when closed, so they can share a slot, as they are never open together) · `38` MR/PR link editor (`CardExternalRefSection`, #352) · `39` multi-select custom-field value menu (`MultiSelectValueInput`'s default, #1391) · `41` dropdown custom-field value menu in `CustomFieldEditRow` (#1478; above 40 because a modal can open over the panel, and it is passed explicitly, so it also applies to the multi-select value menu there). · `43` card title input (`CardDetail`, #2036; returns `false` unless focused) and the `@`-mention suggestion popup (`MentionTextarea`, #2036; returns `false` when closed; they share a slot as they are never engaged together) · `34.5` checklist-row assignee picker and `34.6` checklist-row due-date input (`ChecklistItemControls`, #445; fractional because 30-44 are all taken and they must stay below the 35+ confirms; each returns `false` when closed) · `44` default for the text and URL custom-field inputs (`CustomFieldValueInput`, #2036; a host passing `escapePriority` overrides it, and returns `false` unless one of them is focused) · `42` card-detail block/unblock reason form (`CardBlockSection`, #1149; above the panel's 41 value menu, below modal tiers 45+; returns `false` when no form is open). · `50` SelectDropdown's default (#1480); see the modal list. · `59` is the card context menu's alone (#2059), which is never open over the card sheet; see the `59` entry in the modal list. (The card sheet's History filter is toggle buttons, not a menu, so it has no Escape entry.) Claim the next free integer and record it in this list. **An Escape-revert on an inline text field moves focus to the enclosing dialog, never `blur()`, when the field sits inside a dialog or panel (#2082).** The card title input (`43`) and the text/URL custom-field inputs (`44`) revert through `useEscapeStack`, then call `focusEnclosingDialog` (`src/utils/focusEnclosingDialog.ts`), which focuses the nearest `[role="dialog"][tabindex]` ancestor (the card panel and `ModalWrapper` roots are `tabIndex={-1}`: no tab stop, inside the focus trap) so focus never drops to `<body>` (board-toolbar fields outside any dialog, such as `FilterBar`, keep their reset-and-blur); revert first, because moving focus still fires the field's blur-save. Tests assert `document.activeElement` is the dialog, then that a second Escape closes it. **Any inline Escape `onKeyDown` in `CardDetail` (or a modal, using the modal list below) (a hand-rolled `if (e.key === "Escape")` on an inline editor input) needs a matching `useEscapeStack` entry above the host's close handler** — the document-level stack fires after React's handler, so without an entry the panel or modal closes too (#1972). **`32` is not hosted by `CardDetail`:** `CustomFieldQuickEditPopover` is rendered only by `CardItem` as a portal to `document.body` on the card face, while open. It must sit above BoardView's selection-clear (10), focus-exit (12) and view-toggle (15) so one Escape closes only the popover and leaves the selection intact; its number only matters relative to an open panel (Escape closes the popover first, then the panel), and 31 is taken by `RichTextEditor`'s edit-mode cancel. `59.5` custom color popover in the new-label row (`ColorSwatchPicker`, #2095; see the modal list below). **A nested `aria-modal` dialog inside `CardDetail` closes on Escape solely through its `useEscapeStack` entry, never also through an `onKeyDown` on its inputs (#1891).** The stack listens on `document`, so it fires wherever focus sits inside the dialog (textarea, Cancel, primary action); an input-level handler covers only that one element and invites double handling. Tests must press Escape with focus on a footer button and assert the dialog closes while the panel stays open.
- **`MovementHistoryView` keyboard handling (#1853).** Its document-level arrow/Enter handler returns early on `shouldIgnoreShortcut()` and acts on arrows only when focus is on `body` or inside the table body (never on the filter triggers, the slide-in's Close/Prev/Next buttons, or an open menu); Enter opens the slide-in only from a focused history row. Rows are a roving tab stop (focused row `tabIndex=0`, the rest `-1`), and closing the slide-in returns focus to the row that opened it. The slide-in closes through `useEscapeStack` at priority `18`: above BoardView's history-to-board handler at `15`, below every open menu or dropdown at `25` (the panel has no backdrop, so a filter dropdown or the user menu can be open over it and must take Escape first; the filter dropdowns need no `escapePriority` override). Allocated in the 16-24 band so far: `18` movement-history slide-in · `21` inline board text fields (#1860). It returns `false` when closed so Escape still leaves the view. The table is plain table semantics, never `role="grid"` without the full grid model.
- **Board-surface popover with nested menus (#2205).** `LaneFilterPopover` registers at the dropdown tier, `25`, through `useDropdownEscape` (Escape closes it and refocuses the **Lanes** trigger); every `SingleSelectDropdown` inside it passes `escapePriority={26}`, so Escape in an open inner menu closes only that menu. A board-surface popover that hosts dropdowns claims the next integer above 25 the same way and records it here.
- **Lane-filter text values (#2226).** A text/url `is` / `is not` rule's value control lists the lanes' distinct values. A stored value no lane carries is kept in the list with a `(no lane has this)` label suffix (the stored value stays raw), so a saved or shared rule never silently reads as "Choose a value" or looks live while matching nothing. When no lane has a value, the control is replaced by the `text-xs text-fg-tertiary` hint "No lane has a value yet. Use contains or matches wildcard to match text." **Text operators (#2227):** `contains`, `does not contain` and `matches wildcard` share one text input (`INPUT_CLS`); `matches wildcard` shows an always-rendered `text-xs text-fg-tertiary` hint under it, wired by `aria-describedby`, stating that a pattern matches any part of the value. The operator select is `w-40` so the longest label ("matches wildcard") fits without truncating the word that differs.
- **Deep-link into a tabbed modal (#1458).** When a modal opens onto a non-default tab from a shortcut (e.g. the swimlane `+N` popover's "Edit field order…" opening Board Settings on Swimlane fields), pass the tab as `initialTab` and focus that tab's button with a mount-only `requestAnimationFrame` effect, so it runs after `ModalWrapper` focuses the panel. Run it only when `initialTab` was explicitly passed, and only if focus has not already moved deeper into the modal (`document.activeElement` is the panel, `document.body` or null); a control that already took focus keeps it. Cancel the frame on cleanup. The active tab button carries `aria-current="true"` (nothing on inactive tabs); do not add `role="tab"`/`aria-selected` without implementing the full tablist arrow-key pattern. Every other opener leaves `initialTab` unset and opens on the default tab. Reference: `BoardSettingsModal`.
- **Menu items need a focus ring too — every `role="menuitem"`, not just the one primitive that first got it fixed.** Any `role="menuitem"` button/link inside an open menu (`SingleSelectDropdown`, `OverflowMenu`, `UserMenu`, `BulkActionToolbar`'s Move/Assign/Priority menus, and every `SplitButton` `renderMenu`, e.g. the BoardView/LensToolbar Collapse-Expand menus) is a real tab stop, reached by roving `ArrowUp`/`ArrowDown`/`Home`/`End` focus, not just a pointer target. `hover:bg-surface-hover` or `focus:bg-surface-hover` alone is invisible to a keyboard user who arrowed onto an item without touching the mouse — the ring is mandatory in addition to any focus background. Import `MENU_ITEM_FOCUS_RING` (or `MENU_ITEM_FOCUS_RING_DANGER` for destructive items) from `src/components/Common/menuItemFocusRing.ts` at every menuitem call site instead of retyping `focus:outline-none focus:ring-2 focus:ring-primary-emphasis` — this exact gap regressed independently in four components before #1234 fixed it a second time (first fixed for `SingleSelectDropdown` alone in #1140).
- **The health status popover uses the default dropdown tier, 25, with no new number (#1133).** `HealthStatusPopover` registers `useEscapeStack` at 25 on the board page (Row 1) and the group page, where nothing above 25 is open beside it; it is never rendered inside a modal or the card panel. While a save is in flight it consumes Escape without closing, so the request's outcome is never shown on a closed popover. If it is ever placed inside `ModalWrapper` or `CardDetail`, it needs a priority from the lists below.
- **Element-level Escape on the board page goes through the stack, never an `onKeyDown` (#1860).** `useEscapeStack`'s dispatch ignores `defaultPrevented`, so a React `onKeyDown` that handles Escape without consuming it lets the same keypress also reach BoardView's view-toggle (15), focus-exit (12) and selection-clear (10) handlers. Inline text fields on the board page (add-card input, column/swimlane/board rename, the search box, the Lens text filters) use `useInlineEditEscape(isActive, onEscape)` from `src/hooks/useInlineEditEscape.ts`, which registers at priority `21` (`INLINE_EDIT_ESCAPE_PRIORITY`) and returns `false` when the field is not engaged, so Escape still falls through. It deliberately outranks the `20` inline confirms. A field that can be mounted open in several places at once (the add-card input has no `onBlur` close) must scope `isActive` to focus inside its own editor, or the first-registered instance would take the key. Test it by rendering the real component beside `EscapeLowerHandlers` (`src/test/escapeSelectionProbe.tsx`) and asserting it is not called.
- **Escape priority inside a `ModalWrapper` is the same trap as inside `CardDetail` (#1140).** `ModalWrapper` registers `useEscapeStack` at priority 40; `SingleSelectDropdown` registers at 25 through `useDropdownEscape`. An open dropdown inside a modal therefore does not consume Escape — the modal closes and the user's in-progress form goes with it. Any `SingleSelectDropdown`, picker, or popover rendered inside a modal must be passed an `escapePriority` **above 40**. Allocated so far: `40` modal close · `40.7` a choice-row keyboard drag in a Board Settings field editor (`SortableChoiceList`, and `useSortableFieldDrag` for the field lists, #2190; they share the level because only one drag is active at a time): consumes Escape only while a drag is in progress (dnd-kit cancels the drag on the same key), returns `false` otherwise · `40.6` Board settings column-policy discard prompt (`BoardSettingsPoliciesSection`, #2156): cancels the prompt, returns `false` otherwise · `40.5` Board Settings Danger Zone delete (`BoardSettingsModal`, #2034; deliberately not 41, which the custom-field value dropdown holds): swallows Escape while the delete request runs, clears a typed name or failure text, and returns `false` otherwise so Escape still closes the modal · `45` custom-field value dropdown in `EditSwimlaneModal` · `46` multi-select custom-field value menu in `EditSwimlaneModal` (#1391) · `47` choice color swatch popover in the Board Settings field editors (`ChoiceColorPicker`, #1391). · `48` canceling an in-flight sample load in the Import Board modal (`ImportBoardModal`, #1452) — a loading-only handler that returns `false` otherwise so Escape still closes the modal; the board-name input's own Escape handler must skip while a sample loads, or it closes the modal first. · `49` revoke-confirm prompt of the board invite list in Board Settings → Members (`InviteLinkPanel` with `escapePriority={49}`, #1444) — the group page keeps the panel's default 40, which sits above GroupDetail's priority-0 navigate; inside the modal it must clear ModalWrapper's 40. · `50` SelectDropdown default (#1480): above 48 and above every card-panel tier, below the command palette (60); it registers only while open, so a closed menu never consumes Escape. · `51` Role and Expires pickers of the board "Invite by email" form in Board Settings → Members (`EmailInviteForm` `surface="board"`, portaled, #1444). · `52`–`56` Board Settings → Webhooks (#863, 1.3 UI spec Part 8): `52` delete confirm · `53` turn-off confirm · `54` turn-on-again confirm (including the take-over password) · `55` rotate-secret confirm · `56` secret-reveal guard (`BoardSettingsWebhooksTab`, `WEBHOOK_ESCAPE`). Each confirm returns `false` when it is not the open one. `56` swallows Escape while the reveal is unacknowledged and opens the inline "Close without storing the secret?" prompt (a second Escape closes the prompt); it returns `false` once the acknowledgment is checked, so the modal closes normally. The modal's Close button, its backdrop and every tab button ask the same guard first (`registerCloseGuard`), and the prompt's Confirm runs whichever of those was asked for. **A close or navigation guard is armed only while what it protects is rendered:** the tab derives it from the reveal being on screen, and every path that takes the reveal away (deleting its webhook, the feature turning off) clears it, or the guard swallows Escape, Close and every tab button with nothing to show. · `57` inline confirm that removes an all-content member's explicit board role in Board Settings → Members (`BoardSettingsModal`, #1301); returns `false` unless that prompt is open. `58` Board Settings → Members "Add member" search (`BoardSettingsModal`, #2036); clears the query and suggestions, returns `false` unless the search input has focus and holds a query or suggestions, so Escape still closes the modal and a lingering query never outranks the 57 confirm (focus is on it). A search-clear entry must never outrank a confirm prompt in the same surface. · `59` the card context menu (`CardContextMenu`, #2059; `CARD_MENU_ESCAPE_PRIORITY`). It is never offered over the card sheet (§ Card context menu), and BoardView closes an open menu when a card is selected. The context menu is not a modal surface, but it needs a slot above everything that can be open beside it on the board (dropdowns 25, the card-face quick-edit popover 32) and below the palette (60); no card-panel tier (30-44) is open beside it, and the 1.3 UI spec's 43/44 were already the card-panel title input and custom-field inputs (#2036). One entry serves the menu and its submenu: an open submenu closes first, the same way the palette backs out of its drill-in levels from 60. `61` the shared toast region (#2060, § Toasts): app-wide, above the command palette's `60`, and returns `false` unless focus is inside the region, so it never takes Escape from a modal or the palette. `59.5` custom color popover (`ColorSwatchPicker` `allowCustom`, #2095; mounted only while open). It is deliberately not an integer: it renders in `CardDetail` and in modals, must clear the card context menu's 59 (equal priorities resolve by registration order rather than by what is on top), and must stay below the command palette's 60, so 61 would be wrong. `useEscapeStack` sorts numerically. SingleSelectDropdown defaults to 25 and SelectDropdown to 50 deliberately: SelectDropdown is a form control used almost only inside modals and the card panel, SingleSelectDropdown is toolbar-first and opts in. Do not align the two defaults. Claim the next free integer and record it in this list.
- **Inputs inside a `ModalWrapper` never handle Escape-to-close themselves (#1973).** `ModalWrapper`'s `useEscapeStack` (40) already closes on Escape from any focus position, so an inline `onKeyDown` that calls `onCancel`/`onClose` on Escape runs it twice. Keep `onKeyDown` for Enter and other keys only. Reference: the name and swimlane inputs in `CreateBoardModal`.

## Color swatch pickers

- **Use the shared `components/Common/ColorSwatchPicker.tsx` for any row of color swatches (#1893).** Do not hand-roll a `.map()` of swatch buttons. It owns `type="button"`, `aria-pressed`, the standard `focus:ring-2 focus:ring-primary-emphasis` focus ring and the sizes (`md` 28px in modals, `sm` 20px inline).
