Adopters: `AddColumnModal`, `EditColumnModal`, `AddSwimlaneModal`, `EditSwimlaneModal`, the `CardDetail` new-label row and the `GroupDetail` shared-label row. **Reuse of board colors (#2098):** the custom popover can show an "On this board" row of the colors the board's columns, swimlanes and labels already use (`boardColors`, built once in `BoardView` by `utils/boardColors.boardColorsInUse` and passed to the four column / swimlane add and edit modals). Presets are dropped by the picker, the row is hidden when empty, each swatch is named by its source (`Use color from column To Do`), never by hex. Custom-field choice colors are palette keys, not hex, so they are not offered. The row is the board's own data: do not label it as popular or recommended.
- **Selected = a themed token, never `border-white`.** The shared component uses `border-fg ring-2 ring-fg ring-offset-2 ring-offset-surface` (plus a `scale-110` bump that is only a secondary cue). A white border disappears on the light-theme `bg-surface` panel, which left a 10% scale as the only cue. Keep at least `gap-2` between swatches so the offset ring is not clipped, and give a scrolling host about 6px of padding around the row. Focus shares the one box-shadow with the selected ring, so a focused selected swatch shows the focus color: `border-fg` and the scale bump carry "selected" while focused.
- **Custom colors: `allowCustom` adds one trigger, last in the row, inside the group (#2095).** Empty it is a dashed circle with a plus (`border-dashed border-line-strong text-fg-tertiary hover:text-fg`, same size as the swatches, with a larger `before:` hit area on a coarse pointer), named `Choose custom color`. A custom value, persisted ones included, fills the trigger with the selected classes and no glyph, named `Custom color, selected`; there is no separate swatch for it. A value matching a preset selects that preset and leaves the trigger empty: comparison is on canonical hex (`normalizeHex`), so a stored `#3b82f6` is Blue, and Apply emits the preset's literal string. `title="Custom color"`, `aria-haspopup="dialog"`, `aria-expanded`.
- **A touch hit area never covers a neighboring control (#2095).** A `before:` hit-area pseudo-element stops at each neighbor's edge: it paints above the neighbor, so reaching further steals its taps. Check every host's gap, not just the row's own, and counter a selected `scale-110` on the pseudo-element. Insets measure from the padding box, so add the border width. The custom color trigger is 44x44 at `md` (`before:-inset-2.5`, 8px each way) and 34x44 at `sm` (8px left, 6px right to `CardDetail`'s `gap-1.5` cancel button, 10px up, 14px down), rather than a flat `-inset-3`.
- **`CustomColorPanel` is body only and fires `onChange` only on Apply (#2095).** It holds a draft and has no positioning or Escape logic, so a host can place it in a popover or inline; a host passes `applyLabel` where Apply does more (`Create label` in `CardDetail` once the label is named, whose row creates a label on every change it hears). Import `HexColorPicker` and `HexColorInput` from `react-colorful` only in this file, and do not lazy-load it. Enter in a field or slider applies and must never submit a host form. The panel root stops `keydown` propagation except Escape and Tab, so document-level shortcuts never see an arrow key pressed in a slider; `shouldIgnoreShortcut` also treats `role="slider"` as owning its keys. Pointer styling lives in `index.css` under `.custom-color-panel`, and the pointer's white ring with a dark outline is a deliberate literal, since it sits on arbitrary colors in both themes.
- **One press dismisses one layer for the custom color popover, matching Escape (#2095).** The popover carries `data-dismiss-layer` (`DISMISS_LAYER_ATTR` in `utils/dismissLayer.ts`). While it is mounted, a press on its host's dismiss surface (`ModalWrapper`'s backdrop, `CardDetail`'s click-catcher) closes the popover and leaves the host and its fields alone; the next press closes the host. Hosts ask `hasOpenDismissLayer(panel)` at press time, so no registry or listener can outlive the popover, and the backdrop press-origin rule in § Modals and dialogs above still applies. **The `RichTextEditor` text-color popover is the second opt-in (#2097)**, so a press on the card sheet's click-catcher closes it first. **No other popover opts in yet**, so for those one press still closes both. A portaled one (`SelectDropdown`, `MultiSelectDropdown`) cannot opt in with the attribute alone, because `hasOpenDismissLayer(panel)` searches the host's DOM and cannot see it. A press on a real control still does what it does, and a press on another card's face still swaps the sheet (panels replace, never stack), so a half-typed label name goes with it.
- **Dismissal of the swatch popover (#2095).** It uses non-portaled `useAnchoredPlacement`, so it stays inside the host modal's focus trap. Escape (59.5), Cancel and Apply return focus to the trigger. A click outside or Tab out discards silently with no refocus. **A blur with a null `relatedTarget` is not a dismissal**: that is an app switch or a Safari button click, and an outside click has its own `mousedown` listener. Any popover that closes on blur follows the same rule. While open, the trigger's `mouseDown` calls `preventDefault`, so pressing it toggles closed instead of blurring the panel and reopening. **A press on a non-control part of the panel (the preview swatch, the padding, a label) is default-prevented (#2224)**, or focus moves to the nearest focusable ancestor (a host modal's `tabIndex=0` scroll region) and the blur closes the panel and drops the draft. The "is this a control" test is scoped to the panel (`closest("input,button,select,textarea,[tabindex]")` plus `e.currentTarget.contains(control)`), the same rule as the `RichTextEditor` panel (#2223): an unscoped `closest()` matches that ancestor and lets the press through. Any popover that closes on blur needs the same scoped guard. The `ChoiceColorPicker` popover is the same class and is tracked in #2240 (not reproduced).
- **The accessible name is the color name, never the hex.** `aria-label="Select color Blue"`, from `COLOR_NAMES` / `colorName()` in `src/constants/colors.ts`. Add a name there when you add a palette color; a hex tells a screen-reader user nothing. A caller that needs different wording passes `getAriaLabel` (the `CardDetail` label row says `Create "X" with color Blue`).
- **Not migrated, and why.** `ChoiceColorPicker` (§ Custom fields) is a popover `radiogroup` with roving focus and a check glyph, a different pattern. The `RichTextEditor` text-color panel is a **permanent exception**, not a pending migration: its preset swatches must select on `onMouseDown` with `preventDefault()` so the editor keeps its text selection (a click would blur it; a keyboard press is a `click` with `detail === 0`, handled separately so a mouse press never runs twice). The Custom swatch takes a different path (#2097): it captures `{from, to}` when the panel opens, hosts the shared `CustomColorPanel` inline (non-portaled, inside the editor container, so the editor's blur check and `hasOpenDismissLayer` both see it), and on Apply runs `chain().focus().setTextSelection(range).setColor(hex)`. react-colorful calls `.focus()` itself on pointer-down, so the panel `preventDefault`s mousedown on its non-focusable surfaces (the "is this a control" test is scoped to the popover with `e.currentTarget.contains(control)`, because `closest()` also matches the card sheet's `tabindex=-1`, and the press would then focus the sheet and close the panel, #2223), and it carries a "Default" entry whose value is the empty string, which the shared component's hex palette does not model. It applies the same selected-token (`border-fg ring-2 ring-fg ring-offset-2 ring-offset-surface`), `aria-pressed` and name-label rules by hand. The `ring-offset-2` used here for modal rows intentionally differs from `ChoiceColorPicker`'s `ring-offset-1` in its tighter popover grid. **Stored text colors are theme-adjusted at render, never rewritten (#2097):** view mode maps each color span through `RichTextColorSpan` (`readableTextColor`, 4.5:1 against the worse of `--surface` and `--surface-sunken` per theme, delivered as `--rt-fg-*` properties picked by `[data-theme]` in `index.css`), and a color it cannot normalize renders raw. The sanitizer pattern (`COLOR_STYLE_PATTERN`, `utils/richTextSanitize.ts`) admits hex, named colors and the exact `rgb(r, g, b)` form because browsers serialize the stored hex to that form on save; tests import the pattern rather than copying it. **The editing view gets the same adjustment (#2099)** through `richTextEditColor.ts`, a ProseMirror inline decoration (`.rt-color` plus the same `--rt-fg-*` pair) layered on the `Color` extension, so the stored hex and `getMarkdown()` output are never changed and a theme toggle needs no re-render. Never adjust editor colors by rewriting the mark or the document. The toolbar "A" underline and the picker swatches intentionally keep the raw stored color (they are samples of the value and the `sameColor` selected-state match depends on it); only rendered text is theme-adjusted. Popover details (#2097): ten swatches in `w-40` (5+5); the Custom body widens to `w-max` and shifts to stay inside the editor container; Escape stays at priority 33; Tab out of the open popover discards it; Apply, Cancel and Escape return focus to the editor selection, not the trigger (the named exception to the shared picker's focus return, because Apply recolors that selection); the 20px swatches have no coarse-pointer hit-area extension (existing debt, the 10px gap forbids 44px targets).

## Badges and labels

- **Webhook status pill family (#863, a 1.3 UI spec Part 8 departure).** Active / Failing / Disabled, each with its own shape and a word: a filled circle, a triangle (with `· N failures`), a slashed ring. `px-2 py-0.5 text-xs font-semibold rounded-full border`, `/15` tint, `/60` border and on-tint text (`success` / `warning` / `fg-muted`, the #1550 ratios). The shape is an `aria-hidden` SVG; the word carries the meaning. Rendered only through `WebhookStatusPill`.

- **Priority pills use filled background everywhere** — blue/orange/red/dark-red fill (low/medium/high/urgent, `PRIORITY_COLORS` in `src/constants/colors.ts`), white text. Do not use outline/ring style; filled is more immediately scannable.
- **Tinted non-pill priority controls (#1592, #1594)** — toggles such as `GroupDetail`'s "Allowed priorities" use the `/20` tint + on-tint text pattern (#1550): low=`info`, medium=`warning`, high=`danger`, urgent=`urgent` (`bg-urgent/20 border-urgent/60 text-urgent-on-tint`, dark red matching the board's `#B91C1C`). Urgent no longer needs a heavier border or `font-semibold` to differ from high. `--urgent-on-tint` contrast on the `/20` fill: 9.5:1 dark (11.3:1 on sunken), 7.1:1 light (6.8:1 on sunken). Keep the label text and expose state with `aria-pressed`.
- **`stale-severe` is neither `urgent` nor `danger` (#1841)** — it is reserved for the ≥3× card aging stage (§ Card aging ramp). Do not reuse it for priority or error states, and do not use `urgent` for aging.
- **`stale-tint` is reserved for the 1-2× card aging stage fill (`bg-stale-tint/15`, #2029).** It is pinned to the pre-#2029 orange (orange-600 light / orange-400 dark), independent of `--warning`, so the ramp's measured ratios do not move when the warning text token is retuned. Never use it for generic warning surfaces (those use `bg-warning/N`), and do not retune it alongside `--warning`.
- **`urgent` is not an alias of `danger` (#1594)** — `--urgent` is a fixed red-700 in both themes (matches `PRIORITY_COLORS.urgent`, `#B91C1C`); `--urgent-on-tint` flips per theme. Use it only for the urgent priority level; generic error and destructive states stay `danger`. Priority colors come from `PRIORITY_COLORS` (filled pills) or the `info`/`warning`/`danger`/`urgent` tokens (tinted controls); never hardcode per-priority hex maps.
- **Label pills are an exception to the filled-pill rule** — labels carry arbitrary user-assigned colors (any `#RRGGBB`, including near-white and near-black), so white-on-fill would fail contrast on light labels and the raw color fails as text over its own tint. Never write `color + "22"` (it also breaks on non-6-digit values). Render every label chip through `colorChipStyle(color)` (`src/utils/colorContrast.ts`) with the `cf-choice-badge` class plus `border`: the bg is the `choiceColors.ts` recipe (14% over white, 22% over `#1E293B`), the fg walks from the color toward black (light) or white (dark) until it reaches 6:1 against that bg (asserted in `colorContrast.test.ts` on a 6x6x6 sample of the RGB cube plus the extremes, not exhaustively; the algorithm ends at pure black or white, which always clears 6:1), and a non-hex legacy value gets the neutral slate pair. The `--cf-*` custom properties switch with `data-theme`, so there is no re-render and no `dark:` duplicate. A toggle chip (`CardDetail` label picker) shows its active state with a check mark (always rendered, `invisible` when idle, so the check reserves its space; the `font-semibold` weight can still widen the chip by about 1-2px), `font-semibold`, an inset `currentColor` ring and `aria-pressed`, never a fill change alone; do not dim an idle chip with `opacity`, which drops it below 6:1. **A chip under an ancestor's `opacity` passes its dimmed surface (#2102):** `colorChipStyle(color, dim)` with `dim` one of `DIMMED_CHIP_SURFACES` (`warning`, `stale`, `stale2x`, `stale3x` on the card face, which passes `aging.stage`; `lens-closed` on the closed-issue `LensIssueCard`, `opacity-70` over the lane's `bg-canvas`). The bg (the hue) is unchanged; the fg walks further toward black/white until the pair holds 6:1 *as rendered*, after the opacity blends both toward the backdrop. Pinned floors over the sampled cube, the samples and the legacy neutral pair (`colorContrast.test.ts`): 6:1 on every light surface and the dark warning stage; dark stale / 2x / 3x 5.91 / 5.91 / 5.92 and dark closed Lens 5.08, where pure white is the most the fg can do. Before, they fell to about 4.3 light on the stale stages and 3.1 light / 3.6 dark on the closed Lens card. The opacity stays (it is the stage and closed-state cue); a new dimmed surface gets a `DIMMED_CHIP_SURFACES` entry, whose backdrop `onTintTokens.test.ts` recomputes from the tokens and stage classes. This is one of two permitted tinted, bordered pills in the system — see the custom-field chip exception below for the other.
- **Color dots carry a constant 1px neutral ring (#2094)** — a user-chosen dot can be near-white (1.0:1 on the light surface) or near-black (1.44:1 on dark). Put the `color-dot` class (`index.css`, a mid-gray `box-shadow` ring, so no Tailwind `ring`/`shadow` utility on the same element) on every dot filled from a user or board color: column, lane and group-label dots, filter-chip and dropdown dots. Color stripes beside a name get no treatment; they are decorative.
- **Pinned custom-field chips are a second, distinct exception to the filled-pill rule** — the card-face custom-field chip (`CustomFieldValueDisplay`, `CardItem`) uses `border border-line rounded` with plain `text-fg-secondary` value text; the chip itself is never filled or tinted — the one tint permitted is a colored choice's badge *inside* it (see the colored-choices rule below). Custom fields need their own visual language distinct from both the priority pill (filled, semantic urgency color) and the label pill (tinted, user-assigned color) — collapsing all three into one visual bucket would make it impossible to scan a card face and tell "this is a priority" from "this is a label" from "this is a custom field" at a glance. The neutral bordered chip signals "structured metadata, no inherent color" the way a table cell border does, leaving the field's own semantics (a dropdown choice's color dot or color badge, a date, a number) to carry any further meaning inside it.
- **Colored dropdown and multi-select choices may render as a tinted badge (#1391).** When a field choice has an explicit color, its value renders as a tinted badge inside the neutral bordered chip on the card face and in the row header: `rounded px-1.5 py-0.5 text-xs`, with fg and bg from the 8-key `{light, dark}` palette in `src/constants/choiceColors.ts`. Rules: (1) The text label is always rendered. Color never carries meaning alone, and the badge is never an icon or dot only. (2) Every key must reach at least 4.5:1 against its badge background in both themes (currently at least 6.0), and any new key requires a recorded ratio. (3) A choice with no explicit color keeps the existing neutral chip and hash `choiceColor` dot, so existing boards look unchanged. (4) An orphaned value (choice removed or renamed) renders neutral with no color. (5) Never use the tinted badge for the field name or a non-choice type. It is the second permitted tinted pill, after label pills (priority pills are filled, not tinted), and it remains distinct from a label because it sits inside the field chip. **Mechanics:** render it only through `ChoiceBadge` (`components/Card/ChoiceValue.tsx`, which also owns the one `ChoiceDot`), resolve the key only through `explicitChoiceColor()` (`utils/customFieldValue.ts` — it returns `null` for unset, orphaned and unknown keys), and never inline the palette hex: `choiceBadgeStyle()` passes the pair as `--cf-{bg,fg}-{light,dark}` custom properties and the `cf-choice-badge` class in `index.css` picks the pair for the active `data-theme`, so no `dark:` duplicate is needed. An explicit color *replaces* the dropdown dot; it never sits beside one. On the card face a quick-editable badge carries the edit hint as an inner text span with `underline decoration-dotted underline-offset-2` — the decoration is `currentColor`, so it always matches the badge fg's contrast — never as a `border-b` on the badge, which would change its height. In card detail (`variant="detail"`) the value renders as a bare `ChoiceBadge` with no bordered chip around it, the same way detail renders every other value as plain text rather than a chip.
- **Swimlane-row custom-field chips are the same neutral bordered chip, with two deviations the surface forces (#1140)** — the row chip is `CustomFieldValueDisplay` at `variant="row-chip"` and keeps `border border-line rounded` with a `text-fg-tertiary` name (unless hidden by the board setting, #1418; `fg-muted` until #1862) and `text-fg-secondary` value, so a custom field reads as a custom field whether it hangs off a card or a row. It is **not** a fourth exception to the filled-pill rule. What differs: (a) `max-w-full min-w-0` replaces `max-w-[10rem]` and the value truncates at 20 characters instead of 16, because the swimlane label panel is a `sidebarWidth ?? 220`px *vertical* column, not a horizontal metadata row — row chips stack, so each owns a full line and a 160px cap would truncate for nothing; (b) an `is_admin_only` definition renders a leading `w-3 h-3 text-fg-tertiary` padlock **inline SVG on `currentColor`** (`role="img"`, `aria-label="Admin-only field"`) before the dropdown color dot, and appends ` · Only board admins can see this` to the chip's `title`. Never a `🔒` emoji — an emoji paints in its own fixed color and stops tracking the theme. Only an admin ever receives an admin-only value, so the padlock never renders for a member; it exists so an admin can tell, without opening Board Settings, which values on their screen their team cannot see. The same padlock marks the same fact in the Board Settings → Swimlane fields list — one glyph, one meaning, both surfaces.
- **Row-chip field names are a board-level admin setting, not a per-viewer preference (#1418).** `Board.show_row_chip_field_names` (default on) is one switch for the whole board, at the top of Board Settings → Swimlane fields; non-admins get a read-only line. It is threaded `BoardView` → `SwimlaneRow` (`hideRowChipFieldNames`) → `CustomFieldValueDisplay` (`hideName`), and `computeAutoSwimlaneWidth` takes the same flag so the label column does not stay sized for names it no longer shows. It affects `variant="row-chip"` only: card-face chips always show the name, and `SwimlaneFieldsPopover` (`+N`, `detail` variant) always lists name and value. Do not add a per-field or per-viewer override on top of it.
- **A hidden-name row chip keeps its `title` and carries the name for assistive tech in an sr-only span (#1418).** With `hideName`, the visible `text-fg-tertiary` `{name}:` span is replaced by `<span className="sr-only">{name}: </span>` — never `aria-label` on the chip `<span>`, which ARIA 1.2 prohibits on a generic role. The `title` stays `{name}: {value}` (plus the admin-only suffix), and everything else on the chip is unchanged: padlock, hash dot, `ChoiceBadge`, multi-select sub-chips, empty-value omission, the 20-character cap. The value simply takes the freed width.
- **Checkbox-type fields never hide their name (#1418).** A bare `Yes`/`No` is meaningless without its field, so `hideName` is ignored when `field_type === "checkbox"`, both in `CustomFieldValueDisplay` and in the auto-width estimate.
- **Hiding a visible label must not remove the only touch/keyboard path to it (#1418).** When a display setting hides text that a hover `title` otherwise supplies, either keep a focusable trigger that reveals it (for example the `+N` field popover) or the helper text must not promise one. A swimlane row whose values are all pinned keeps an "All fields" trigger when row chip names are hidden (#1509).
- **An interactive control's visible text must be contained in its accessible name (WCAG 2.5.3 label-in-name) (#1509).** When a button has both visible text and an `aria-label`, the `aria-label` starts with the visible text, for example `All fields: Show all 3 field values for Acme`. Voice-control users say what they see.
- **Compact chip-style triggers keep a minimum 24px hit area (`min-h-6`) (WCAG 2.5.8) (#1509).** Small `text-xs` pill triggers such as the swimlane row `+N` / `All fields` button carry `min-h-6` so the target is at least 24px tall.
- **A multi-select value is neutral sub-chips inside the same neutral bordered chip (#1391).** The value cell holds `MultiSelectChips`: `text-xs px-1.5 py-0.5 rounded truncate bg-surface-hover text-fg-secondary`, each with its full text in `title` — never color-dotted. An entry whose choice has an explicit color is a tinted `ChoiceBadge` with the same width cap instead (see the colored-choices rule above); pass the field's `definition` to `MultiSelectChips` so it can resolve them. Card face: at most **2** sub-chips (each `max-w-[6rem]`) then a `+N` (`text-fg-tertiary text-xs`, #1862) whose `title` lists every entry; the outer chip is `max-w-[14rem] min-w-[4rem]` (not `shrink-0`, because the card metadata row is single-line `overflow-hidden`) and the field name (`min-w-0 shrink-[3]`) truncates before the value chips do. Swimlane row header: at most **3**, wrapping (`flex-wrap gap-1`, sub-chips `max-w-full`). Detail and the editor trigger show all, wrapping. The card-face chip is read-only for this type — no quick-edit role and no dotted underline; edit in card detail.
- **One shared admin-only padlock component — never a second hand-rolled copy (#1140).** The `is_admin_only` padlock (`w-3 h-3 text-fg-tertiary` inline SVG; `fg-faint` until #1862, which is 1.93:1 dark and below the 3:1 a meaningful glyph needs; `role="img"`, `aria-label="Admin-only field"`) is rendered from `components/Common/AdminOnlyFieldGlyph.tsx` and imported everywhere it appears — the row chip, `SwimlaneFieldEditRow`, `SwimlaneFieldsPopover`, and the Board Settings → Swimlane fields list. Do not re-inline the `<rect>`/`<path>` markup at a new call site. This is the same drift the codebase already guards against for `toolbarIcons.tsx` and `relativeTime()`, and it had already happened here: the glyph shipped as four inline copies and one of them announced `aria-label="Admin only"` while the other three said `"Admin-only field"`, silently splitting the accessible name across surfaces the rule above requires to say one thing.
- **Status pill tone map.** Invite status pills (pending/used/expired/revoked) come only from `INVITE_STATUS_STYLES` in `src/constants/invites.ts`. Do not define a local status-to-class map. A danger status uses `bg-danger/20 text-danger-on-tint`, never `bg-danger/60` or another high-alpha fill behind same-hue text (about 2:1 contrast) (#1501). The text half of every status tint comes from the on-tint tokens, not `text-danger`: see **On-tint status text** below (#1550).
- **Health status pills (#1133).** Board and group health (On track / At risk / Off track / No status) renders only through `HealthStatusPill` / `HealthListingPill` (`components/Common/HealthStatusPill.tsx`), with every class, word and description from `src/constants/healthStatus.ts` (`HEALTH_STATUS_STYLES`, `HEALTH_STATUS_LABELS`, `HEALTH_STATUS_DESCRIPTIONS`). Never define a local health-to-class map. Rules: (1) tint + shape + word, always: `bg-<hue>/20 border-<hue>/60 text-<hue>-on-tint` with success / warning / danger, and `bg-fg-muted/20 border-fg-muted/60 text-muted-on-tint` for No status; the four pairings are pinned at >= 5.00:1 on canvas, sunken and surface in both themes (5.26 before #2029 darkened the light `/20` fills) in `src/test/onTintTokens.test.ts`. (2) An **inherited** value (the entity sets nothing; `health_status_source` names the group) is the same pill with `border-dashed`, an sr-only `, inherited from {Group}` suffix and a `title` naming the group; a value explicitly set equal to the parent's is "set here" and solid. (3) **Listings** (board rows, subgroup cards, the group tree, the dashboard) show a read-only `<span>` pill only when `effective_health_status` is non-null, for everyone, and never on a `navigation_only` group; No status is never listed. (4) **Headers** (board Row 1, group meta line) use `HealthStatusControl`: an admin gets a `<button>` trigger, including the hollow No status pill as the place to set one; a non-admin gets a trigger that opens the popover **read-only** (header, set line, note, History auto-expanded, one **Close** button; #2242), or nothing when no status applies — never a greyed-out control. A plain non-admin pill remains only in `mode="basic"` (every lane) or without `fetchHistory`. (5) The accessible content is real text (`Health status: ` sr-only prefix + word), never an `aria-label`, so a row button's name reads "{name} Health status: At risk" and the visible word is always in the name. Header pills may pass `collapseLabel` (word `sr-only md:not-sr-only`, shape only below `md`). The swimlane label panel (#2243) is also a header under rule (4), with one recorded exception: on a repeated-row surface the admin's unset affordance is a hover/focus-revealed icon button (visible at rest on coarse pointers via `[@media(hover:none)]:opacity-100`), not a No status pill; a set value is always visible, and non-admins see nothing when unset.
- **Lane health status (#2243).** Renders only through `HealthStatusControl entityKind="swimlane"` (fields from `laneHealthFields()` in `utils/healthStatus.ts`, so `inheritedFrom` is always null): solid, never dashed, because a lane never inherits from the board and never rolls up to it. Expanded lane: the set pill is its own line under the name (`scope="lane"`, `collapseLabel`); collapsed lane: a shape-only inline pill (`shapeOnly`) beside the name; its wrapper carries `-my-0.5` so the 24px hit target does not make the `py-1` row grow past the 20px name line (guarded by `e2e/swimlane.spec.ts`), and nothing when unset, even for admins. The unset trigger (`trigger="icon"`) is the first button of the right-hand cluster, admins only, expanded only. The popover has four choices (On track / At risk / Off track / No status), no Inherit, no note and no history, plus the line "This lane's status is separate from the board's status."; board/group extras added to `HealthStatusPopover` must stay off for `entityKind="swimlane"` until lanes get history: both `HealthStatusControl` and `HealthStatusPopover` force `mode="basic"` for that kind, and `SwimlaneRow` also passes `mode="basic"` explicitly (#2242). The pill's sr-only prefix names the lane (`Health status for lane {name}: `). Clicks and double-clicks on the pill, the icon and the portaled popover never reach the label panel's double-click edit shortcut (wrap in `onDoubleClick={e => e.stopPropagation()}`). A save that swaps triggers hands focus explicitly (set: to the new pill; clear: to the icon, or to the lane name on a collapsed lane, which has no icon) via `autoFocus`; never leave a pending focus request for an element that is not rendered, or it fires on a later mount. An open popover survives remote updates: the trigger whose popover is open stays mounted (`onOpenChange`) until it closes, even if the value flips set <-> unset. The unset icon keeps a 24px hit target (`min-w-6 min-h-6`) around its 16px glyph. The announcement goes through the row's own live region (`onAnnounce`) because the saving control may unmount. Escape stays at tier 25.
- **Health status attribution and history (#2242).** (1) The "Set 3d ago by Priya" copy comes only from `healthSetLine` (`utils/healthStatus.ts`; tooltip via `healthTitle`, group caption via `healthCaption`); never build it inline. Time is `formatRelativeTime` (no new formatter): relative within 7 days, "on Sep 14" after; wherever a time renders it is `<time dateTime={iso} title={formatDateTimeUser(iso, prefs)}>`. A null `changed_by` renders **System** (with the title "Automatic change or removed account"); a null `changed_at` omits the clause. (2) A reconfirm is the explicit **Confirm status** action only (`POST .../health-status/`); **Save** never records a no-change, and Enter on an unchanged radio closes with no request. Enter in the note with an unchanged draft moves focus to Confirm, never confirms. (3) `HealthStatusPopover` / `HealthStatusControl` take `mode`: `"detailed"` (default: set line, note, Confirm, History) and `"basic"` (exactly the #1133 editor), forced for `entityKind="swimlane"` (see Lane health status above). (4) History is bounded: the last 20, a static "Showing the 20 most recent changes." line when `has_more`, never load-more or a cursor. (5) The non-admin header pill opens a read-only popover (amends health-pill rule (4) above). (6) The note is saved only with explicit **Save** or **Confirm status** and discarded on Cancel, Escape or outside click; it always starts empty and is one line (newlines become spaces, Shift+Enter does nothing). Disabled-button reasons are sr-only `aria-describedby` spans placed outside the button so they stay out of its name.
- **On-tint status text (#1550).** Text on a `bg-<hue>/20`..`/30` status tint uses `text-danger-on-tint`, `text-warning-on-tint`, `text-success-on-tint`, `text-info-on-tint`, or `text-muted-on-tint` (for `bg-fg-muted/20`), never the plain `text-<hue>` token, which is tuned for text on a solid surface and falls below 4.5:1 on its own tint. Never put a `/40`..`/60` fill behind same-hue text; selected states use `bg-<hue>/20` with the on-tint text and a stronger `border-<hue>/60`. Measured WCAG ratios (token RGB composited over the surface; worst case across canvas, sunken and surface):

  | Pairing on `/20` tint | Before dark | After dark | Before light | After light |
  |---|---|---|---|---|
  | danger | 3.93 | 5.73 | 3.24 | 5.58 |
  | warning | 4.54 | 6.09 | 2.56 | 5.26 (5.01 since #2029) |
  | success | 5.36 | 6.65 | 2.43 | 5.27 (5.00 since #2029) |
  | info | 4.03 | 5.68 | 3.59 | 6.06 |
  | muted (revoked) | 2.49 | 7.97 | 3.42 | 7.44 |

  Heatmap/strip `/30` fills (danger, warning, success, info) measure at least 4.69:1 dark and 4.75:1 light with the on-tint text; `/40` and above fail (danger `/40` is 4.03 dark). The revoked invite pill adds `line-through` because its text tone is now close to the `used` pill's (`text-fg-tertiary`, 4.62 dark, 5.44 light). Any new on-tint token needs a recorded ratio here (`success-on-aging-tint`, used only through `card-aging-meta`, is recorded in § Card aging ramp). The same text applies on the lighter `/10` and `/15` tints (banners, toggles, connection chips, overdue chips): plain `text-danger` on `bg-danger/10` is 3.79:1 in light mode, `text-warning` and `text-success` are 2.7 to 3.2:1, whereas on-tint text measures at least 5.56:1 on `/10`..`/15` in both themes (dark 6.22 or more). A stronger armed or `isOver` fill (for example the column trash zone's `bg-danger/50`) keeps the fill and border as the signal and sets the label to `text-fg` (5.17:1 dark, 7.33:1 light, worst surface); never reach for an on-tint token above `/30`. Remaining `/10`..`/15` hits with no same-hue text are intentional: the aging overlay, the swimlane search pulse, and the selected history row, which carry no text of their own. A `hover:bg-<hue>/30` on a bordered action uses `hover:bg-<hue>/20 hover:text-<hue>-on-tint`.
- Filter active-count badge: `bg-primary-emphasis/20 text-info` — always use the `primary-emphasis` token for the fill so the badge tracks the active theme
- Consistent badge sizing: `px-2 py-0.5 text-xs rounded-full`
- Neutral informational identity chip (admin Users table **Superuser**, #2083): `px-1.5 py-0.5 text-xs rounded-full bg-surface-hover text-fg-secondary`. It does not use the `primary-emphasis` fill, which is the **Admin** chip's fill, so the two read as distinct facts in the same cell.
- When an `OverflowMenu` is shortened by a visible reason line, give the line an `id` and pass it as `triggerDescribedBy` so the trigger is described by it (#2083).
- Active filter chip (`FilterChip.tsx`, the dismissible tag shown per applied filter in the filter row): `bg-primary-emphasis/20 text-info` fill with a `border border-primary-emphasis/40` border — same fill/text token pair as the badges above, plus the border so it reads as removable rather than a plain count/state indicator (#1239)
- **Tagged filter chips (#2205).** `FilterChip`'s optional `tag` prop renders a leading `px-1.5 rounded-full bg-surface-hover text-fg-secondary text-xs` pill naming the filter family (lane chips use `tag="Lanes"`), and a tagged chip is named `{tag}: {label}, remove`. Untagged chips keep `Remove {label} filter`; do not migrate them piecemeal. Between lane chips a plain `<span className="text-xs text-fg-tertiary">and</span>` / `or` (not `aria-hidden`) carries the join. Dismissing a lane chip moves focus to the chip that takes its place, else the **Lanes** trigger.
- **`bg-info/*` vs `bg-primary-emphasis/*` — know which one your state is.** These are not interchangeable, and `--info`/`--primary` are different values in dark mode (they only coincide in light mode, which is why this drifts invisibly). Two patterns, never cross them:
  - **Transient disclosure / toggle-open state** (a button whose job is "is this popover/menu/panel currently open" — Filters button, kebab trigger, SplitButton chevron, dropdown trigger's open state): `text-info bg-info/10`.
  - **Persistent selection / "where you are" / "what's active" state** (nav-active item, tree-active item, flyout-active item, filter active-count badge, active filter chip, radio selected, grid-overlay cell tint, lens "Current" pill, mode-indicator banners use `bg-primary/15` instead): `bg-primary-emphasis/*` (paired text token varies by context — `text-info`, `text-warning`, etc. — keep whatever paired text token the surface already uses; only the background fill is the `primary-emphasis`-vs-`info` decision).

  When introducing or touching any new "this item is the active/selected/current one" treatment, grep for `bg-info` in the file first and ask which bucket above it falls into before picking a token. Prior incidents: #1002, #1239 (`SavedFiltersDropdown`/`AdminPage`), #1336 (`AppSidebar`/`CollapsedFlyout`).

## Motion and reduced motion (#199)

- **Every decorative animation carries `motion-reduce:animate-none`** — `animate-pulse` (highlighted card, search-match cells, "Working..." text, connection-status dots, indeterminate progress) and `animate-fade-in` (e.g. a newly created board row) must stop under `prefers-reduced-motion`.
- **An indeterminate progress bar also falls back to a static one-third width** (`w-full motion-reduce:w-1/3`), never a full bar that reads as "done" — see § Import Board sample gallery (#1452).
- Functional `animate-spin` loading spinners are exempt.

## Truncation inside nested flex chips

Any chip built as an `inline-flex` container around one or more `truncate` text spans (custom-field chips, multi-select sub-chips, and any future nested-flex chip) must give **every** truncating span an explicit `min-w-0` (or a numeric `min-w-[Nrem]` floor), and give the **chip itself** `overflow-hidden`. A flex child's default `min-width: auto` is content-based — without an explicit override, `truncate`'s ellipsis never engages inside a flex container, and long content silently overflows the chip's own box onto whatever renders next in the row (#1411: this is why pinned custom-field chips rendered on top of their neighbors instead of clipping). A chip that needs to shrink in its own parent flex context (e.g. the card metadata row) must not be `shrink-0` with only a `max-w` — pair an explicit `min-w-[Nrem]` floor with the `max-w` instead, or it will overflow its parent rather than shrink into it.

`overflow-hidden` on a chip is a backstop, not the truncation mechanism: every flex child that can hold long text still needs `min-w-0` plus `truncate`. Anything inside a clipped chip with a focus ring needs at least ring-width (2px) of padding on each clipped edge, so do not reduce the chip's `py-0.5`.

## Decorative glyphs (#2012)

- **A decorative glyph rendered in its own `<span>`/element is `aria-hidden="true"`.** This covers separators and hover affordances that sit beside the real content: the hover "+" (`SeparatorPlus`, used by `RowSeparator`, `ColumnSeparator` and `SwimlaneRow`), breadcrumb dividers (`/`, `›`), the `·` between notices, and the sidebar's split "+" spans next to "New board" / "New group". Otherwise a screen reader announces a stray "+" or "slash".
- **Inline "+ Label" text is out of scope.** When the "+" is part of the same text node as the label (e.g. `+ New board`), it is read as part of the label; do not split it out just to hide it.
- **A glyph that is the only content of a control needs an `aria-label` on the control, not `aria-hidden`** — hiding it would leave the control unnamed.
- A bare `eslint-disable` also needs a `-- reason` (see `docs/development/suppressions.md`).

## Top chrome — two-row composition

The authenticated UI is framed by two horizontally-divided chrome rows and a main region. Maintain this skeleton across all routes; feature work lands inside the rows, not on top of them.

| Row | Purpose | Surface | Height | Typography | Landmark |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Row 1 | App header (logo, breadcrumb, star, utilities, account) | `bg-sunken border-b border-line` | `h-14` | `text-sm text-fg` | `<header role="banner">` |
| Row 2 | Board chrome (view tabs, actions, utilities, connection status) | `bg-surface border-b border-line` | `h-10` | `text-xs text-fg-tertiary` (default; active view tab keeps its own active styling) | `<nav aria-label="Board toolbar">` |
| Filter row | Conditional card filter chips | `bg-surface border-b border-line` | auto (single row, wraps) | inherits | `role="search" aria-label="Board filters"` on the wrapper (was "Card filters" before #2205 added lane filters) |
| Main | Route content | inherits from route | `flex-1` | — | `<main role="main">` wraps `<AuthenticatedRoutes>` in `App.tsx` |

- **The accessible name for Row 2 is `"Board toolbar"`, not `"Board chrome"`** — "chrome" is developer jargon that announces as meaningless noise in screen readers. If internal design docs reference "chrome", the landmark label still stays `"Board toolbar"`.
- **Vertical dividers** between logical zones inside Row 2: `w-px h-4 bg-surface-hover mx-1` with `aria-hidden="true"`. Use between zone 1/2 and zone 2/3; never as a heading/section break.
- **Row 2 single source of vertical rhythm** — the outer `<nav>` owns the `h-10 flex items-center`. The inner toolbar container uses `h-full flex items-center` and must not re-introduce `py-*` padding, which would double-pad against zone button `p-1.5`/`px-3 py-1` heights.
- **Horizontal overflow behavior on narrow viewports:** when Row 2 has a pinned trailing cluster (overflow kebab + connection status), the scrollable region is an inner wrapper with `flex-1 min-w-0 overflow-x-auto`, not the outer `<nav>`. The inner toolbar inside that region uses `min-w-max` so the control set scrolls horizontally. The pinned cluster sits as a sibling flex-child of the scrollable region with `shrink-0` and `border-l border-line` so it is always visible even when the scroll region overflows. When a Row 2 layout has no pinned trailing cluster (e.g. non-board routes), `overflow-x-auto` may stay on the outer `<nav>`.
- **Never add a fifth landmark in chrome** — if a future feature needs a new region, fold it into one of the existing landmarks. Four landmarks (`banner`, `navigation: Breadcrumb`, `navigation: Board toolbar`, `search: Board filters`) plus `main` is the cap.
- **Always query landmarks by accessible name in tests** — with multiple `<nav>` elements in the tree, bare `getByRole('navigation')` is ambiguous. Use `getByRole('navigation', { name: 'Board toolbar' })`.

## Board navigation bar

This section refines the Row 2 landmark described in § Top chrome — two-row composition above. Anything here is scoped to individual controls inside that `<nav aria-label="Board toolbar">`; the outer surface, height, typography, and dividers are set by the top-chrome section. Do not re-specify them here.

The sub-nav bar directly below the main navbar contains view tabs, actions, and status:

- **Active tab** (e.g. "Board"): `bg-primary text-on-primary rounded px-3 py-1 text-sm font-medium`
- **Inactive tabs** (e.g. "Summary", "Analytics"): `text-fg-tertiary hover:text-fg hover:bg-surface-active px-3 py-1 text-sm rounded` — same height and padding as the active tab so the bar never shifts (the active tab adds only `font-medium`); the tabs sit in a `bg-surface-hover rounded p-0.5` group, so the hover uses the stronger `bg-surface-active` to stay visible
- **Vertical separators** between logical groups: `<div className="w-px h-4 bg-surface-hover mx-1" aria-hidden="true" />` (see § Top chrome), never a `|` character
- **Connection status**: use the `ConnectionStatus` component (see § Connection status indicator below) — single canonical component for the sub-nav live indicator and for the group detail header
- **Settings and utilities** (shortcuts, export, board settings): icon-only buttons sharing one treatment, `p-1.5 rounded text-fg-tertiary hover:text-fg hover:bg-surface-hover` plus the standard focus ring

## Column kebab menu (#965)

Each column header carries a `⋮` overflow kebab as the discoverable surface for column-scoped actions. The kebab is the only keyboard-reachable path to column settings and delete — no separate `✎` icon button. Inline rename is additionally reachable by Tab + Enter/Space on the column name itself, which is a real `<button>` for admins (#1376).

- **Trigger:** `OverflowMenu` instance with `ariaLabel={\`Actions for column "${column.name}"\`}` so screen readers announce *which* column is being acted on. The trigger uses the standard kebab styling and is wrapped in `opacity-0 group-hover/col:opacity-100 focus-within:opacity-100 transition` so it is hidden at rest, revealed on column hover, and stays visible when keyboard focus enters the menu (per the hover-reveal-controls rule).
- **Items, in order:**
  1. `Rename` — sets the column name into the inline editor (the same editor the admin column-name button opens on click, Enter, or Space)
  2. `Edit settings…` — opens `EditColumnModal` (color, WIP/weight limits, allow card creation, is_done)
  3. `Add policy…` — only on a column **without** a policy (#1101); opens `EditColumnModal` on its Policy tab. A column with a policy reaches the editor through the header's Policy button instead (its popover's **Edit policy**), so the kebab never offers two paths to the same editor.
  4. `Delete column` — danger-styled (`OverflowItem.danger: true`), preceded by an engraved separator. Routes to the confirmation dialog.
- **Non-admins see no kebab affordance** — the control is hidden entirely from the DOM, per the *Conditional admin-only elements* rule. Do not render a greyed-out `⋮` glyph, a disabled trigger, or a tooltip explaining missing permission; affordances Sam (occasional, non-admin) cannot use should not look like affordances at all.
- **Double-click on the column header** continues to open `EditColumnModal` as a power-user shortcut (the name button itself does inline rename on a single click / Enter / Space); the kebab is the discoverable path.

## Column delete confirmation (#965)

Deleting a column is permanent and removes every card in it (active and archived). The confirmation dialog applies a tiered safety pattern:

- **Empty column** — a plain `Cancel` / `Delete` modal is sufficient. There is nothing destructive to mistype against; adding name-typed friction here is noise.
- **Column with cards** — the dialog renders the same name-typed danger-zone pattern used for board deletion in `BoardSettingsModal`: an input with the column name in `font-mono`, a `text-xs text-fg-muted` instruction line above it, and a `Delete` button that stays disabled until the typed value matches the column name exactly. Pressing Enter inside the input commits when the value matches. Closing the dialog (Cancel, Esc, or backdrop click) resets the input.
- The confirmation copy explicitly says how many active cards and that archived cards in the column will also be deleted. *This cannot be undone.* is the closing line.

The swimlane delete confirmation (`EditSwimlaneModal.tsx`) reuses the same copy pattern for the deletable (empty) case: a declarative `{name} will be permanently deleted.` line, a `text-danger` line warning that any archived cards in the swimlane will also be permanently deleted, and the `text-fg-muted` *This cannot be undone.* closer. It does not reuse the tiered pattern above — a swimlane that still has cards blocks deletion outright (a single `OK` acknowledgment, no typed-confirmation tier) rather than offering the column's name-typed override; that `OK` button has no column counterpart, but still takes the standard `px-3 py-1.5` sizing rather than a one-off size. Keep the two dialogs' shared copy and button sizing (`px-3 py-1.5`) in sync; a wording or sizing change to one without the other is a drift, not a deliberate divergence.

## Column drag-to-trash gating (#965)

The destructive column trash drop zone is **opt-in via ⌥ (Alt)**, never visible by default during a column drag. Reorder is the common case; deletion is a deliberate, modifier-gated gesture.

- The trash zone renders only when `activeColumn !== null && altHeldDuringColumnDrag === true`. The Alt-tracking `keydown` / `keyup` / `blur` listeners register only while a column drag is active so the global-keyboard footprint is empty at rest.
- The drag overlay shows a small hint below the dragged column name: `Hold ⌥ to delete` (`text-xs text-fg-muted`) flips to `Drop on trash to delete` (`text-xs text-danger`, `aria-live="polite"`) the moment Alt is held. The hint is the discoverability cue for the gated gesture.
- Dropping on the trash zone routes to the same name-typed confirmation dialog as the kebab `Delete column` path — there is one canonical column-delete dialog, never two.

## Column headers

- Background: `bg-surface` — one level above the cell canvas
- Layout: left nav arrow · colored dot · name (truncated) · right nav arrow, then a single stat row below
- Column name: `text-sm font-medium text-fg truncate`
- **Stat row — single line, worst-offender wins.** A column header renders exactly one stat line, never two stacked lines. The decision tree (priority order: Over WIP > Over Weight > At WIP > calm):
  - **Over WIP** → `⚠ Over WIP · {count}/{limit}` (or `⛔` glyph when `hardWipEnforced`), `text-danger font-medium`, `title="Over WIP limit"`
  - **Over Weight** (and not over WIP) → `Weight {weight}/{limit}`, `text-warning font-medium`, `title="Over weight budget"`
  - **At WIP** (`cardCount === wip_limit`, not over WIP/Weight, board opt-in `show_wip_at_limit` on) → `WIP {count}/{limit}`, `text-fg-muted` (no accent strip, no glyph — it's calm, not a warning), `title="Column is at its WIP limit ({count}/{limit})"` (#973)
  - **Calm** → `{count} cards` (`1 card` for a single card), `text-fg-muted`, `title="Cards in column"`
- **Drop the `WIP` / `Weight` label words in calm states.** The limit phrasing only earns its place when a column is actually over, or the board has opted into the at-limit indicator — otherwise the count alone reads faster on a scan.
- **At-limit indicator is off by default and board-scoped (`Board.show_wip_at_limit`, admin-only toggle in Board Settings → Rules → Limit enforcement).** It never adds chrome beyond the calm-state text color — no top accent strip, no glyph — so it stays visually subordinate to the two over-limit states above it in the priority chain.
- **Top accent strip — peripherally scannable cue for over-limit state.** When over WIP, render `border-t-2 border-t-danger-emphasis` on the header surface; when over weight (and not over WIP), `border-t-2 border-t-warning-emphasis`. The strip is the *only* over-limit chrome; the existing rule "text color + `font-semibold` only, no filled background" still holds for the stat text. Never use a filled `bg-danger/…` background for over-limit stats — it competes with the column header surface color.
- The collapsed (40px-wide) header mirrors the same top accent strip and shows a `⚠` glyph in red (over WIP) or amber (over weight, not over WIP). Title includes the over-limit reason for screen readers.
- Column color dot: `w-2.5 h-2.5 rounded-full flex-shrink-0` in the column's assigned color
- Nav arrows (`◄` / `►`): `text-fg-faint hover:text-fg-secondary text-xs transition`
- Column name truncates with ellipsis — never wraps
- **Policy button (#1101) — a recorded exception to § Hover-reveal controls.** A column with a policy shows a **Policy** button (clipboard icon + the word) right after the name, **visible at rest** for every reader, not hover-revealed like the kebab. The policy is information everyone needs before moving a card, not an action, and Sam (occasional) would never find a hover-only cue. Below `lg` it is the icon alone (`hidden lg:inline` on the word) with the same accessible name: `aria-label="Column policy for {name}: {n} entry criteria, {m} done criteria"` (singular "criterion" for 1), `aria-haspopup="dialog"`, `aria-expanded`. At rest `text-fg-tertiary hover:text-fg-secondary hover:bg-surface-hover`; open `text-info bg-info/10`. It is `shrink-0`, so the name truncates first. A column without a policy shows nothing (admins get **Add policy…** in the kebab). No other column-header control may claim this exception without recording its own reason here.
- **Policy popover (`ColumnPolicyPopover`).** `role="dialog"`, `aria-label="Column policy for {name}"`, 340px wide (capped at the viewport), anchored and dismissed exactly like `SwimlaneFieldsPopover` (§ Modals and dialogs, #1455: `useAnchoredPlacement`, focus after placement, a focusable `role="region"` body with the bottom fade). Header: `{name} policy` plus the mode tag (**Advisory** / **Confirm on entry**, a neutral `bg-surface-hover` pill). Sections **Entry criteria** and **Definition of done**; an empty list reads `No criteria yet.` An admin gets **Edit policy** in the footer, which moves focus to the Policy button before opening `EditColumnModal`, because the popover unmounts and the modal restores focus to its opener. It is non-modal and deliberately untrapped, so it also closes when keyboard focus moves outside the panel and its trigger (a document `focusin` check), never floating over the board without focus in it. It also closes, and its anchor is cleared, when the policy is removed over the socket, so a policy added back later never reopens it at a stale position. Escape closes the popover first (`useDropdownEscape`, 25, above BoardView's 10/12/15). "Updated by / when" is cut from v1: there are no `policy_updated_*` fields.
- **A non-modal popover that closes on `focusin` outside it must not portal its nested menus (#2205).** A portaled menu lives under `document.body`, outside the panel, so focusing one of its items fires the outside-`focusin` close and the popover vanishes mid-choice. Leave `portalMenu` off for dropdowns inside such a popover (`LaneFilterPopover`); the menu then scrolls within the panel's list, which is why that list carries `scroll-pb-10`. An in-flow menu is clipped by that list's `overflow-y-auto` when the list is short (#2231), so the popover tracks open menus through `onOpenChange` (a keyed Set, cleared when a row unmounts) and measures the open menu (its bottom edge relative to the list) in a layout effect and gives the list `min-height: min(<measured px>, <max-height>)` while any is open, then scrolls the list so the menu's first options (and its trigger row) are in view when the menu is taller than the capped list; a new popover with in-flow menus in a scrolling body must do the same. Its list height reserve (`visibleMaxHeight(2 * POPOVER_VIEWPORT_MARGIN, "9rem")`) is verified by `e2e/lane-filters.spec.ts` at a 520px-tall viewport; re-run it after adding chrome to the panel. Single-key board shortcuts listen on the document and skip only text fields, so such a popover stops propagation of unmodified printable keys on its root (Escape and modified keys still pass).

## Board grid cells

- Background: `bg-canvas` — darkest level, creates depth contrast with cards and headers
- Grid lines: `border-r border-line/50` on populated cells; an empty cell swaps it for the dashed `border border-dashed border-line/50` frame — subtle, not prominent
- **Empty addable cells (#962)** — when a cell has no cards, the user can create cards in it (`column.allow_card_creation && canEdit`), and is not currently editing, the cell wrapper itself is the keyboard-reachable creation surface. Spec:
  - `role="button"`, `tabIndex={0}`, `aria-label="Add card to {column.name} in {swimlane.name}"` so screen readers announce *which* slot the action targets. When a grid overlay gives the cell a value, that reading is appended to this same label — see § Board grid overlay slot; an `aria-label` overrides every descendant's text, so an `sr-only` reading inside the cell would be silently discarded
  - `cursor-pointer hover:bg-surface-hover/30` on the cell so hover gives a soft wash; `focus:outline-none focus:ring-2 focus:ring-inset focus:ring-primary-emphasis` for keyboard focus
  - Dashed inset border (`border border-dashed border-line/50`) is the visual frame
  - The visible **`+ Add card`** label is a centered, `pointer-events-none`, `aria-hidden="true"` overlay (`absolute inset-0 flex items-center justify-center text-xs text-fg-muted`) that brightens on cell hover/focus via `group-hover/cell:text-fg group-focus/cell:text-fg`; the label text sits in an inner `rounded px-2 py-1` span that also gains `group-hover/cell:bg-surface-hover/50 group-focus/cell:bg-surface-hover/50` so it reads as a button (#200). Cells keep `min-h-[80px]` so empty rows hold a consistent height
  - Click anywhere in the cell — or Enter / Space when focused — opens the new-card input. Double-click and right-click on cell space continue to work as before; a right-click on a card face opens the card context menu instead (§ Card context menu, #2059)
  - During an active card drag, hide the centered overlay so the existing drop-target indicator owns the visual frame
- **Populated cells** keep the dense info-rich layout. The `+ Add card` affordance is the bottom-aligned button (`w-full text-left text-xs text-fg-faint hover:text-fg-secondary hover:bg-surface-hover/50 mt-1`) and the cell wrapper is *not* `role="button"` — power users can Tab from the last card directly into the bottom button without the cell intercepting Enter.
- **Never render two creation affordances on the same cell.** Empty cells have the cell-as-button only; populated cells have the bottom button only. The two states are mutually exclusive.
- Board stats corner cell (top-left, where header row meets swimlane column): stacked `text-xs text-fg-muted` lines for col/lane/card counts

## Board grid overlay slot (#1147)

The board grid has one pluggable shading layer. An overlay (`src/gridOverlays/`) supplies
a per-cell `{value, label}`; the slot owns the scale, every class, the legend, and the
empty state. Rules:

- **An overlay supplies numbers, never classes.** `GridOverlay.compute()` returns values
  and labels only. The ramp, the non-color channels, the legend and the empty state live
  in `src/gridOverlays/scale.ts` and `slot.ts`, so #969's dual-encoding rule is enforced
  once for every overlay instead of once per overlay. An overlay that *could* ship a
  hue-only ramp is a bug in the slot, not in the overlay.
- **Severity is encoded three ways, and none of them is a font glyph (#969).** Tint
  (`bg-primary-emphasis/10 /20 /30 /40`) + a bar pinned to the cell's bottom edge
  (`h-px / h-0.5 / h-[3px] / h-1`, `bg-primary-emphasis` at `/50 /70 /85 /100`) + the
  value itself. Never `▁▃▅▇` or any block-element glyph: they do track `currentColor`,
  but their advance width and baseline are font-dependent, so the ramp's step size is not
  reproducible across platforms. Never `⚠` / `⛔` on a magnitude overlay — those belong to
  actionable severity (§ Column headers, § Move-blocked toast), and a high card count is
  not a problem.
- **A generic overlay ramps one hue; it never borrows success→warning→danger.** A scalar
  the slot cannot interpret means "more/less", not "safe/dangerous" — `bg-warning/*` is
  the card aging tint and `bg-danger/*` is over-WIP (and, on cards, the 2× aging stage), so a red cell would assert a judgment
  the overlay cannot justify. (The card aging ramp is the deliberate exception: it is a
  severity signal, not a generic scalar, so it does step yellow → orange → red → deep red —
  see § Card aging ramp. It still has no green step.) The ramp is `primary-emphasis`, identical in both themes, so
  it is verified once. **Never `bg-info/*` inside a cell:** `/10` is the active-toggle
  state, `/15` the filter-match pulse on collapsed stubs, `/20` the drop-target indicator.
  The ramp starts at `/10`; `/5` is below the perceptibility floor over `bg-canvas`.
- **The tint is the first child of the cell root, `z-0`, `pointer-events-none`,
  `aria-hidden`; the value badge is a sibling at `z-[5]`.** A positioned descendant paints
  above non-positioned in-flow content regardless of DOM order, so the card stack and the
  affordances after it carry `relative` and beat the tint on DOM order. The badge needs the
  opposite outcome and cannot get it from DOM order at all: `CardItem`'s root is
  `relative z-0`, so a z-auto badge earlier in the subtree paints *underneath* the first
  card. The three numbers in play are exact — `z-[5]` clears the cards, stays **below** the
  sticky swimlane label panel (`z-10`) and the sticky header row (`z-20`) which a badge
  must never cover when the grid is scrolled, and still yields to a hovered card
  (`hover:z-20`) so the card's own selection checkbox stays usable in the same corner.
  The built-in `cards.length` badge (`data-testid="cell-card-count"`) is the same case and
  carries the same `z-[5]` (#1162); any new absolutely positioned cell affordance that must
  sit over the cards needs it too.
  Never put the tint on the cell root's `bg-*`: the root already carries `bg-canvas` and
  the drag-over `bg-surface-hover/40`, and a third `bg-*` on one node is a Tailwind
  ordering coin-flip.
  The overlay value badge is an opaque tab (`top-0 right-1 rounded-b bg-canvas px-1
  leading-[13px]`), #459. Opaque, because a card's border would otherwise run through a
  value as wide as `12.0d`. Exactly 13px tall, because that is the room above a card's
  contents: the cell's 8px padding, the card's 1px border, then the checkbox's 4px inset;
  the title starts lower still, at 17px, or 15px in the compact grid. Never lower or in
  another corner: a full compact grid has no free row, so a bottom corner lands on a card's
  avatar, and adding room would reflow the grid, which this slot forbids. `e2e/grid-overlay-dwell`
  measures the tab's box against every card's title, checkbox and avatar in both layouts.
  `text-fg-secondary` on `bg-canvas` is 13.59:1 dark and 9.45:1 light, so the badge passes on
  every step. Cards are opaque and paint above the tint, so a card's own text is never on
  it. What does sit on the tint is the populated cell's `+ Add card` button, which takes
  `text-fg-secondary` on a tinted cell (7.71 dark, 6.01 light at `/40`) instead of its usual
  `text-fg-faint`; `text-fg-tertiary` is 4.47 and 4.40 there and fails. The bottom bar is not
  a text carrier and need not reach 3:1 against the tint (2.14 light at `/40`): it repeats
  the numeral, it is not the only way to read it. The tab is above a card at rest, so the
  card needs no lift to show its checkbox; a hovered card (`hover:z-20`) still rises over it.
  The checkbox's box starts at 13px, but its 2px focus ring starts at 11px and `CardItem`'s
  classes are pinned by the Classic snapshots, so the tab fades
  (`group-has-[[data-card-id]_:focus-visible]/cell:opacity-0`) while a keyboard user is on
  a control inside a card. `opacity-0`, never hidden, so the reading stays available. The
  coarse-pointer button's 44px hit area also starts under the tab (a 25×4px band), but the
  tab is `pointer-events-none` and the visible `⋯` starts at 17px, so nothing is lost there.
- **An overlay may declare a `scale` of absolute steps, as numbers and words only.**
  `GridOverlay.scale` holds lower bounds and legend wording, never classes, so the ramp
  still lives only in `scale.ts`. Use it when the unit has a fixed meaning (#459: days), so
  a cell is not called hot merely because it is the worst on a healthy board. On an absolute
  scale: a value below the first bound is a real reading (the badge alone, no tint, no bar,
  announced as `below level 1`), a 0 is a reading too and the overlay omits a cell to mean
  "no value", the flat rule does not apply, and the legend names each step and adds a row
  for below the scale. Everything else stays relative-to-max. **The value the slot steps is
  the value the badge shows:** an overlay on fixed bounds rounds before it returns, so
  `3.0d` can never sit in the "under 3" step.
- **An overlay that leaves visible cards out says so in the legend.** `GridOverlay.note`
  returns one sentence with the count, or null. The per-cell reading is not enough, since
  only a screen reader hears it, and a badge has no room. A legend's unused-level dash is
  `aria-hidden` with `sr-only` "no cells" beside it: screen readers skip a bare em dash.
- **`compute()` never reads the clock.** An overlay whose value depends on time declares
  `refreshMs` and reads `ctx.now`, which `useOverlayClock` floors to that interval. So the
  grid is rebuilt only when the bucket changes, and an overlay that declares none is never
  rebuilt because time passed. Never add a per-second tick for a value that shows to a
  tenth of a day.
- **A dwell time comes from `column_entered_at`, never from `last_moved_at`.** A
  swimlane-only move, an archive and a restore all write a movement, so `last_moved_at`
  is not when the card entered its column. `last_moved_at` is the staleness signal (§ Card
  aging ramp); the visit rules are the *Flow metric definitions* ADR's, computed on the
  server. A client-side move has to keep the field true until the response lands, which is
  why `useBoard` writes it in two places and no others: `placeCardOptimistically` sets it
  for the move, and `rollbackMovedCard` restores it if the move fails. A column leaving
  done needs a re-read, since its cards have no time yet; `BoardView` routes that through
  the drag-deferred resync (#1463), never a bare `silentReload()`.
- **A cell tint or z-order change needs a browser check in both themes before merge.**
  jsdom renders no paint order, so a vitest suite will pass over a layer that is invisible
  in the product. Class-level assertions (the z value, the tint token) are a guard against
  regression, not evidence that the layer is visible.
- **The layer is suppressed on the cell being dragged over.** Drop feedback owns the
  background channel — same precedent as hiding the centered `+ Add card` overlay during a
  drag.
- **One scalar per cell corner.** The top-right slot renders *either* the built-in
  `cards.length` badge (at ≥ 2) *or* the overlay's value badge, never both.
- **A cell whose `aria-label` is set must carry the overlay reading inside that label.**
  An empty addable cell is `role="button"` with an `aria-label`, and an `aria-label`
  overrides every descendant's text — an `sr-only` reading there is silently discarded.
  Append it to the cell's label and mark the visible badge `aria-hidden` instead.
- **The legend floats; it is never a strip.** Every above-grid strip is in-flow and
  `shrink-0`, so it shrinks the `flex-1 min-h-0` scroll container — that is displacement,
  which this slot forbids. The legend is `absolute bottom-4 right-4 z-30
  pointer-events-none` inside a `relative` wrapper around the **scroll container only**,
  so it can never cover `BoardActivityDrawer`. `bottom-4` clears `.board-scroll`'s
  always-visible 10px custom scrollbar (`src/index.css`) and `pointer-events-none`
  means a graze can never eat a scrollbar drag. `z-30`
  is deliberately below `BulkActionToolbar` (`z-40`) and the shared toast region (`z-[55]`, § Toasts) — a
  transient message always wins over a passive key. It fades (never unmounts) during a
  drag, and drops to a single compact row below `lg` via `useIsLargeViewport`.
- **The legend's empty state is filter-aware, and it is the second one-line empty state
  in the system.** § Empty states' centered-icon pattern is for a surface with nothing in
  it, and the board is full of cards, so the legend uses the one-line form at `text-xs
  italic` — but `text-fg-muted`, not the `text-fg-faint` the card-detail sub-sections use:
  faint is a decorative tone and this is the only sentence the panel shows. The copy must
  name the actual cause — `No values match the active filters.` when `isFiltered`, and
  `No values on this board yet.` otherwise. "Nothing on this board" is a lie when the
  filter bar is what emptied the overlay.
- **`role="group"`, never `role="region"`** — the board is capped at four landmarks
  (§ Top chrome). Swatches are `aria-hidden`; each legend row carries an `sr-only` level
  prefix, and an unused level renders `—`, never `0`.
- **The picker never folds into `OverflowMenu`.** It is the one Zone 2 control exempt from
  the fold set: an `OverflowItem` carries an action, not a selection, and the never-folded
  set still fits Row 2 at every supported width. It lives right after **Filters** (Filters
  changes *which cards*; the overlay changes *what is drawn over them*), passes
  `portalMenu`, and maps "None" to `selected={null}` so the trigger reads `Overlay` at
  rest and takes the primitive's active treatment — showing the overlay's own name —
  exactly when one is on. `triggerPrefix={OverlayIcon}` is not decorative: it is what keeps
  the control identifiable once its text becomes the option name.
- **Overlay ids are a persisted contract.** `board:{boardId}:grid-overlay` stores the raw
  id and unknown ids decay to `none` through `resolveGridOverlayId` — a board preset
  (#1146) or a stale value may name an overlay this build has never heard of, and an
  un-overlaid board is a strictly better failure than a board that does not render. Never
  rename a shipped id; `label` is free to be reworded.
- **Never gate the overlay on `canEdit` or `isAdmin`** — it is a local reading preference
  with no server write, so every role gets it. It renders only in `view === "board"`.
- **Two live regions on the board is deliberate.** The overlay announcer sits beside the
  DnD announcer because they are driven by disjoint actions (a pointer drag vs. a toolbar
  click); the one-region-per-action rule targets two regions racing on a *single* action.
  Cell values themselves carry no `aria-live`: they change from other users' WebSocket
  events, and announcing those would make the board unusable with a screen reader.

## Board presets (#1146)

A preset is a named bundle of how the board view presents a board (`src/boardPresets/registry.ts`). In 1.3 there are exactly two, code-defined: **Classic** and **Default**. Rules:

- **Classic is current behavior, byte for byte, and stays that way.** Its bundle is the no-op bundle (board density, no overlay, no fields, no filter), and `boardPresetsBoardView.test.tsx` renders the real grid under `?preset=classic` and pins it with one snapshot per density tier (comfortable, standard, dense). A card-face change that would alter that snapshot (e.g. #871's redesign) must be gated out of Classic, not land by updating the snapshot. Named exception: the Comfortable urgency border tint and its screen-reader priority text (#977, owner decision 2026-11-13) apply in Classic too; they reinforce the urgency badge Classic already shows, and the snapshot change was accepted. Second named exception: the number of pinned swimlane chips shown on a row follows card density in Classic too (#1417, owner decision 2026-11-13; see § Swimlane label panel). Classic has no preset density of its own, so gating it would leave the cap with no effect on boards without a preset; `boardPresetsBoardView.test.tsx` pins it with a Classic fixture of pinned row values (the snapshots above use none). **How a card-face change is gated (#871):** `CardItem` calls `useClassicCardFace(boardId)` (`boardPresets/activePresetStore.ts`), which reads the active preset `useBoardPreset` publishes (in a layout effect, so a Default board never paints the Classic face first) and is `true` for Classic, for no active preset (the snapshot test pins Classic equal to it), and outside a board view. Branch on it inside `CardItem` and keep the Classic branch's markup and classes identical to before the change; do not thread a prop through `SwimlaneRow` / `BoardCell`. The #871 urgency glyphs, the untinted `text-warning-on-tint` due-soon tone and the dense due pill's glyph + tone are gated this way, so Classic keeps `⚑` / `⏱` and plain `text-warning` until it retires.
- **Named exception (#1122): custom-field threshold marks render under Classic.** A board admin opts in per field (Board settings → Card fields → Thresholds), so every existing board stays byte-identical until then, and a mark that appeared only under Default would make a threshold's meaning depend on the viewer's preset. `boardPresetsBoardView.test.tsx` pins this with its own Classic case (a marked chip), separate from the per-density snapshots.
- **Resolve density and overlay once, through `useBoardPreset`.** `BoardView` reads `boardPreset.display` for both; never compute `override ?? board.card_density` or read `board:{id}:grid-overlay` anywhere else, or a board default preset is half-applied. Precedence: a `?preset=` link (applied by writing the keys), then the member's own keys element by element, then `Board.default_preset`, then the board itself.
- **No new server state; one per-user marker (accepted by the owner ruling on #1146, 2026-11-03).** A preset writes the two existing per-user keys (`board:{id}:card-density-override`, `board:{id}:grid-overlay`), the URL, and the marker `board:{id}:preset` naming the preset that wrote them (none when the board default was applied, which clears the keys). The active preset is the marker, else `Board.default_preset` (`resolveActivePresetId`), resolved from those current inputs on every render: no in-tab choice may outlive the input it recorded, or a tab and a reload disagree (applying the board default records no marker, so a later change of board default is followed). **Never infer a preset from the keys' values**: they predate presets, so a member's own 1.2 density or a stored `none` overlay is their own setting even when it matches a preset, and Settings → Display must not say "Set by preset" for it (`densitySource`). A new element (fields, filter, attention) writes its own existing state, and a change to an element's meaning bumps `BOARD_PRESET_SCHEMA_VERSION`.
- **Preset state belongs to one board.** `App` renders `BoardView` with `key={board.id}`, because the `/boards/:id` route stays mounted across ids; without it every board-scoped hook (density, overlay, preset, its notice and Undo) carried over to the next board. `useBoardPreset` also resets itself if `boardId` changes under it, and an Undo never writes to a board other than the one its snapshot came from.
- **Ids are a persisted, public contract.** They are stored in `Board.default_preset` and in `?preset=` links and mirrored by `BoardPresetId` in `backend/boards/models.py`. Never rename or remove one; names and descriptions are free to change.
- **No domain semantics.** A preset references field definitions and choices by id and ships none of its own; no preset names a label, a field, or a business concept.
- **Picker.** `SingleSelectDropdown` with `portalMenu` and `triggerPrefix={PresetIcon}`, first control in Zone 2 right after the zone 1/2 divider, never folded into the overflow kebab (a selection, like the Overlay picker). Trigger reads `Preset` at rest and the preset's name otherwise, truncated at `max-w-[10rem]`; accessible name `Preset: <name>` plus `, modified`. Each option shows its one-line summary as its `description`; the board default is marked `Board default` in that line (not `Default`, which is also a preset's name). Choosing the active, unmodified preset changes no state but raises a short `info` toast (`“<name>” is already applied`, 4s, key `preset-already-active`, no Undo, #2124) so the choice is acknowledged and announced (a no-op that the member can mistake for a dropped click is acknowledged this way, never closed silently; it is a courtesy toast with no action, so at the cap of 3 it may wait behind a real notice); the ⌘K apply command does the same. The trigger carries `title=<preset name>` while a preset is active (the truncated name is otherwise unreachable), via `SingleSelectDropdown`'s `triggerTitle`.
- **Modified.** A dashed `border-line-strong` `Modified` text tag on the trigger; the menu's `menuHeader` lists each difference as `Density: Dense (preset: Standard)`, and a `Reset to “<name>”` menu action re-applies it. Once modified, `?preset=` is dropped from the URL, so a reload never silently re-applies the preset over the member's changes. Opening a `?preset=` link overwrites the member's keys, so it raises the same Undo notice (`Applied “<name>” from the link`); its Undo restores the old keys and marker and drops the link. A notice for a change the member did not start stays 20s, not 8s (`PRESET_LINK_NOTICE_MS`): focus is on the page, so pause-on-focus never engages. Every notice's live text ends `Undo is also in the Preset menu.`, visible copy unchanged.
- **⌘K commands.** On a board the palette offers `Apply preset: <name>` for every preset but the active one, and `Reset to preset: <name>` when the active one is modified. They are hidden while the palette has a card target (#2059): the palette is then about that card. They reach BoardView as the `visiban:preset-command` window event and take the picker's apply path (same no-op rule, same notice); the palette learns the active preset from `boardPresets/activePresetStore.ts`, which `useBoardPreset` publishes. No new shortcut key. **Board-scoped palette commands read `<Verb> <noun>: <name>` and reuse the picker's verb** (`Apply preset: Default`, `Reset to preset: Default` beside the picker's `Reset to “Default”`), so a command and its matching picker action and notice say the same thing.
- **Every apply and reset gets an Undo notice** (`usePresetNoticeToast`, an `info` toast in the shared region, § Toasts), in every view (Board, Summary, History, Analytics, Lens): a ⌘K command or a link can apply a preset anywhere, so `BoardView` calls the hook once, above every view branch. A `?preset=` link that changes nothing the member has stored (they picked it, then reloaded) raises no notice. Informational tone like § Post-import notice, auto-dismiss after 8s (20s for a link), paused on hover or focus by the region, announced with an Undo hint.
- **An auto-dismissing notice's Undo must be reachable from the keyboard without racing its timer.** The notice sits far later in the tab order than an 8s timer lets a keyboard user travel, so while it is up the control that caused it offers the same action (the picker's `Undo: Applied “<name>”` menu action). Undo or × in the notice returns focus to that control, because the buttons unmount with it. A new toast that carries the only undo follows the same pattern. `ImportSkippedToast` needs no such path (#2107): it persists when it lists warnings, and its list and `×` are in the Tab order of the region, one Tab from the board grid.
- **Name the source when two controls write one value.** Where a preset wrote the personal density key, the `Use my own density` toggle's description names the preset and what turning it off returns to, and the standalone `Set by preset` line is not repeated; that line appears only when the density comes from a board default preset, which writes no key. The admin section is titled `Board default preset`, never just `Default preset` beside a preset named Default. **Copy that predicts a consequence is conditional on the state that causes it**: the toggle says "the preset will then show as Modified" only when turning it off changes the density (`densitySource.offChangesDensity`).
- **Two writers share the density key.** The standalone line `Set by preset “<name>”: your view shows <density> density.` (`text-xs text-fg-tertiary`, the `aria-describedby` of the admin Card density group) appears only when the density comes from the board default preset and the member has no key (`densitySource.via === "board-default"`). When a preset the member applied wrote their key (`own-key`), the `Use my own density` toggle's description says so instead, names what turning it off returns to (the board default preset's density, else the board's), and is the `aria-describedby` of the switch and of the personal density group. Turning the toggle on seeds the density the member sees now, never `board.card_density` regardless. The board default preset is an admin radio group in the same tab (None / Classic / Default), with a read-only line for everyone else, like Card density.

## Card density (#961)

Each board has an admin-controlled `card_density` setting that drives how much metadata renders on the card face. The three tiers are:

| Tier | Default for | Card face shows |
|---|---|---|
| `comfortable` | New boards (1.1+) | One urgency badge, one primary label + `+N`, checklist progress, assignee. *Weight, attachments, last-moved, extra labels, priority badge, description indicator → moved to the hover peek.* |
