| `standard` | (admin opt-in) | Adds a second label (2 + `+N`), due date when not folded into the urgency badge, weight pill (`>1`), attachment count. Still suppresses the priority badge (the colored card border carries priority) and last-moved text. **Named `standard` not `compact`** to avoid colliding with the per-user *Card layout: Compact / Expanded* toolbar pref. |
| `dense` | Existing boards migrated from 1.0 | Today's pre-1.1 layout — every metadata field on the card face. **Does not render the new urgency badge** (the per-field cues already cover the same signals). Its standalone due pill carries the same glyph + word instead: a flag and a screen-reader `Overdue: ` prefix before the date in the `danger` tone, or a stopwatch before the date in the `warning-on-tint` tone when due within 72 h (#871). Under the Classic preset (and with no preset) the pill keeps its pre-#871 hue-only form: `text-danger` when overdue, else `text-fg-tertiary`, no glyph (§ Board presets). |

- The new urgency badge is a worst-offender classification: **overdue > due-soon (≤72h) > stale (server `is_stale`) > recent (<24h since last move)**. Implemented in `frontend/src/utils/cardUrgency.ts` (`classifyCardUrgency()`); pure function, server-anchored staleness, deterministic for tests via the optional `now` argument.
- The badge tone follows the design tokens (`text-danger` / `text-warning` / `text-info`) — no filled background, no second-tier border. **When the card carries an aging tint, the badge text uses the on-tint token of its hue** (`text-danger-on-tint` etc., #1550), because it sits on that tint. *Stale* is never the softer `text-fg-secondary` (VoC feedback: too quiet next to a calm card); it follows the aging stage — `text-warning-on-tint` below 2× the threshold, `text-danger-on-tint` from 2× (`staleBadgeToneClass()`, #1841) — and reads `Stale · {idle days}d`. See § Card aging ramp. **Untinted (`stage: none`) warning is `text-warning-on-tint` (#871, § System signals departure 2).** Plain `text-warning` was 3.56:1 on a white card before #2029, so `urgencyBadgeToneClass()` returns the on-tint token for a warning-toned badge at every stage; danger and info keep their plain tokens untinted. **Classic keeps the old face (#871 gate, 2026-11-13):** under the Classic preset, and on a board with no preset (Classic equals it), `urgencyBadgeToneClass(tone, stage, classicFace)` still returns plain `text-warning` for the untinted due-soon badge until Classic retires; see § Board presets. Since #2029 retuned light `--warning` to orange-700 that badge is 5.18:1 on white, so neither it nor the `success` checklist-complete count (green-700, 5.02) is an exception any more; the Classic branch stays as a face decision and keeps its own test in `onTintTokens.test.ts`.
- **Date-based urgency badges carry the formatted date inline** rather than the generic word: an overdue card reads a flag glyph then the date, a due-soon card a stopwatch glyph then the date (`UrgencyGlyph`, inline SVG, #871; the spec's `2d late` / `Tomorrow` wording is still undecided and the code renders `Due 10/30/2026` / `Due tomorrow`). The glyph is `aria-hidden`, so an overdue badge carries a screen-reader-only `Overdue: ` prefix: the visible date alone would not say it has passed. The standalone due-date pill is suppressed at lower densities so the date is never duplicated, and never lost.
- At Comfortable density the standalone due-date pill is suppressed entirely (urgency badge is the single date signal; non-urgent dates move to the peek). At Standard the standalone pill is restored for cards outside the urgency window. At Dense the urgency badge is not rendered at all — the existing per-field cues stay.
- `density` is a *required* prop on `CardItem`'s public TypeScript shape but defaults to `"comfortable"` so any caller that forgets to pass it (e.g. drag overlay constructed without a board context) degrades gracefully.
- The hover peek (`CardPeekPopover`, suppressed while the card detail sheet is open: see § Card detail sheet placement, #1918) renders the hidden metrics as a single muted line — `Weight 5 · 3 attachments · Moved 2d ago`. **Never stack them into multiple rows** — that recreates the wall-of-icons we just removed from the card face.
- **Non-pinned custom fields get exactly one line of their own in the peek, above the metrics line — never one row per field (#1308).** This is the one deliberate exception to the single-line rule above: a label that scopes custom fields cannot also scope weight/attachments sharing its line. When the board has any `show_on_card` definition, the line opens with `Other fields — ` (`font-medium text-fg-tertiary`) so a peek entry (`AE: Bob`) can never be read as the card-face chip (`SA: Glenda`) disagreeing with itself; with nothing pinned, no label. Key the label off `show_on_card`, never a pin count (#1146 presets). Pinned fields are **not** repeated in the peek. Each entry is its own `<span className="inline-block max-w-full truncate align-bottom" title="{name}: {value} — {help_text}">` (clipped like the card-face chip, so a long unbroken value never overflows the 288px panel) (help text omitted when empty) — there is no separate full-name field, so the field's help text is the only place an abbreviated name can be spelled out, and a per-entry element keeps room for #1122 threshold coloring. Cap at 6 visible slots: 5 entries + `+N more`, whose `title` lists the hidden field names.
- Per-user per-field hide toggles (the prior `hideLabels` / `hideDueDate` / `hideAssignee` / `hidePriority` / `hideLastMoved` checkboxes in Board Settings → Display) are removed in 1.1. Density is the single knob; legacy localStorage values for those keys are silently ignored.
- The Board Settings *Display* tab gives admins a radio group (Comfortable / Standard / Dense) with a one-line description of each tier. Non-admins see a read-only line stating the current density.
- **Per-user density override, layered on the board default (#974).** Every user (admin or not) can additionally set a personal density that overrides the board admin's `card_density` for their own view only, via a "Use my own density" toggle beneath the admin radio in Board Settings → Display. Rules:
  - **Storage is board-scoped, not global**: `useCardDensityOverride` (`frontend/src/hooks/useCardDensityOverride.ts`) persists to `board:{boardId}:card-density-override`, holding one of `comfortable` / `standard` / `dense`. Absence of the key — not a stored `null` — means "follow board default"; toggling the override off calls `localStorage.removeItem`. A global `user:prefs:*` key would be wrong here because "follow board default" only has meaning relative to *this* board's admin setting.
  - **Resolution happens once, in `BoardView`**: `effectiveCardDensity = cardDensityOverride ?? board.card_density` is computed a single time and passed through the *same* `density` prop that already flows `BoardView` → `SwimlaneRow` → `BoardCell` → `CardItem` (and the drag-overlay `CardItem`). Never read `board.card_density` directly at a card-rendering call site again — always the resolved value — or a density change ends up half-applied (e.g. dense card face but a comfortable-computed row height).
  - **The admin's board-level radio and the personal toggle+radio are separate, non-stomping storage locations by construction** — writing one never touches the other. An admin changing the board default does not clear any user's personal override, including the admin's own.
  - **The personal radio group is a distinct `name` (`personal-card-density`) from the admin group's (`card-density`)** so the two native radio sets never collide, and is conditionally rendered (not just visually hidden) when the toggle is off, matching the personal Columns/Swimlanes section's own conditional-render pattern in the same tab.
  - **Gate the personal-override UI on `onSetCardDensityOverride` alone** — never couple it to `isAdmin` or to the `viewPrefs`/hidden-column gate. Density override and column/swimlane visibility are unrelated personal settings that happen to share this tab; admins get both the board radio and their own personal toggle, since an admin may want a personal view that differs from the team default they set.

**Card aging ramp (#1841).** `agingTint()` in `src/utils/agingTint.ts` is the single source for the card's aging stage, idle days, overlay and body-opacity classes. Never recompute the idle ratio in a component. The analytics heatmap's `heatLevel` shares its edges through `agingStage()`.

- **Stages, by uncapped ratio = idle / `staleness_threshold_days`, lower bound inclusive:** none (below the warning point), `warning` (warning point ≤ r < 1), `stale` (1–2), `stale2x` (2–3), `stale3x` (≥ 3). The multiples are fixed, not a setting. The warning point is `stale_warning_pct / 100` (percent of the threshold *used*). `0` turns it off, `100` leaves it empty.
- **No green stage.** Color means "needs attention". A fresh card is never tinted.
- **Cards in a done column (`column.is_done`) never age.** `BoardCell` (and the drag overlay in `BoardView`) passes `inDoneColumn`, and `CardItem` then shows no tint, no aging tooltip and no "Stale" badge, even when the server's `is_stale` is true (it does not look at columns). A finished card is not idle. The stale-card digest (`notify_stale_cards`) currently still includes Done-column cards, tracked in #1845.
- **Age from `agingReference(card)`, never `card.last_moved_at` directly.** It falls back to `created_at` for a never-moved card (Trello/CSV imports), mirroring the server's `is_stale` fallback, so imported boards get the ramp and `Stale · Nd` too. Test fixtures with `last_moved_at: null` therefore need a relative-to-now `created_at`, or the card tints.
- **A selected card drops its `bg-primary-emphasis/20` fill while an aging tint is shown** and keeps only `ring-2 ring-primary-soft`. The fill under a 3× tint pulled the badge to 3.71:1 light / 3.95:1 dark.
- **Urgency badges on a tint use an on-tint token that clears 4.5:1 on that stage** (`urgencyBadgeToneClass()`): the badge hue's on-tint token, except that on the red 2×/3× stages a warning-toned badge (due-soon) takes `text-danger-on-tint`, because amber `warning-on-tint` measures 4.38:1 / 3.94:1 in light mode there. `onTintTokens.test.ts` pins these ratios.
- **Overlay + opacity per stage:** `bg-warning-emphasis/10` + `opacity-90` (yellow), `bg-stale-tint/15` + `opacity-85` (orange; `--stale-tint` is pinned to the pre-#2029 orange-600/400 so the light `--warning` retune cannot darken it, #2029), `bg-danger/15` + `opacity-85` (red), `bg-stale-severe/20 ring-2 ring-inset ring-stale-severe` + `opacity-85` (deep red). Do not go below `opacity-85` or above these fills. The old `bg-warning/20` + `opacity-60` stale stage put the card title at 4.15:1 dark / 4.33:1 light and the badge at 1.9 to 2.6:1.
- **The 3× outline is an inset ring on the overlay div, never the card border.** The card's border carries the priority color (or the urgency hue on overdue/due-soon cards at Comfortable) and must stay visible. In dark mode `--stale-severe` is red-500, not a maroon: a maroon `/20` over slate-800 measures about 1.03:1 and is invisible, so the ring is what separates 3× from 2× there. Never use black or near-black for the last stage, because the dark canvas is near-black.
- **Text carries the age; color and opacity only reinforce it.** The non-color cues are the badge `Stale · 41d` (comfortable/standard, server `is_stale`), the `AgingCue` stage meter where the badge is not shown (#1843; the warning stage's hollow-bar meter, #1863), the tooltip (`formatAgingTooltip()`) and the 3× ring. The opacity step (100 → 90 → 85) is a weak supplementary cue, not a channel of its own: 1–2× and 2–3× share `opacity-85` and differ by hue plus text. The tooltip claims "Stale" only when the server agrees, and otherwise reads `Idle 15d · stale at 14d`.
- **The aging tooltip opens on hover and on keyboard focus (`:focus-visible`), never on a pointer click's focus.** It wraps at `max-w-[16rem]` (`w-max`, centered) instead of `whitespace-nowrap`, so the longest string (`Stale · 41d · 2.9× the 14d threshold · blocked by 2 cards`) cannot overflow a narrow column.
- **Every tinted card carries a non-color cue at a glance (#1843, #1863):** the `Stale · Nd` badge where it is visible, otherwise the `AgingCue` stage meter at the start of the title. The badge is absent on every compact card (any density), at dense, and when overdue/due-soon preempts it. The `warning` stage never gets the badge (it is not stale), so its meter shows on every warning card in every layout and density: the same three bars with no solid bar and the shortest bar drawn hollow (outline only). The dense `moved MM/DD` text is a date, not a stage statement, so it does not replace the meter.
- **`AgingCue` (`components/Card/AgingCue.tsx`) is the only stage glyph — never re-inline it.** CardItem renders it when `!staleBadgeShown` (`!compact && urgency?.kind === "stale"`), and it returns null only for `none`. Three ascending bars in a 12×12 viewBox, solid count = stage multiple (1/2/3), the rest `opacity="0.3"`, tone `staleBadgeToneClass()` (no new tokens; the badge's measured on-tint ratios apply). `warning` has 0 solid bars and draws the shortest bar hollow: `fill="none" stroke="currentColor" strokeWidth={1}`, inset by half the stroke (`x 0.5, y 7.5, 2×4`) so the outline's outer edge matches the filled bar's footprint and stays inside the viewBox. Hollow → 1 → 2 → 3 solid reads as a progression by shape, and the outline reads as a hint, not an alarm. The hollow bar's 1px interior was checked in a browser at device pixel ratios 1, 1.25, 1.5 and 2 in both themes and stays open (it does not smear into a solid bar). Any change to the meter's geometry or stroke must repeat that fractional-DPR check, because jsdom renders no pixels. A new `AgingStage` needs a `SOLID_BARS` entry, or the cue silently returns null for it. Gate on the client `aging.stage`, never `is_stale`, so the cue always matches the tint.
  - **Placement: first, inline, inside the title `<p>`** (`inline-block w-3 h-3 mr-1 align-[-0.125em]`). It shares the clamped title line box, so card height cannot change. Never in the metadata row (that adds a row and `mt-1.5` to a title-only compact card), never trailing (the bulk-select checkbox covers the top-right), and never wrapped in `inline-flex` (moves the baseline onto the SVG).
  - **Height guard:** any inline glyph in the card title `<p>` is at most 12px (`h-3`) and sits at `align-[-0.125em]`, not `align-baseline`: on the baseline a 12px glyph leaves under 1px of headroom in a `text-xs leading-snug` line, so a different font stack can grow the line. `card-aging-compact.spec.ts` measures the stale and warning titles' line boxes against the fresh card's and their own `line-height`, compact and non-compact dense, and the warning title at non-compact comfortable and standard.
  - **Contrast model (WCAG 1.4.11):** the solid bars carry the stage and must hold at least 3:1; they inherit the badge's on-tint ratios (at least 4.55:1), so never give the cue its own tone. The warning stage's hollow bar is its only stage-bearing mark and must stay at full opacity: `text-warning-on-tint` in the `opacity-90` body measures 6.25:1 dark / 5.53:1 light on the warning tint (pinned in `onTintTokens.test.ts`). Never render the warning stage as an all-ghost meter: the ghosts are well below 3:1 (not pinned). The `opacity="0.3"` ghost bars are deliberately below 3:1 and redundant, since the solid count (or the hollow bar) alone carries the stage. Do not raise their opacity, or the stages blur together. The cue is a text-free graphic, so the `text-xs` floor does not apply; never add text such as `41d` to it at a smaller size.
  - **Accessibility:** `role="img"` on the `<svg>` with an `aria-label` that states days and multiple (`Idle 41 days, at least 2 times the stale threshold`; warning: `Idle 9 days, approaching the stale threshold`), never "Stale" and never `×`. No `title`: the card's aging tooltip already covers hover and keyboard focus (touch has the accessible name only, as with the tooltip). Because the cue leads the title, the card button's content-derived name starts with `Idle N days, …` before the title. That reading order is accepted, since the leading position is the hard visual constraint. Do not move the cue after the title to fix it.
  - **A compact card's `hasMetadataRow` must not count anything the compact branch does not render.** Only the assignee, blocked glyph, recently-moved dot and dense priority badge render in compact; every other condition sits inside a `!compact &&` group. Counting the rest (urgency badge, labels, checklist, moved-ago text and others) added an empty row and 6px to compact cards.
- **Metadata on a tint is rescoped, not restyled per element (#1844).** Whenever a tint is shown, `CardItem` adds `AGING_STAGE_CLASSES[stage].metaClass` (`card-aging-meta`) to the metadata row, and that class in `index.css` rescopes the row's text tokens for the subtree: `fg-tertiary`, `fg-muted` and `fg-faint` → `muted-on-tint`, `info` → `info-on-tint`, `danger` → `danger-on-tint`, `success` → `success-on-aging-tint`. At rest, every metadata element (blocked glyph, external-ref badge, description glyph, `+N` labels, custom-field chip names and links, the dense empty-field ghost, multi-select `+N`, checklist, attachments, due date, `moved MM/DD`) and every child component in the row picks it up with no per-element class, in both default and compact layouts. Rules: (1) Never put the class on an untinted card, and never on the title or the avatar, which sit outside the row. (2) A new metadata element must use a token in that list, `fg-secondary`, or an explicit `*-on-tint` token, and, because the same element also renders untinted, not `fg-muted` or `fg-faint` (#1862, below). `onTintTokens.test.ts` scans `CardItem` and every component it mounts inside the row (`CustomFieldLink`, `MultiSelectChips`, `ChoiceValue`, `ExternalRefGlyph`; a `./` import that is in neither that list nor the portaled list fails the test) for every `text-<color>` utility and fails on any other (for example plain `text-warning` or `text-primary-soft`, which the class does not rescope) and on any opacity modifier on a text color (`text-fg-muted/60`), because the pinned ratios assume full-opacity text. (3) On a tint, `fg-tertiary`, `fg-muted` and `fg-secondary` all render slate-300 dark / slate-700 light (`fg-secondary` is not rescoped; its value currently equals `muted-on-tint`, which the test asserts). The tertiary/muted gradation is lost there on purpose: slate-400 dark and slate-600 light both measure below 4.5:1 on the 3× stage, so size, weight and position carry the hierarchy. The empty-field ghost takes the same tone; its dashed border and `—` still mark it as unset. (4) Tuning the overlay alpha or body opacity cannot fix this. `fg-muted` is 3.07:1 on an untinted dark card, below AA before any tint, so only the text tone can clear it. The ramp's fills and opacities are therefore unchanged. (5) `success-on-aging-tint` exists because `success-on-tint` (green-800) is 3.80:1 in light mode on the 3× stage. Light uses green-900 (4.62:1). Dark is green-300, the same as `success-on-tint`.
- **Exceptions, not rescoped:** label pills (user-assigned colors, § Badges and labels), which compensate for the stage opacity instead: `CardItem` passes `aging.stage` to `colorChipStyle`, so a label chip holds 6:1 as rendered on every light stage and 5.91 at worst on a dark one (#2102, pinned in `colorContrast.test.ts`; before it was 4.26-4.34 light on the stale stages); choice badges and multi-select chips, which carry their own background and measure at least 4.66:1 under the stage opacity (asserted in the same test; the AA floor for them, #2102 left their recipe unchanged); and two filled elements whose contrast is a property of their fill, not of the tint, neither fixed by the tint rules (the #1861 audit that tracked them is closed, and no open issue covers them): the dense-only priority badge (white on `PRIORITY_COLORS`: medium 2.80:1 and high 3.76:1 even untinted, 2.43 and 3.18 at worst under the stage opacity; urgent stays at least 5.12) and the assignee avatar initials (`Avatar`, `text-on-primary` on its `PALETTE` fill: teal 3.74 and cyan 3.68 untinted, as low as 3.06 on a tint; `warning-bg` was 3.19 / 2.67 until #2169 made it amber-700, now 5.02 untinted and 3.88 at worst on a tint). The avatar sits outside the metadata row, so `card-aging-meta` does not reach it.
- **Drag preview exception (measured, transient).** The `DragOverlay` copy of a card renders at `opacity-95` (`overlay` in `CardItem`), which lets the board behind it bleed through the tint, so a tinted card's text dips below 4.5:1 only while it is being dragged. Worst measured values, over canvas, sunken, surface and surface-hover backdrops: light 3× `info-on-tint` 4.14, `success-on-aging-tint` 4.18 and `danger-on-tint` 4.21; the #1841 `warning-on-tint` badge on light stale 4.18; dark worst `danger-on-tint` 4.47 on stale over a surface-hover backdrop. The at-rest guarantee above is unaffected. Do not change the drag-preview opacity to fix this, because it would restyle every dragged card, and do not cite the ramp as AA during a drag.
- **Untinted card-face text is `fg-tertiary` at its quietest (#1862).** `text-fg-muted` is 3.07:1 on an untinted dark card and the dense empty-field ghost's `text-fg-faint` was 1.93 dark / 2.56 light, both informative text, so every such element moved to `text-fg-tertiary` (5.71 / 7.58): the external-ref badge without a link, the description glyph, custom-field chip names (card face, and `CustomFieldValueDisplay` `chip`/`row-chip` so the row chip still matches), the multi-select `+N`, the dense empty-field ghost and the `moved MM/DD` text, plus, in the swimlane row-chip cluster, the `+N`/`All fields` trigger and the admin-only padlock (`AdminOnlyFieldGlyph`, every surface). The sibling surfaces that carry the same metadata or field names got the same floor: `CardPeekPopover` (other fields, the metrics line that comfortable density moves off the face, the footer, completed checklist items), `SwimlaneFieldsPopover` (field names and the `Fields`/`On row` headings), `SwimlaneFieldEditRow` (field label and help text) and `ArchivedCardsPanel` (the count, `Loading…`, and the archived-from / archived-at lines on its `bg-sunken` rows, where `fg-muted` is 3.75:1 dark and `fg-tertiary` 6.96 / 7.24). **Deferred to #2030: `LensIssueCard`**, whose muted text sits on a card with `hover:bg-surface-hover`, where even `fg-tertiary` is 4.04:1 dark, so a plain swap does not clear AA and it needs a design call. **Also deferred to #2030: the card-detail panel** (`CardDetail`, about 36 `fg-muted` uses; `CustomFieldEditRow`, the card-detail twin of `SwimlaneFieldEditRow`; and the shared `CustomFieldValueInput`, whose prefix/suffix adornment, `Select a date` text on `bg-surface-hover` and `fg-faint` clear button are informative), because it belongs to the app-wide token decision rather than the card face. **Decision: promote per use, do not retune the tokens.** Retuning dark `fg-muted` to clear 4.5:1 on `bg-surface` (for example `132 147 169`, 4.69:1) would change about 570 uses app-wide, compress the muted/tertiary step to 1.22:1, and drop the `Avatar` `?` fallback (white on `bg-fg-muted`) from 4.76 to 3.12; that needs its own rendered review, #2030. `fg-faint` stays the decorative/hover-reveal tone. Retuning it would erase the faint step everywhere, so the ghost is promoted instead. Consequences: (a) on the card face, field name and the other quiet metadata share one tone, as they already did on a tint; the value (`fg-secondary`, 9.85 / 10.35) is still a step above. (b) The ghost now reads as loud as a set chip's name, so the dashed border and `—` are what mark it unset; never rely on tone alone for that. (c) Tinted rendering is unchanged, because `card-aging-meta` maps `fg-tertiary` to `muted-on-tint` just as it mapped the old tones. `onTintTokens.test.ts` scans the card-face files plus `CustomFieldValueDisplay`, `AdminOnlyFieldGlyph` and the sibling surfaces above, each with the backgrounds its text sits on (`ArchivedCardsPanel`: `bg-surface` and `bg-sunken`), fails on any `fg-muted`/`fg-faint` text (the archived panel's 📦 emoji is the one listed non-text `fg-faint`, exempted as that exact element, so any other `fg-faint` in the file still fails), and pins every other text token used at >= 4.5:1 on each background in both themes (worst: `danger` 5.29 dark on surface / 4.62 light on sunken). No contrast exceptions since #2029: the checklist-complete count (`text-success`, now green-700, 5.02:1 on white / 4.79 on sunken) is checked by the floor test like every other token, and the Classic preset's untinted due-soon badge in plain `text-warning` (now orange-700, 5.18 / 4.95), which Classic keeps until it retires (#871 gate; reachable only through `urgencyBadgeToneClass(…, classicFace)`, never a class literal, with its own test), clears AA too. Outside Classic the badge and dense pill use `text-warning-on-tint` (#871). A new card-face element must use `fg-secondary`, `fg-tertiary`, `text-info`, `text-danger` or an explicit `*-on-tint` token. The scan reads class literals in the listed files only, so a tone produced by a util (`urgencyBadgeToneClass`, `staleBadgeToneClass`) must be fed to the test separately, as the untinted urgency tones and the stale badge tone (`text-warning-on-tint`, 8.67 / 7.31 on surface) are. Known trade-off: the unlinked external-ref badge (`fg-tertiary`) and the linked one (`text-info`, no underline at rest) now have nearly equal luminance in dark mode and differ by hue; accepted because the unlinked form only appears for an invalid URL and its `aria-label`/`title` differ.
- **Subtree token rescope is a narrow pattern (#1844).** Rebinding semantic tokens for a subtree, as `card-aging-meta` does, is allowed only when all of these hold: (1) the subtree always sits on one known tinted surface; (2) only text-role tokens are rebound (`fg-*`, `info`, `danger`, `success`), never `surface`, `line`, `primary` or focus-ring tokens; (3) each rebound token maps to an existing on-tint token, or to a new one recorded with its ratio; (4) a test parses the CSS block and pins the ratios, and lists any non-text utility (`bg-`, `border-`, `ring-` and so on) on a rebound token with a reason, because those shift too; (5) portaled children (the peek, the quick-edit popover) are outside the subtree and are not covered. A token the scope does not rebind (for example `warning`) uses its explicit `*-on-tint` class, as the urgency badge does. Prefer this pattern for a whole tinted region and per-element `*-on-tint` classes for one-off elements, and never use both for the same token in one component.
- Measured WCAG ratios, effective text (opacity-blended over the tint) vs the tint composited on `bg-surface`, dark / light. `onTintTokens.test.ts` derives hue, alpha and opacity from `AGING_STAGE_CLASSES` and fails below 4.5:1, so a change to a stage's classes is re-checked automatically:

  | Stage | `text-fg` (title) | Badge on-tint | Ring vs fill |
  |---|---|---|---|
  | none | 11.87 / 17.85 | — | — |
  | warning | 8.45 / 12.70 | warning-on-tint (AgingCue hollow bar) 6.25 / 5.53 | — |
  | stale (1–2×) | 7.14 / 9.97 | warning-on-tint 5.33 / 4.56 | — |
  | stale2x | 7.42 / 9.60 | danger-on-tint 4.97 / 5.06 | — |
  | stale3x | 7.57 / 8.63 | danger-on-tint 5.08 / 4.55 | 3.21 / 6.91 (vs canvas 5.36 / 9.15) |

  Metadata row, inside `card-aging-meta` (#1844). "Plain" is the unscoped token, which is what the row used before:

  | Stage | `fg-tertiary` plain | `fg-muted` plain | `muted-on-tint` (fg-tertiary, fg-muted, fg-faint; fg-secondary equal) | `info-on-tint` | `danger-on-tint` | `success-on-aging-tint` |
  |---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
  | none (no class) | 5.71 / 7.58 | 3.07 / 4.76 | — | — | — | — |
  | warning | 4.21 / 5.48 | 2.37 / 3.67 | 7.07 / 7.23 | 5.87 / 6.44 | 5.59 / 6.41 | 7.45 / 6.55 |
  | stale (1–2×) | 3.64 / 4.48 | 2.10 / 3.09 | 6.01 / 5.80 | 5.01 / 5.28 | 4.78 / 5.27 | 6.31 / 5.33 |
  | stale2x | 3.78 / 4.32 | 2.18 / 2.98 | 6.24 / 5.59 | 5.21 / 5.07 | 4.97 / 5.06 | 6.54 / 5.14 |
  | stale3x | 3.85 / 3.88 | 2.21 / 2.67 | 6.36 / 5.03 | 5.30 / 4.55 | 5.08 / 4.55 | 6.66 / 4.62 |

  Plain `info` and `danger` were also sub-AA on every tint (as low as 2.97 / 2.96 in light mode on the 3× stage), and plain `success` was 2.05:1 in light mode there. The test pins the lowest value per stage and theme.

**Card-face blocked indicator (#449) — the one card-face signal with no density gate.** When `blocker_count > 0`, `CardItem` renders a danger-toned glyph as the **first** element of the metadata row, outside the `!compact` branch and outside every density conditional.

- **No density gate, and outside `!compact`.** The checklist badge has no gate either, and blocked-ness is more actionable than checklist progress. `comfortable` is the default for every board created since 1.1, so gating it out there would remove the whole-board scan signal — the entire point of the feature — from most boards.
- **First in the row.** The metadata row is `overflow-hidden` with no wrap at rest, so it clips right-to-left: anything placed after a variable-width neighbor (a long label pill, an overdue badge with a long date, a `max-w-[10rem]` custom-field chip) can be cut off on a narrow column. Leading position also keeps every blocked card's marker in one x-gutter, which is what makes a top-to-bottom column scan work.
- **Inline SVG with `currentColor`, never an emoji.** `⛔`/`🚫` paint in their own fixed color and would ignore `text-danger`, so the badge would stop tracking the theme. The neutral `📎`/`✓` badges get away with it; a danger signal does not.
- **Urgency glyphs are inline SVG too (#871).** `UrgencyGlyph` (flag = overdue, stopwatch = due soon) follows the rule above: `currentColor`, `w-3 h-3`, `aria-hidden`, so the badge's tone token colors it and it never renders as an emoji or a fallback-font character the way `⚑`/`⏱` did. The text-variation-selector allowance (`U+FE0E`, § Threshold and status encoding) is for the analytics heatmap and **does not apply to the card face**: no system-signal glyph on the card face (the urgency/overdue flag, the stopwatch, the checklist overdue flag) is a text character, except under the Classic preset, which keeps its `⚑` / `⏱` text glyphs byte for byte until it retires (§ Board presets, #871 gate). Because the SVG is `aria-hidden`, its meaning must also be in text (visible, or `sr-only`), and the file is in `CARD_FACE_FILES` in `onTintTokens.test.ts` so its color classes are scanned.
- **Icon always, numeral only above 1.** At one blocker the numeral repeats what the icon says. The exact count always appears in `aria-label` and `title`.
- **`role="img"` + `aria-label` are required, not optional.** At a count of 1 the element contains no text node, so without them it has no accessible name at all. `title` is retained for the sighted hover tooltip only — it is not a reliable accessible name.
- **Tone only — no fill, no border.** It is therefore not a pill and creates no third exception to the filled-pill rule in § Badges and labels. The precedent is the urgency badge, not the label or custom-field chip.
- **`blocker_count` must be in `arePropsEqual` and in `hasMetadata`.** Without the first, the memo swallows the WebSocket update and the badge never appears; without the second, a card whose only metadata is "blocked" drops the whole metadata row.
- Never duplicate it into `CardPeekPopover` — the peek carries metrics *hidden* at low density, and nothing is hidden here.

## System signals (#2046)

Since 1.2 an admin can color any dropdown or multi-select choice, danger red included, and name one "Blocked". Without a reserved vocabulary, red would mean three unrelated things on one card. These signals are reserved for the system: **shape and text carry the meaning, hue only reinforces it** (WCAG 1.4.1). Required reading before `ux-design` on #871, #1149, #1150 and #1133.

| Signal | Shape + text (the meaning) | Tokens | Owner |
|---|---|---|---|
| **Blocked** | the existing #449 blocked glyph (filled circle with a bar, never a padlock) in the first metadata slot, with the elapsed time appended (`⊘ 3d`) when a manual block exists. One indicator covers manual and relation blocks; a relation-only block keeps today's icon-only form with its `blocker_count` numeral (§ Card-face blocked indicator) and no duration | `danger` text, `danger-on-tint` on a tint | #1149 |
| **Overdue** | flag SVG + date (`Due 10/30/2026`; `2d late` pending) | `danger` / `danger-on-tint` | #871 (`cardUrgency.ts`) |
| **Due soon** | stopwatch SVG + date (`Due tomorrow`; `Tomorrow` pending) (<=72h) | `warning-on-tint`, tinted or not (departure 2) | #871 (`cardUrgency.ts`) |
| **Health** | SVG shape + word, never a bare dot. Reserved slot only: whether health appears on the card face is undecided (#1133 scopes it to Board and Group views), and a card-face health signal needs its own decision | `HEALTH_STATUS_STYLES` + a shared shape component, both added by #1133 | #1133 |
| **Stale / aging** | `Stale · 9d` badge, `AgingCue` meter | `warning-on-tint` below 2x, `danger-on-tint` from 2x; `stale-severe` ring at 3x | § Card aging ramp |
| **Lane over limit** | shape + text such as `Over WIP · 5/4` on the lane header; never a hue-only ring or accent. Reserved here; details belong to #1150, see § Placement limits on the board | fixed danger tokens, defined by #1150 | #1150 |

- **Default priority when several apply on one card face: blocked > overdue > due soon > health > stale.** This orders the signals when more than one applies; it does not redefine the urgency badge, whose own order stays overdue > due soon > stale > recent (§ Cards, `cardUrgency.ts`). Health is a reserved slot in this order, not a promise of a card-face indicator (see the table). Lower-priority signals stay reachable in the card detail and tooltips. Lane over-limit lives on the lane header, so it never competes for the card face.
- **Admin-colored choices render as chips, never as card borders.** Custom-field choice colors (`CHOICE_COLORS`) and label pills (arbitrary user-chosen colors, § Badges and labels) are decoration their owners control: they appear only inside their own chip or pill. The card border and tint belong to the system (priority color, aging ring, selection). System signals use the fixed tokens and shapes above, so a "Blocked" choice or label painted red stays visibly a chip and cannot be mistaken for the blocked signal.
- **No signal is keyed to a label or choice name.** Never match on the text "Blocked", "Urgent" or similar to trigger a signal; signals derive from system fields (block state, due date, `is_stale`, health).
- **Do not reuse the reserved tokens for unrelated meaning.** `urgent` is the priority color and `stale-severe` is the 3x aging stage (see Color tokens); neither is generic danger.

**Departures from the 1.3 UI spec (decided, do not relitigate per feature):**

1. **`HEALTH_STATUS_STYLES` plus a shared shape component** replace the spec's color-only health dot. Each status has a distinct SVG shape and a word. Neither exists in the tree yet; #1133 adds them, and must not ship a hue-only health indicator.
2. **`text-warning-on-tint` on untinted light surfaces, for the card face (shipped in #871; Classic preset excepted, it keeps plain `text-warning` until it retires, § Board presets).** Plain `text-warning` was orange-600, 3.56:1 on white, before #2029 (now orange-700, 5.18:1); the due-soon badge and the other warning-toned text this section specifies (due soon, stale below 2x) use the on-tint token even without a tint behind it. #2029 settled the token question by retuning light `--warning` app-wide (§ Color tokens), so other `text-warning` uses on `bg-surface` clear AA without a swap. Verified against `index.css`: dark `--warning-on-tint` `#FDBA74` (253 186 116) is 8.67:1 on `surface` and 10.59:1 on `surface-sunken`; light `#9A3412` (154 52 18) is 7.31:1 on `surface` and 6.98:1 on `surface-sunken`. `--danger-on-tint` (`#FCA5A5` dark, `#991B1B` light) holds 7.71 / 8.31:1 on `surface`. Pinned in `onTintTokens.test.ts` (#2046).
3. **The blocked glyph carries a duration for manual blocks.** The card face shows one blocked indicator for manual and relation blocks, not two badges: the glyph, plus the elapsed time (`⊘ 3d`) when a manual block exists. A relation-only block keeps the icon-only `blocker_count` form with no duration (#1149 architect review). A glyph alone says "blocked" but not "for how long"; the elapsed time is part of the signal, not a tooltip extra.
4. **Escape priority 42** is allocated to the card-detail block/unblock reason form (§ Escape priority inside the card detail panel). Like any nested form there, it registers through `useEscapeStack`, not an input-level `onKeyDown`.
5. **No `X` shortcut.** The #871 spec proposed `X` to toggle selection; it was denied because selection already has a mechanism. Block/unblock is reached from the card detail's header **Block…** and the banner's **Unblock** (#1149), and since #2059 also from the card context menu (**Block card…** / **Unblock card**, § Card context menu) and the ⌘K palette's card commands (§ Command palette ownership). Both open the sheet with the form open and its field focused, through `CardDetail`'s `blockFormRequest`; neither adds a dialog. Still no bare `X`, and none of `⌘⇧B` / `⌘⌥B` / a scoped `⌘Z` (1.3 UI spec Part 9 keyboard budget: recommended by the spec, none added; owner sign-off pending on the #2059 MR).

**Implementing the blocked signal (#1149).** Decide "is this card blocked" with `isCardBlocked()` (`src/utils/blockedState.ts`), never by reading `card.is_blocked` on the client: CardDetail adjusts `blocker_count` optimistically when a relation changes, and the server-derived flag lags until the next copy. `isManuallyBlocked()` alone decides the elapsed time and the `border-danger-emphasis` card border; a relation-only block keeps the priority border. The indicator's accessible name and `title` come from `blockedIndicatorLabel()`, so the face, the tooltip and a screen reader say the same thing. `blocked_at` / `blocked_reason` change only through `blockCard()` / `unblockCard()` (`src/api/cards.ts`); never send them in a PATCH (the server ignores them). The indicator's name cuts the reason at `BLOCKED_LABEL_REASON_MAX` (120) characters so a column scan does not read paragraphs; the full reason lives in the card detail banner. Durations come from `formatBlockedDuration()` everywhere, Analytics included, so a sub-day block reads in hours and never as `0d`; a duration column pairs with a count column under unambiguous headers (**Time blocked** / **Times blocked**, never "Blocked" / "Blocks").

**When an action's success removes the trigger that opened its form, name the focus target in code.** `useConfirmFocusReturn` covers Cancel and Escape only: it returns focus to the trigger that opened the form. When success unmounts that trigger (Block replaces the header's **Block…** with the banner; Unblock replaces the banner's **Unblock** with **Block…**), focus would fall to `<body>`. Move it explicitly to what replaced the trigger (`CardBlockSection` focuses the banner after a block; `CardDetail` focuses **Block…** after an unblock), and pin it with a test.

**An inline form's reserved helper/error slot is a live region when focus does not move on error** (`role="status" aria-live="polite" aria-atomic="true"` on the always-mounted slot, as `CardBlockSection` does). A server error that lands while focus is already in the field changes the `aria-describedby` target's text, which screen readers do not re-read; the live region announces it.

## Cards

- Container: `bg-surface rounded-lg border p-2.5 cursor-grab`
- **Border color = priority/status indicator** — full border on all sides (not just left accent); at Comfortable, overdue/due-soon override it; see § Card border urgency tint at Comfortable density (#977):
  - Default / low: `border-primary-emphasis`
  - Medium: `border-warning-emphasis`
  - High / blocked: `border-danger-emphasis`
  - No priority: `border-line`
- Card title: `text-sm text-fg`
- No drop shadow — the colored border provides visual weight
- Cards sit directly on the dark cell background; the contrast between `bg-surface` card and `bg-canvas` cell creates depth without shadow
- **Metadata row** (when present — assignee, tasks, due date): `flex items-center gap-1 mt-1.5`, each element `text-xs` in its own tone; the quietest is `text-fg-tertiary`, never `fg-muted`/`fg-faint` (#1862, § Card aging ramp)
  - Task count: `✓ {done}/{total}`
  - Due date: `Due MM/DD/YY`
  - Assignee badge: see Avatar chips section
- **The hover expansion is an overlay inside the cell, never a layout change.** The metadata row clips to one line at rest and wraps while the card is hovered, has keyboard focus on the card itself, or has focus inside the row, so every chip is readable. **Keyboard parity (#1414):** keyboard focus on the card root (`:focus-visible`, the aging tooltip's gate) is the twin of hover, so a card whose overflow is all non-interactive (labels, text chips, checklist and due pills) still expands for a keyboard user; a pointer click's focus does not count (the pointer is already hovering), and focus on another control on the card (the select checkbox, the coarse-pointer ⋯) does not either. Moving focus from the card into the row keeps it expanded. Never drop the card-root trigger back to row-only focus. That expansion must not move anything: the cell's height is shared by every cell in the swimlane row, so a card that grows in flow jumps the whole board under the pointer. `CardItem` freezes its wrapper div at the card's at-rest height and renders the card `absolute left-0 right-0` on top of its cell-mates, then clamps it to the cell's content box (`[data-board-cell]`, minus padding): shifted up when it would run past the cell's bottom edge, `max-height` the cell's inner height, `overflow-hidden`. Covering the other cards in the cell is the intent; growing or leaving the cell is not. The expansion is React state (`expanded`), not `group-hover:`/`focus-within:` classes, so the measure and the overlay land in one layout-effect pass before paint. Do not reintroduce `group-hover:flex-wrap` on the row, and do not put `overflow-hidden` on the cell to clamp it instead: that clips the aging tooltip, the hover lift and the focus ring of the first card. While a card is expanded, `BoardCell` dims every other card in that cell (`dimmed` on `CardItem`: `opacity-[0.08] blur-[2px]` on the wrapper, with a 150ms opacity/filter transition), so the expanded card reads as the cell's only content; `CardItem` reports the expansion through `onExpandChange(cardId, expanded)`, one stable callback per cell, and the cell compares the id on the way out so a stale "ended" from the previous card never clears its neighbor's. The dim is on the wrapper, never the card, so the card's own stacking and the fixed-position peek are unaffected. `cardItemHoverExpand.test.tsx` pins the clamp arithmetic and the dim classes, `boardCell.test.tsx` the dim bookkeeping, and `e2e/card-hover-expand.spec.ts` measures the cell and the cell-mate's opacity in a browser.

### Card border urgency tint at Comfortable density (#977)

At **Comfortable density only** (and not in the compact layout, which shows no urgency badge) the card border is owned by the most urgent signal: **manual blocked > overdue > due soon > priority**. Blocked and overdue use `danger-emphasis`, due soon `warning-emphasis`; otherwise the priority color applies. Stale and "just moved" never change the border. Standard and Dense keep the priority border (a manual block still takes `danger-emphasis` at every density). No extra priority mark is added: when overdue or due soon owns the border, a non-low priority is appended to the card's accessible name (`, Priority: high`, `sr-only`) and the peek already states it. The rule is `classifyCardBorder` in `utils/cardUrgency.ts`, separate from `classifyCardUrgency` (badge only). The border is reinforcement only: the urgency badge stays the authoritative signal, because a high-priority border and an overdue border share the same red, a medium-priority border and a due-soon border are near-identical, and the amber border is under 3:1 on a light surface. Do not give a new border meaning a hue already used by `PRIORITY_COLORS`. The open (`current`) card keeps its primary border, and an overdue card in a done column is tinted like its badge (both ignore the done state).

## Avatar chips

- Shape: `rounded-full flex items-center justify-center flex-shrink-0`
- Size: `w-6 h-6 text-xs font-medium text-on-primary`
- Background: deterministic color based on user — use a consistent palette (teal `bg-palette-teal`, amber `bg-warning-bg`, violet `bg-palette-violet`, rose `bg-palette-rose`, etc.)
- Content: 2-letter uppercase initials only
- **Position on cards: `absolute bottom-1.5 right-1.5 z-10`, not a flex child of the metadata row (#1411).** `CardItem`'s metadata row is `overflow-hidden` with no wrap at rest; an avatar rendered as the row's last flex child (`ml-auto`) is the first thing silently clipped when the row overflows — backwards, since the avatar is the one metadata element with no density gate. The content wrapper carries `relative`, and the metadata row carries `min-h-5 pr-7` whenever `card.assignee` is set: `pr-7` keeps clipped chip content from running under the avatar's corner, and `min-h-5` (20px, the `xs` Avatar's height) gives the row the vertical footprint the avatar needs. Both are required — the reservation is the row's height plus its right padding together. **This reservation must hold even when the assignee is the only metadata on the card** — `card.assignee` stays in `hasMetadataRow`'s OR-chain for exactly this reason: an otherwise-empty row still has to render, and because an empty flex row has zero height, `pr-7` alone reserves nothing vertically (the avatar would overlap the last title line by ~12px); the `min-h-5` is what pushes the card's bottom edge below the avatar, or the absolutely-positioned avatar sits on top of the title with nothing stopping it. Dropping that clause because "the avatar isn't a row child anymore" was a real regression caught by ux-review (#1411) — don't reintroduce it by a different path.
- Never show more than the initials — no full name, no tooltip required (but allowed)
- **Always use the `Avatar` component** (`src/components/Common/Avatar.tsx`) — never hand-roll avatar circles with inline palette arrays or hardcoded background colors. The `Avatar` component owns the canonical `-600` tone palette and handles initials, image avatars, and deterministic color assignment.
- **Sizes:** `xs` (20px), `sm` (24px, default chip on cards), `trigger` (28px, reserved for nav/chrome menu triggers — sits between chip and user-header sizes), `md` (32px), `lg` (40px)

## User menu (top-chrome)

The avatar-triggered user menu in `Navbar.tsx` is the single entry point for all account-scoped actions (`Profile & preferences`, `Keyboard shortcuts`, `Help & docs`, `Sign out`). Rules:

- **Trigger:** `Avatar` at `size="trigger"` (28px) + a small inline `▾` chevron. `aria-haspopup="menu"`, `aria-expanded={open}`, `aria-label={`Account menu for ${displayName}`}`. Never render a bare text `Sign out` button in the navbar — Sign out lives only inside the menu, is always the last item, and uses the danger treatment (`text-danger hover:bg-danger-bg/20`).
- **Panel:** `w-56 bg-surface border border-line-strong rounded-lg shadow-xl py-1 z-50`, `role="menu"`, positioned `absolute right-0 top-full mt-1` relative to the trigger.
- **Header row:** non-interactive `role="none"` block — display name in `text-sm font-medium text-fg`, email in `text-xs text-fg-muted`, both `truncate` with `title`. Omit the email line entirely when missing; never render a placeholder.
- **Items:** each a `<button role="menuitem">` (or `<a role="menuitem">` for external links) with the shared dropdown item classes plus a fixed `w-4 text-center flex-shrink-0` icon slot so labels align across rows. `tabIndex={-1}` on all items; roving focus via arrow keys, Home/End.
- **Separators:** standard engraved double-`<div>` pattern, `mx-4 my-1`. Two total: after header, before Sign out.
- **Dismissal:** Esc (via `useEscapeStack` at priority 25), outside-click, Tab out. On Esc close, refocus the trigger.
- **Theme entry is prohibited until a theme system ships** — do not add a dead link for theme switching.

## Connection status indicator

`ConnectionStatus` (`src/components/Common/ConnectionStatus.tsx`) is the single canonical component for surfacing WebSocket state. Do not re-introduce a second `LiveIndicator`, and do not render a bare `●ᅠLive` in feature components.

- **Prominence rule — quiet when healthy, loud when degraded.** Connected state is a bare success dot with the word "Live" shown only at `lg+` viewports (`labelClass: "hidden lg:inline"`). Every other state — `connecting`, `reconnecting`, `stale`, `failed` — always shows its label with an amber or red pill background so Maya/Jordan can see degraded state at a glance.
- **Pulse and reduced motion.** The healthy dot stays static per the prominence rule (a pulse was proposed in #199 and declined in favor of #851). Degraded-state dot pulses follow the general reduced-motion rule in § Motion and reduced motion.
- **Five states:**
  - `connected` → `text-success` dot, no background
  - `connecting` / `reconnecting` / `stale` → `text-warning bg-warning/10 border border-warning/30 rounded px-2 py-0.5` (the connecting and reconnecting dots pulse with `animate-pulse motion-reduce:animate-none`; stale is static)
  - `failed` → `text-danger bg-danger-bg/30 border border-danger-emphasis/40 rounded px-2 py-0.5`
- **Stale detection:** `useIsStale(status, lastEventAt, thresholdMs = 60_000)` flips `connected` into the `stale` variant when no event has arrived within 60 seconds. Do not poll; the hook schedules a single `setTimeout`.
- **Popover:** click trigger opens a `role="dialog" aria-label="Connection status"` popover (`w-64 bg-surface border border-line-strong rounded-lg shadow-xl p-3`). Dismissal via Esc (`useEscapeStack` priority 25), outside-click, or re-clicking the trigger. On Esc close, refocus the trigger.
- **Popover actions:**
  - connected / stale → "Refresh board" secondary button (only when `onRefresh` is passed — board pages yes, group page no)
  - failed → "Reload page" danger button
  - connecting / reconnecting → informational only; no button
- **Accessibility:** trigger carries `aria-haspopup="dialog"`, `aria-expanded`, and a state-specific `aria-label` ("Real-time updates active" / "Reconnecting to real-time updates" / …). Include an inner `<span role="status" aria-live="polite" aria-atomic="true" className="sr-only">` that announces degraded states to screen readers; stay silent when connected.

## Swimlane label panel

- Background: `bg-surface` — visually distinct from the `bg-canvas` grid cells
- Color stripe: **`border-l-4`** in the swimlane's assigned color — thick enough to read at a glance; no thin stripes
- When a swimlane has a color, that same color bleeds as the left border of the entire swimlane row across all columns (applied to the label panel, not individual cells)
- Drag handle (`⋮⋮`): `text-fg-faint hover:text-fg-tertiary cursor-grab` — show on row hover via `group-hover:opacity-100`, default `opacity-0`
- Swimlane name: `text-sm text-fg-secondary`
- Collapse/expand chevron: prominent, `text-fg-tertiary hover:text-fg transition` — must clearly communicate interactivity
- Edit (✎) button: `group-hover:opacity-100 opacity-0 transition` — visible at full opacity on hover, not discoverable by accident
- **Pinned custom-field chips stack below the name, and do not survive collapse (#1140).** Up to eight values can be pinned (the cap, a data contract), and the number of chips visible on the row follows card density (#1417): Comfortable 3, Standard 5, Dense 8, an undefined density is treated as Standard (`visibleRowChipCount`). They render inside the label panel in a `flex flex-col items-start gap-1 mt-1 min-w-0` container directly beneath the name/`contact_email` block — never as a full-width band across the cells, which the row's `flex` label-panel-then-cells structure has nowhere to put. A trailing `+N` chip in the same container counts the pinned values folded by the density cap plus the unpinned ones, and opens `SwimlaneFieldsPopover` with the row's full visible field list; render it only when `N > 0`, and label it `+N more` when no pinned chip precedes it, so the affordance is readable standing alone. A value of `""` renders nothing at all — never a dashed ghost chip; dashed already means "unset ghost" on the card face and must not acquire a second meaning here. The whole cluster is gated on `!collapsed`, matching `contact_email` on the line above: a collapsed row is `py-1` and exists to give vertical space back, so stacked chips would undo the gesture. The tradeoff — row metadata is least visible exactly when a user is scanning many rows — is accepted because the `+N` trigger disappears with the chips, so nothing is silently truncated, only folded behind the chevron the user just pressed.
- **`SwimlaneFieldsPopover` leads with what `+N` counted (#1455).** The `+N` chip counts the fields that are *not* on the row, so the popover lists those first (pinned values folded by the density cap in pin order, then unpinned ones, with no sub-heading between them) and only the chips actually on the row after an `On row` sub-heading, styled exactly like the `Fields` heading plus `border-t border-line`. A folded pinned field is not on the row, so it never sits under `On row`. The `+N` count and the popover's leading block are the same set: a change to what `+N` counts must change the popover's leading group in the same commit. Omit the sub-heading when either group is empty. Viewer-facing surfaces say "On row"; only admin settings say "Pin" or "Pinned" (`BoardSettingsSwimlaneFieldsTab`), because members never see the pin control.

## Board Settings tab bar (#1140)

- **Field-list type labels never wrap (#1391).** The type label cell in Board Settings → Card fields and Swimlane fields is `w-20 whitespace-nowrap shrink-0`. `w-16` truncated "Multi-select" onto two lines and pushed the row taller than its siblings; size the cell for the longest type label, and widen it again (in both tabs) rather than abbreviating a new one.

- **Label tabs by scope as soon as two of them configure the same kind of thing.** `Fields` became `Card fields` when `Swimlane fields` landed. Two tabs named for *what* they configure and not for *what it attaches to* is a coin flip on first encounter, and the cost of the wrong guess is an admin defining a field on the wrong object and only finding out when it fails to appear. Whenever a new tab configures a sibling of an existing tab's subject, rename **both** to carry the scope noun. Never disambiguate with an icon, a tooltip, or a help line under an otherwise identical label. Keep the pair adjacent in the tab array — a renamed pair split across the strip does none of the work the rename was for.
- **Use the product's own noun.** "Swimlane fields", not "Row fields": `EditSwimlaneModal`, `AddSwimlaneModal`, the collapse `aria-label`, and the Display tab all say *swimlane*, and a label is the wrong place to introduce a synonym.
- **The tab strip scrolls; it never wraps — and the panel is sized so it rarely has to.** The strip is `flex border-b border-line px-6 gap-1 overflow-x-auto` with `shrink-0 whitespace-nowrap` on every tab button, which is what actually prevents a second row of tabs appearing under the border; that guarantee holds at any panel width. The panel's `max-w-2xl` is a separate, weaker call: at `max-w-lg` the seven current labels overflow and the strip scrolls on first open, which reads as a broken tab bar rather than an affordance, and `Members (N)` grows with the member count. So the width buys breathing room, not correctness — do not justify it as a wrapping fix, and if a future change makes the label set short again, narrowing it back is safe.
- **A board-wide boolean display setting is an admin `Toggle` row with a read-only state line for everyone else (#1418).** Use `flex items-center justify-between py-2 border-b border-line/60` with a `text-sm text-fg` label, a `text-xs text-fg-muted` helper tied to the switch via `aria-describedby`, and a `<label htmlFor>`. Non-admins, or a caller with no write callback, get `text-sm text-fg-secondary` "{Setting}: shown/hidden". Never a native checkbox for a board-level setting.

## Withheld row actions and refusal reasons (#1785)

When an action is withheld from a row for a policy reason (group owner, last admin), keep the reason visible on the row (for the owner, an `Owner` pill in the inherited-badge style `text-xs bg-surface-hover text-fg-tertiary px-1.5 py-0.5 rounded`) rather than silently omitting the control. When the server refuses an action with a 400, show its `detail` string in the nearest `role="alert"` `text-danger text-xs` line; use a generic fallback only when `detail` is absent or not a string.

## Radio groups

Never render browser-default radio circles. Use `sr-only` native `<input type="radio">` inside a `label` container — this preserves native keyboard navigation (arrow keys, Tab, Space) while hiding the visual control.

Represent selection state on the container:
- Selected: `border-primary-emphasis bg-primary-emphasis/10` — **never `bg-info/10`**; the `primary-emphasis` token tracks the active theme, `info` does not, so an `info`-tinted fill reads off-brand in dark mode
- Unselected: `border-line-strong hover:bg-surface-hover/40`
- Keyboard focus: `focus-within:ring-2 focus-within:ring-primary-emphasis rounded-lg` on the `label`
- Transition: `transition-colors duration-150`

Option text: `text-sm text-fg font-medium` for the label, `text-xs text-fg-muted mt-0.5` for the description line below it.

**Compact / chip-style radio groups** — a 2-4 option inline segmented choice (e.g. the relation-type picker in `RelationCardPicker`) may use `rounded` instead of `rounded-lg` on the option container, matching its smaller `px-2 py-1 text-xs` chip sizing. The `rounded-lg` guidance above assumes the larger card-with-description-line layout (e.g. the export format choice). Both variants keep the same selected/unselected token pair and the same `focus-within:ring-2 focus-within:ring-primary-emphasis` treatment — only the corner radius and text sizing shrink. A chip row also takes `flex-wrap`: three short chips fit at 320px today, but a longer translation or a fourth option would clip silently without it, and there is no action button to push the overflow onto.

**Popover radio cards (#1133).** A radio group inside an anchored popover (the health status popover, `HealthStatusPopover`) keeps the card variant above (`rounded-lg border px-3 py-2`, the same selected / unselected / `focus-within` tokens) with a leading `aria-hidden` shape in its on-tint color, and one deliberate deviation: the description line is `text-xs text-fg-tertiary`, not `text-fg-muted`, because `fg-muted` is sub-AA on `bg-surface` (#1862). Arrow keys move only the draft (native radio behavior); Enter on a radio and the primary **Save** submit; an unchanged draft closes with no request. Save stays in the tab order while unchanged or saving (`aria-disabled`, `opacity-40`), and activating it with an unchanged draft closes the popover with no request; the always-mounted error slot is a polite live region.

**Focus on closing a non-modal anchored popover (#1133).** Return focus to the trigger on Cancel, Escape and a successful save. On an outside mousedown or focus leaving the panel, close without moving focus: the user has already moved. On window resize and outside scroll, return focus to the trigger only if it is still inside the panel. No close path may drop focus to `<body>`. An admin trigger that opens such a popover shows `cursor-pointer` and a `hover:ring-1 hover:ring-line-strong` ring (never a stronger fill behind on-tint text); a demo-mode trigger that never opens drops `aria-haspopup` / `aria-expanded`.

**Inline add/edit rows (#2036).** Every exit of an inline input (Cancel button, Escape, successful commit) returns focus to the control that opened it; set a `focusTriggerRef` flag and focus in an effect keyed on the editing state, and test all three exits. A blur-commit (clicking elsewhere) must not set the flag, or it steals focus back from wherever the user went (`ColumnHeader`, `SwimlaneRow`, `InlineBoardName`, `BoardCell`, #2087; the `GroupDetail` group rename and description, #2170). When fixing focus rings or focus return on one control, audit its siblings in the same row (pills, tabs, ✕ buttons) in the same commit. The card description `RichTextEditor` follows the same rule but cannot return focus to its trigger (the pencil has `onFocus={enterEdit}` and would reopen the editor), so Escape, Cancel and Save call `focusEnclosingDialog` before unmounting (#2182), and its blur-save is suppressed for that exit. Save and Cancel act on `mousedown` (to beat the editor blur) and on keyboard `click` only when `event.detail === 0`, so a mouse click never fires twice.

**`SingleSelectDropdown` marks its selected option without color (#2108).** The selected option gets an `aria-hidden` check icon (`singleselect-selected-mark`) in a reserved `w-3` slot, plus `aria-current="true"` on the default `menuitem` role or `aria-checked` with `optionRole="menuitemradio"`. `text-info` alone fails WCAG 1.4.1. A new select-like menu must carry a non-color cue too. **Every other select-like surface follows the same rule (#2171, WCAG 1.4.1):** `SelectDropdown` puts the same check in a reserved `w-3` slot (`select-selected-mark`, with `aria-selected`); the `MoveBoardModal` destination rows are `role="radio"` `aria-checked` inside a `role="radiogroup"` with the check in a reserved slot (`move-board-selected-mark`); the `CreateBoardModal` template cards, native radios, show a check (`template-selected-mark`) beside the name. A tint, border or text color is never the only cue. The selection slot reserves its width so selecting never shifts text. The `MoveBoardModal` radio rows follow the WAI-ARIA radio group pattern (#2184): the checked row is the only radio Tab stop (the first enabled row when it is hidden in a collapsed subtree), while each expand/collapse toggle stays its own Tab stop so the tree remains reachable. Arrow keys and Home/End move focus through every row with wrap-around, and move the selection with it except on a blocked row: a blocked row (`aria-disabled`) stays focusable per § Blocked rows in pickers, so arrows land on it to show why it is unavailable, but selection stays on the last enabled row and Enter/Space do nothing. Prefer a native `input type=radio` where the layout allows, as `CreateBoardModal` does.

**Segmented, chip and toggle controls mark the active state with a check, not fill alone (#2185, WCAG 1.4.1).** A button group whose active member differs only by fill, tint, border or text color fails the same rule as a select. Use `components/Common/ActiveMark` (the #2171 check, `aria-hidden`, `data-testid="active-mark"`) as the first child of every button in the group, inside an `inline-flex items-center gap-1` button; the slot is a fixed `w-3` on every button, so toggling never shifts the label. Keep the semantics honest: `aria-pressed` for toggle buttons (the analytics mode/period toggles, the Chart | Table switch, the activity drawer kind and window filters, `SavedFilterTabs`, the group allowed-priorities chips); a table row selected by click gets the mark in its first cell plus `aria-current` (`MovementHistoryView`); only a real ARIA tabs widget (roving tabindex, arrow keys) uses `role="tab"` and `aria-selected`. Every inactive button carries the empty slot (about 16px with the gap), the accepted cost of no layout shift; never swap an icon in and out. Tests assert the mark and the ARIA state move together. A hand-rolled Save/Cancel pair gives both buttons the identical `focus:ring-2 focus:ring-primary-emphasis` set, because the keyboard click path (`detail === 0`) is now a real path (#2182). Icon-only toggles take the underline cue in the next paragraph instead (#2188). The FilterBar "My cards" and "Blocked only" chips carry the mark.

**Binary connector toggle (And / Or, #2205).** A join between two rule rows is a two-button segmented group on its own line, left-aligned with the field column: `<div role="group" aria-label="Join filter {i} and filter {i+1}">` (`group {k}` for a group), `inline-flex rounded overflow-hidden border border-line-strong`; buttons with visible text **And** / **Or** (the accessible name is the text, so label-in-name passes), `aria-pressed`, `min-h-6 px-2 text-xs`, the `ActiveMark` check above, and `font-semibold` on the pressed one as a second non-color cue. Selected `bg-primary-emphasis/20 text-info`, unselected `text-fg-tertiary hover:text-fg-secondary hover:bg-surface-hover`. No chevron, no menu; both buttons are tab stops. Reference: `JoinToggle` in `LaneFilterPopover.tsx`.

**An item that leaves a filtered set after a data edit hides immediately (#2205).** Do not keep it visible until the next filter change: the count and any "Reads as" text would disagree with the screen. When it concerns the user (this client made the edit, keyboard focus was inside it, or its card is open in the panel; an item this client just created always), show an in-place notice between the filter row and the board (`flex items-center gap-2 px-3 py-1 text-xs text-fg-secondary bg-surface border-b border-line`) whose text goes in the always-mounted polite region, with a secondary action and an icon-only `aria-label="Dismiss notice"` ×. No timeout; it clears on any filter change, focus-mode change, or dismiss. Anything else (a teammate's edit elsewhere) hides silently and only the count changes. If keyboard focus was inside the item, a `useLayoutEffect` sends it to `BOARD_SCROLL_SELECTOR` before paint, never to the notice. Reference: `useLaneFilterNotice`.

**Icon-only toggles mark the active state with an underline bar, not tint alone (#2188, WCAG 1.4.1).** An inline check has no label to sit beside on an icon button, so a standalone `aria-pressed` toggle renders `components/Common/ActiveUnderline` as its last child: a 12px by 2px `bg-fg` bar centered under the icon (`aria-hidden`, `data-testid="active-underline"`), rendered only while active and absolutely positioned, so the host button adds `relative` and toggling never shifts layout. `bg-fg` reads on both themes whatever tint the active state also carries; keep the tint as reinforcement and keep `aria-pressed` as the state. `placement="inset"` (default, `bottom-px`) sits in the bottom padding of a padded button, clear of the line box (`p-1.5`, `px-2 py-1`); `placement="below"` hangs 4px under a bare glyph button with no padding (the swimlane focus and collapse toggles), so the bar never covers the icon. Apply it to every such toggle, including ones whose glyph also flips (collapse chevrons, the sidebar toggle), so the rule has no exceptions to judge. Sites: the BoardView and `LensToolbar` Row 2 toggles (Filters, card layout, Archived, activity drawer, keyboard shortcuts), the `SwimlaneRow` and `LensSwimlaneRow` focus and collapse toggles, the `RichTextEditor` toolbar marks and the `AppSidebar` collapse toggle. Tests assert the bar and `aria-pressed` move together. **Color swatches are the one exception and take the ring, not the bar:** a swatch is itself a color, so its selected state is the themed `ring-fg` ring in § Color swatch pickers (`ColorSwatchPicker` and the `RichTextEditor` text-color panel), with `aria-pressed` and a color-name label. A labeled button group still uses `ActiveMark` above; never put both cues on one control. A menu trigger that displays a set value (the `RichTextEditor` "Text color" button) is not a toggle, so it takes no `aria-pressed` but renders the same `ActiveUnderline` while a value is set.

Group the options in a `<fieldset>` with a `<legend>` (`sr-only` when there is no visible heading for it) — never put `role="radiogroup"` on a `<fieldset>`, which already carries group semantics.

The action button following a radio group uses the primary variant (`bg-button-primary hover:bg-button-primary-hover text-on-primary`) and its label should reflect the current selection (e.g. "Export JSON" / "Export CSV") to eliminate ambiguity.

## Subordinate settings blocks (toggle/checkbox-gated)

When a block of settings is only relevant while a toggle or checkbox directly above it is on (e.g. the Rules tab's Hard-WIP-mode options gated by "Enforce WIP limits", or the Display tab's personal density radio group gated by "Use my own density"), indent and rule off that block with exactly:

```
ml-6 border-l-2 border-line pl-4
```

**Do not invent a per-instance variant** — no custom border color (`border-line-strong`), no opacity modifier (`border-line-strong/40`), no different indent/padding pairing. This is the one sanctioned treatment for "a settings block that visually belongs to the control above it," established by the Rules tab's Hard-WIP-mode block (`BoardSettingsModal.tsx`) and reused as-is by the Display tab's personal density override block. `RoleTooltip`'s `pl-3 border-l-2 border-line` is a pre-existing, unrelated exception (tooltip content formatting, not a gated settings block) — do not treat it as a third variant to reconcile against.

**Not every control-plus-dependent-fields layout is toggle-gated.** The rule above applies when the dependent block is *inert* while the controlling toggle is off — there is nothing useful to do with it. It does **not** apply when the dependent fields stay independently meaningful whichever option is selected, because the safe path to switching requires filling them in *before* the switch. Admin Settings → Email is the reference case (`EmailSettingsSection.tsx`): the SMTP host/port/credential fields stay unindented and fully editable whether "Environment variables" or "Database" is selected, because indenting or disabling them under an unselected radio would force an admin to switch mail configuration blind. When leaving a block unindented for this reason, add an inline status line saying whether it is currently in effect (e.g. "Saved, but not in use — {other option} is selected above"), sourced from the **last-saved server state, never the in-progress draft** — do not assert a saved fact about a selection the user has not committed.

**Render-and-disable instead of conditional render is permitted only for a single dependent toggle whose existence is itself the thing being advertised (#356).** The default stays "conditionally render an inert block" (Hard-WIP options, personal density radio). But when the dependent control is *one* switch, the controlling toggle defaults to off for most users, and hiding the switch would make the capability undiscoverable — Sam checks in twice a week and would never learn per-event email notification exists — render it always inside the standard `ml-6 border-l-2 border-line pl-4` block and set `disabled`. Four requirements, all of them load-bearing:

1. The block carries a **visible, always-rendered reason line** whose `id` is passed to the switch via `aria-describedby` (`Toggle` supports that prop), and whose text changes with state — including a distinct state for a stored-ON value under an off parent. A helper that reads as a claim ("Sends a copy…") must not appear under an off switch.
2. The stored value is **never auto-cleared**. Clearing it needs a second write that can half-fail, and it discards a choice the user will want back the moment they re-enable the parent. The disabled switch stays visibly ON and the reason line says it is paused and kept.
3. Dim the label to `text-fg-muted` only. No new opacity modifier — the switch's own `disabled:opacity-40` is the sole dimming.
4. Never use this for a **multi-control** block. Those stay conditionally rendered.
5. **Use `ariaDisabled`, not `disabled`, on the switch whenever the reason line is the "why is this off" case rather than a transient in-flight save (#1159).** Native `disabled` removes the control from the tab order, so a sighted keyboard user never reaches the reason text a screen-reader browse-mode user sees. `Toggle` supports `ariaDisabled` as an opt-in: the switch stays focusable, click and keyboard (Space/Enter) activation are both ignored, and the same disabled visual styling is kept. Keep plain `disabled` for genuinely transient states (e.g. a save in flight) that have no reason line to reach.

Reference implementation: `NotificationRow` in `SettingsPage.tsx`.

**The same `aria-disabled` + `aria-describedby` principle extends to a plain `<button>` whose action is blocked for a reason (#1314).** The rule above is written for `Toggle`, but any standalone action button that can be legitimately blocked (e.g. "Disconnect" on a connected-accounts row when it is the user's last remaining sign-in method) follows the same shape, by hand: `aria-disabled={condition}` (never native `disabled`, for the same tab-order reason), `aria-describedby` pointing at a reason `<p>` rendered **only while the condition holds** (the hint and the block share one gate), and `onClick` set to `undefined` while blocked rather than guarded inside the handler. Keep the control's own semantic focus-ring color (e.g. `focus:ring-danger-emphasis` for a destructive action) in both states. Reference implementation: `ConnectedAccountRow` in `SettingsPage.tsx`.

**A row containing two controls is never wrapped in a single `<label>` (#356).** A click on label text forwards to the first labelable descendant, so text belonging to the second control would toggle the first. Give each control its own `<label htmlFor>` around only its own text block, wrap the pair in `role="group" aria-label="{event}"` so each control has context, and give the repeated visible label a unique accessible name that *begins with* that visible text (`aria-label="Also send by email: {event}"`) so label-in-name holds and `getByRole('switch', { name })` stays unambiguous.

## Native checkbox and radio accent

When a native `<input type="checkbox">` or `<input type="radio">` is rendered with its default browser control visible (i.e. *not* the `sr-only` custom-styled radio pattern above), it **must** set `accent-primary` — never a raw palette value like `accent-blue-500` / `accent-blue-600`. The `accent-primary` token tracks the active theme (including dark mode); a hardcoded `accent-blue-*` stays the same hue regardless of theme and reads as an off-brand control. This applies to filter checkboxes (`CheckboxDropdown`), column-option checkboxes (`EditColumnModal`), and any admin toggle that keeps the native control.

## Ancestor breadcrumbs

Use this pattern whenever showing a full ancestor chain (e.g. group hierarchies):

```tsx
<nav aria-label="Group breadcrumb" className="flex flex-wrap items-center mb-1">
  {ancestors.map((ancestor, i) => (
    <span key={ancestor.id} className="flex items-center">
      {i > 0 && <span className="text-fg-faint mx-1.5 select-none" aria-hidden="true">/</span>}
      <a
        href={`/groups/${ancestor.id}`}
        onClick={(e) => { e.preventDefault(); navigate(`/groups/${ancestor.id}`); }}
        className="text-sm text-fg-tertiary hover:text-fg focus:outline-none focus:ring-2 focus:ring-primary-emphasis rounded transition max-w-[12rem] truncate"
        title={ancestor.name}
      >
        {ancestor.name}
      </a>
    </span>
  ))}
  <span className="text-fg-faint mx-1.5 select-none" aria-hidden="true">/</span>
  <span className="text-sm text-fg-secondary max-w-[12rem] truncate" title={current.name}>{current.name}</span>
</nav>
```

- Ancestor links: `text-sm text-fg-tertiary hover:text-fg focus:outline-none focus:ring-2 focus:ring-primary-emphasis rounded transition`
- Separator `/`: `text-fg-faint mx-1.5 select-none` with `aria-hidden="true"`
- Current item (non-linked): `text-sm text-fg-secondary`
- Per-item max-width: `max-w-[12rem] truncate` with `title` attribute for full name
- Container: `flex flex-wrap items-center` — wraps on narrow viewports
- Root-level items with no ancestors render nothing (omit the `<nav>` entirely)

**Navbar breadcrumb override:** the top-chrome breadcrumb in `Navbar.tsx` uses the same pattern but renders the terminal (current) segment as `text-sm text-fg font-medium max-w-[18rem]` instead of `text-fg-secondary max-w-[12rem]`. The board/page name is the primary context indicator in the chrome, so it reads brighter and has more room to breathe. Standalone ancestor breadcrumbs elsewhere keep `text-fg-secondary max-w-[12rem]`.

## Inline description fields (non-RTE)

For plain-text description fields that are inline-editable by admins:

- **Idle / view state**: a `relative` wrapper holds a `<button type="button">` (`block w-[calc(100%+1rem)] text-left border border-transparent hover:border-line-emphasis cursor-text rounded px-2 py-1.5 -mx-2 transition-colors`, standard focus ring) whose children are `<span className="block ...">` text (no `<p>` inside a button). This button is the single keyboard tab stop. The hover-reveal pencil is an overlay sibling (`absolute top-1 right-1`, `tabIndex={-1}`, `focus:opacity-100 focus:ring-2 focus:ring-primary-emphasis`) so it adds no height (reference: `GroupDetail`)
- **Edit state**: `bg-sunken border border-primary-soft rounded px-2 py-1.5 focus:outline-none focus:ring-2 focus:ring-primary-emphasis focus:border-transparent` on the `<textarea>`; `resize-none`; Escape cancels, blur saves
- **Error slot**: always render `<p className="text-xs h-4">` below the field unconditionally; place error text in a `<span className="text-danger">` inside it — never conditionally render the container itself
- **Non-admin, non-empty**: render plain `<p className="text-sm text-fg-tertiary whitespace-pre-wrap">`
- **Non-admin, empty**: render nothing (`null`) — do not show a placeholder the user cannot act on

## Composite inline editors — explicit Save/Cancel (#371)

The pattern above (and the single-value autosave pattern used everywhere else — Weight, Due date, priority, labels) commits on blur or on selection with no separate save step. That pattern only holds for a **single interdependent value**. It breaks down once an editor has **two or more sub-fields that depend on each other** — e.g. Board Settings → Fields' add/edit panel, where the `field_type` selector changes which other sub-fields are relevant (dropdown needs a `choices` list; other types don't) and requires cross-field validation ("a dropdown field needs at least one choice") before any of it is safe to persist.

**Rule:** an inline editor with ≥2 interdependent sub-fields, or any cross-field validation that must pass before a partial state is safe to write, uses explicit `Cancel` / `Save {noun}` text buttons (secondary + primary variant, standard focus rings, `gap-3`) instead of blur/immediate-commit. A single-value inline editor (one text field, one toggle, one dropdown with no dependent fields) stays on blur/immediate-commit — do not add a Save button to a field that doesn't need one; that adds friction without a matching risk.
