Reference implementation: `BoardSettingsFieldsTab`'s `FieldEditPanel` (name + type + choices + help text + pin toggle, all validated together in `handleSave`).

**Exception: filter builders apply complete rules live with a Done close (#2205).** The Save/Cancel rule covers persisted data. A filter is reversible view state, `FilterBar` already applies live, and an incomplete rule never filters, so a multi-field rule builder such as `LaneFilterPopover` applies each complete rule as it is built and closes with a primary **Done** (and Escape / outside click) instead of Save/Cancel. A saved filter is still saved explicitly through the Saved dropdown.

## Tooltips

- Consistent delay: 300 ms show, immediate hide
- Placement: prefer `top` for icon buttons, `bottom` for nav items
- Style: `bg-sunken text-fg text-xs rounded px-2 py-1 shadow-lg`

## Security notices

- **Security notices ignore email opt-in (#863, a 1.3 UI spec Part 8 departure).** A webhook disable notice (`webhook_disabled`) is emailed even when the recipient turned notification email off, and its footer says so. It carries exactly three facts: board name, subscription id and reason code (with the plain label derived from the code), never the URL, the host or anything the receiver sent back. In-app it reads as one notification whose hint is "Open board settings →", linking to `/boards/{id}?settings=webhooks` with no subscription id; BoardView opens the Webhooks tab for an admin and strips the parameter.

## Inline status messages

- **Address refusals share one message (#863, Part 8 departure 26).** Any outbound-target refusal that depends on where an address points (a private range, loopback, cloud metadata, or a name that resolves to one) uses one neutral message that names the operator: "Visiban can't send to this address. If it should be allowed, ask your Visiban operator." (`ADDRESS_REFUSED_COPY`). Separate copy would let a user map the internal network. Only input-shape errors (scheme, format, embedded credentials) get their own copy, and the client checks only those; the test event's "not sent" result reuses the same message.

- **Reserve vertical space unconditionally** — render the container element always (`<p className="text-xs h-4">`) and conditionally render the text content inside it. Never conditionally render the container itself; doing so causes buttons and surrounding elements to shift when messages appear or disappear.
- Error text: `text-danger`; success text: `text-success` — always on a `<span>` inside the reserved container, not directly on the `<p>`
- **Relabel destructive-escape actions after success** — if a modal stays open after a successful action, relabel "Cancel" to "Close" once success state is set, so the button's semantics match the user's situation
- **`h-4` reserves exactly one line — size the reservation to the copy.** A slot whose longest state wraps to two lines uses `h-8`; a slot holding a full sentence whose wrapping depends on viewport width uses `min-h-4`, which still reserves space unconditionally but cannot clip. A fixed `h-4` under a wrapping sentence overflows into the block below, and the states most likely to carry long copy are first-run states, so the bug ships visible on day one (#306).
- **On form fields, the reserved slot doubles as the helper slot.** Render helper text there by default and swap it for the error when the field is invalid. This satisfies the reservation rule while spending the space productively instead of leaving a dead gap under every input, and it keeps `aria-describedby` pointing at one stable id.
- **Pending server-side effects follow the same rules.** A note describing a deferred/pending server-side effect (e.g. "confirmation email sent") is the affected field's reserved helper slot: render its container unconditionally, with a stable id that the field's `aria-describedby` always points at, and vary only the text. The note itself is not a live region. If the page doesn't navigate away after the triggering save, the save-result line is the terminal state, so make it `role="status" aria-live="polite" aria-atomic="true"`. When part of the change hasn't taken effect, put state-specific copy in that live region instead of a generic "Changes saved." (e.g. "Profile updated. Check your inbox to confirm your new email address."). The two must never coexist (#1273, ProfileTab's pending email change).
- **Actions on a pending effect sit under its note, outside the `<label>`.** Follow-up actions on a pending server-side effect (e.g. **Resend link** / **Cancel change** under ProfileTab's pending email note) are bare-text secondary buttons (`text-xs`, `px-1.5 py-0.5`, `gap-3`, standard focus ring, `type="button"`) rendered only while the effect is pending, placed after the note and outside the field's `<label>` so they never join the input's accessible name; each carries `aria-describedby` pointing at the note so it is announced with what it acts on. Their results go to the same save-result live region as the form's own save (success / `text-warning` for a 429 cooldown whose copy comes from the server / `text-danger`), and a new action or save replaces the previous result — never two at once. When an action ends the pending state and the buttons unmount, move focus to the field they belonged to rather than letting it drop to `<body>`. A personal, reversible withdrawal like Cancel change commits immediately without an inline confirm (#1293).
- **Re-authentication field for a sensitive profile change (#1641).** Show a Current password field only while the sensitive edit is pending (for example, email differs from the saved value) and only for accounts with a usable password. Otherwise omit it from the DOM, never disabled. It uses `type=password`, `autoComplete="current-password"` and `required`, and keeps its state out of the main form object. A helper line states why it is needed. The value clears when the triggering edit is reverted, on success, and on a server `current_password` error. That error renders in the field's helper slot (`text-danger`) with `aria-invalid` and focus moved to the field, not only in the page-level status line.

## Paired numeric settings fields

When two related numeric inputs belong to the same conceptual setting (e.g. threshold + warning percentage):

- Group them in a single `<section>` with a shared `<h3>` heading
- Render each as its own `flex flex-col gap-1.5` block (input row + helper text), stacked in a `flex flex-col gap-3` container
- Each input row: `flex items-center gap-2` with a `w-20` number input and a `text-sm text-fg-tertiary` unit label
- Helper text: `text-xs text-fg-muted` — explain the relationship between the two values with a concrete example
- Non-admin read-only view: single `text-sm text-fg-secondary` line combining both values (e.g. "14 days · 50% warning")
- `onBlur` saves each field independently via `patchBoard`; clamp values client-side before patching

## Loading and spinner states

- **Canonical animated spinner:** `w-5 h-5 border-2 border-primary border-t-transparent rounded-full animate-spin` (inline context). Use `w-8 h-8` for full-page center spinners. Always wrap in `flex items-center justify-center gap-2` with a `text-sm text-fg-tertiary` label when the wait context is not obvious.
- **In-button variant: `w-3 h-3`.** A spinner rendered *inside* a `px-3 py-1.5 text-sm` button uses `w-3 h-3` with the same border treatment, plus `shrink-0` and `aria-hidden="true"` (the button's own label text carries the state, e.g. "Sending…"). The canonical `w-5 h-5` overflows a `py-1.5` button. Established by `AutosaveIndicator`, reused by `EmailSettingsSection`.
- Center spinners with `flex items-center justify-center`

## Empty states

- **Full-page not-found pages for signed-in users** (unmatched route, #1740; deleted or inaccessible board/group, #2041 — `UnavailablePage`, one copy for 404 and 403 alike, "This {board|group} isn't available", title "Not available – Visiban", and never say which cause it was; the expired-share page and JoinPage's countdown redirect are outside the app shell and do not follow this): render inside the normal app shell (`Navbar` + `bg-sunken` body) with an `<h1 class="text-fg text-2xl font-bold">`, one `text-sm text-fg-tertiary` sentence saying what happened, and exactly one primary-button `<Link>` back to a safe page (`/`). Set `document.title` to "<Reason> – Visiban" and restore it on unmount. On mount move focus to the `<h1>` (`tabIndex={-1}` + `focus:outline-none`) so assistive tech announces the change. A silent redirect is never an acceptable response to an unmatched route.
- Consistent pattern: centered icon (muted, `text-fg-faint`) + heading (`text-fg-tertiary`) + optional CTA button
- No one-off inline empty messages with different styling
- **Card-detail sub-sections are the exception — use the one-line form, not the centered icon.** The centered-icon pattern is page/panel-level; inside the 540px card detail panel it visually out-weighs its own section header. Every sub-section there uses:
  - empty — `No {noun} yet.` · `text-xs text-fg-faint italic`
  - loading — `Loading {noun}…` · `text-sm text-fg-tertiary`, **no spinner** (the panel already fires several fetches without one, and a spinner inside a 20px sub-section is more motion than signal). **Exception: the sheet's first load (#1914, design frame 6).** While the comments, checklist and attachments fetches of a freshly opened card are pending, those three sections show `SectionSkeleton` rows (`bg-surface-hover`, `animate-pulse motion-reduce:animate-none`, `aria-hidden`) sized like their rows, the body is `aria-busy`, an always-mounted `sr-only` `role="status"` line says "Loading card details" once, and Archive is `aria-disabled` until all three settle (success or failure). A later refetch (`refreshSignal`) shows nothing.
  - error — `Failed to load {noun}.` · `text-sm text-danger`, with a `Retry` text button **only when that section is the sole path to an action the user needs** (e.g. Relations is the only way to remove a stale relation, so a dead-end failure would block the user's only remedy)

## Typography

- **Minimum text size for informational content is `text-xs` (12px).** Never use `text-[10px]` or smaller arbitrary pixel sizes for stats, labels, status text, or any text the user is meant to read. Sub-12px arbitrary sizes are reserved for decorative single-glyph indicators where the meaning is carried by an adjacent label or `title` attribute (the collapsed column abbreviation is **not** one of these: it is the only way to identify a collapsed column, so it is `text-xs` (#1738)).
- **Info-tooltip triggers (`RoleInfoTooltip`) are `w-6 h-6 text-xs`** (24px minimum target, 12px glyph floor) (#1857). A child `<strong>` inside an on-tint strip uses the same `*-on-tint` text token, never plain `text-<hue>`.
- Page headings: `text-xl font-semibold text-fg`
- Page-level section headings (Dashboard-style landmark regions like "My Boards", "Groups", "Favorite Boards"): `text-lg font-semibold text-fg`. These are top-level navigation regions on a full page, not sub-sections inside a card or panel. Pair each heading with an `id` and `aria-labelledby` on the enclosing `<section>` so the landmark has an accessible name.
- Section headings (sub-sections inside a card, panel, or modal): `text-sm font-medium text-fg-tertiary uppercase tracking-wide`
- **One section-heading style across the Members surfaces (#1495).** Within Board Settings → Members ("Add member", "To be added"), the board invite panel ("Invite by email", "Pending invites") and the group page's invite panel ("Invite by email"), section headings use the section-heading style above. On the group page, "Invite links" is the panel's title (`text-sm font-semibold text-fg-secondary`), not a section heading; "Pending invites" is a heading of the board panel variant only. Do not use `text-xs font-semibold text-fg-muted uppercase` for a section heading on these Members surfaces: it makes the newer sections look more prominent than the primary one. Two uses of that style in the Members tab are not section headings and stay as they are: the Member/Role column-header row and the "Role permissions" tooltip heading.
- Body: `text-sm text-fg-secondary`
- Muted/secondary: `text-sm text-fg-muted`
- Monospace (version strings, IDs): `font-mono text-xs text-fg-muted`

## Version display

- **Do not show a version badge or pill in the Navbar** — remove it entirely from the main nav
- Surface the version string in **Settings → About** only, where users can find it when filing support requests
- Style: inline `font-mono text-xs text-fg-muted`, no badge/pill wrapper

## Rich text editor

- **`RichTextEditor` is the only editor component** — never add a second Tiptap instance or markdown editor
- View mode uses `react-markdown` with `prose prose-sm` + `rehypeRaw` plugin — **never `dangerouslySetInnerHTML`**
- **Color token overrides: use `[&_el]:text-*` not `prose-el:text-*`** — the `prose-p:text-fg-secondary` modifier syntax is unreliable when class names appear in dynamically-joined arrays; always use explicit arbitrary variant selectors:
  ```
  text-fg-secondary                              ← base color on the prose wrapper itself
  [&_h1]:text-fg [&_h2]:text-fg [&_h3]:text-fg
  [&_p]:text-fg-secondary [&_li]:text-fg-secondary
  [&_strong]:text-fg [&_em]:text-fg-secondary
  [&_code]:text-fg [&_code]:bg-surface-hover
  [&_pre]:bg-sunken [&_blockquote]:text-fg-tertiary
  [&_a]:text-info
  ```
- **`html: true` (default) on the Markdown extension is required** — `html: false` strips `<span style="color:...">` from the serialized output, silently discarding text colors on save. Never set `html: false`.
- **`rehypeRaw` is required on `<ReactMarkdown>`** to render `<span style="color:...">` HTML spans from the Color extension in view mode
- **`onKeyDown stopPropagation` is required on `EditorContent`** — prevents board-level single-key shortcuts (e.g. `f` for filter) from firing while the user types in the description
- **View/edit toggle pattern:** hover shows `border-line-strong cursor-text` + ✎ icon (`group-hover:opacity-100 opacity-0`); click enters edit mode with `border-primary-soft bg-sunken`. Apply this pattern to all future rich editable fields.
- `readOnly` prop must be wired to `!canEdit` at every call site — viewers see rendered markdown only, no hover affordance

## Conditional admin-only elements

- Admin-only nav items and UI elements must be **hidden entirely** for non-admin users — never greyed out or rendered with reduced opacity. Use `{user.is_site_admin && ...}` (or the equivalent condition) to omit the element from the DOM entirely.
- Never use `disabled` or `opacity-50` to signal lack of permission for a navigation link — if the user cannot access it, it should not be visible at all.
- **Card-detail sub-section gating.** The collapsible sub-sections in `CardDetail` (Custom fields, Relations, Checklist, Attachments) follow one three-way rule, all outcomes being *omit from the DOM*: **board-config-gated** — the board has nothing to show (no custom field definitions): omit the section and its divider; **permission + empty** — a reader with no rows: omit the section and its divider; **permission + non-empty** — a reader with rows: render the rows read-only with every control omitted, never disabled. **Never render a header that a resolving fetch will then remove** — render nothing until the data is in; a section that appears late is better than one that appears and vanishes.
- **Permission + load-error is treated the same as permission + empty: omit.** A reader has no remedy for a failed fetch — no retry worth offering, nothing to act on — so a dead error message is worse than silence, and the section stays absent. This is deliberate, not an oversight in the rule above. A future sub-section that genuinely needs a reader-visible retry is a new exception to argue for, not an extension of this one.
- **Collapsing hides rows, never controls.** A sub-section that collapses itself when empty must keep its add affordance outside the collapse gate, or creating the first item would require expanding a section that looks empty. It must also expand itself when an item is added, or the new row lands in a hidden list and reads as a silent failure.
- **Permission-gated and context-gated are distinct reasons to hide, with the same outcome: omit from the DOM.** *Permission-gated* — the user's role forbids the action (admin-only delete, site-admin nav). *Context-gated* — the action is valid for this user but meaningless in the current surface (e.g. the "Refresh board" affordance on `ConnectionStatus` is omitted on the group page, which has no single board to refresh). In both cases render nothing rather than a disabled/greyed affordance; do not show users a control they cannot act on, regardless of *why* they cannot. Named exception: a row in a choice picker (e.g. the Move board group list) stays visible and blocked with its reason, so the user sees why a known option is unavailable (see *Blocked rows in pickers*).
- **An omitted row action the actor would otherwise expect may leave a one-line reason (#1644).** On an admin surface, when the actor holds the role for this kind of action but the server refuses it on this row (a site admin on a superuser row), omit the controls and render one `text-xs text-fg-tertiary` sentence in their place, naming who can act. Visible text, never a `title` or tooltip, so keyboard and screen-reader users reach it without hover. Tell the two cases apart by whether the reader has a remedy: this one does (ask a superuser). A reader who lacks the role altogether gets silent omission, as above.
- **A server-omitted value is omitted, not explained (#1140).** `is_admin_only` on a swimlane custom field strips the value from the payload a member or viewer receives; the frontend has nothing to hide, because it was never sent. Do **not** render a placeholder chip, a count of hidden fields, or a "some fields are only visible to admins" note on the board for that user. It is a dead end with no remedy, and it advertises that structured entity data exists on a row whose contact details the same visibility split already hides. This is the permission+load-error rule above, applied to data the client never received. The admin-side signal for the same fact belongs in Board Settings → Swimlane fields — a surface only admins can open, where it has context and an action attached.

## Swapping the open card (#449)

- The only sanctioned way to open a different card from inside `CardDetail` is `window.dispatchEvent(new CustomEvent("visiban:open-card", { detail: { cardId } }))`. `BoardView` owns the listener; no new props, no reaching into board state.
- **Panels replace, never stack.** Stacking card detail panels would create an unbounded Escape / z-index / focus-trap chain with no precedent anywhere in the app. Replacement is what the breadcrumb, the `?card=` deep link, and the command palette already do.
- **Every `<CardDetail>` mount site must carry `key={selectedCard.id}`.** The component seeds `localCard` and its collapsible-section state from `useState(card)` initializers, and its fetch effects key on the card id. Without the key, swapping cards renders the previous card's title, weight, labels, and relations while the new card's data loads — a silent stale-state bug. The only prop-sync is the `card.version` effect below.
- **`BoardView` passes `CardDetail` the live card from `board.cards`, not the `selectedCard` snapshot (#1773).** A conflict reload or forced move replaces the card there; the snapshot never changes. When `card.version` changes for a **remote** reason, `CardDetail` resets the Move popover (closes it, clears its inline error) and syncs only `version`, `column` and `swimlane` into `localCard` (the rest can hold in-progress edits; the version must follow so a checklist `onUpdated` never writes a stale one back). Key the reset on **version, never on location alone**: `useBoard`'s optimistic relocation and the rollback of the user's own failed move change the location but not the version. The reset runs only when the version goes **up**: `useBoard`'s rollback of a failed move restores an older snapshot, which is not a remote change. Versions this component caused are **not remote**: every save bumps the version and returns through `onUpdated`, so `CardDetail` remembers the version of each of its own saves and ignores a bump that matches, ignores bumps that arrive while its own move is in flight (a plain Move or the popover's forced move, #1772), and ignores bumps that arrive while its own save is in flight **unless they come with a location change** (a save never moves a card, and a socket echo of our own PATCH can beat the HTTP response). Return focus to the Move button only if the popover was open and focus was inside it or on `body`. The title field's blur check compares against the last title the field was in sync with (seeded from the card, updated by its own saves), never the live `card.title`: another session's rename must not turn a plain focus+blur into a PATCH of the old title.
- **A remote change that closes an open Move popover leaves a notice in the panel (#1773).** If the popover was open (it always pre-fills its targets, so open means "may hold a selection") and the card did not land exactly on the popover's target, `CardDetail` sets "This card was changed elsewhere. Its current location is shown." in an always-mounted `role="status"` line under the breadcrumb row (empty, so no layout space, otherwise). A card that lands on the popover's target (another session placed it exactly there) is the user's own outcome: it closes silently. So does a remote edit that leaves the card where it was while the popover still holds its untouched pre-filled targets: nothing the user chose is lost. A remote change that arrives while the user's own move is in flight is deliberately not acted on, so the failure keeps its inline error visible; the version-conflict reload that follows is a newer version and then closes the popover with the notice. A successful forced move shows no notice: the popover's own override (#1772) is in flight when its version bump lands, and it lands on the popover's target anyway; a conflict leaves the notice, and the toast remains. A remote bump with the popover closed clears an older notice. The notice also clears when the popover next opens. Exception to one live region per action (the `move_failed` bullet below states the rule): the conflict toast is announced assertively from the shared toast region (§ Toasts), which floats above the sheet at `z-[55]` but sits outside its focus trap, so this line is the in-sheet record of the conflict; a screen reader may announce both, which is accepted.
- **Never render a navigation affordance to an archived card.** `visiban:open-card` resolves against the board's active cards and silently no-ops, so an archived row renders its title as a `<span>`, not a `<button>`.

## Card detail sheet placement (#1914)

Rules this work surfaced that apply beyond the sheet:

- **The card hover peek is suppressed for every card while the card detail sheet is open (#1918).** A floating peek over the sheet covers the fields being edited, and the sheet already shows full detail. BoardCell passes `peekSuppressed={currentCardId != null}` to CardItem (BoardView threads `currentCardId={selectedCard?.id ?? null}` down through SwimlaneRow; the prop is included in `arePropsEqual`). Any new board CardItem call site must pass it too. Opening the sheet clears any pending peek timer and unmounts a visible peek; closing it does not re-show a peek until the next mouseenter. Any new hover-only floating preview on the board follows the same rule; the aging tooltip and hover lift are not covered.
- **A handler that default-prevents `mousedown` to keep focus where it is must first blur or flush any save-on-blur field it is about to unmount.** React runs no `onBlur` on a node it unmounts, so the edit is silently lost otherwise. Reference: the sheet's outside-click dismissal.
- **A ring around a board card uses `ring-offset-sunken`**, the surface the board paints behind its cards, never `ring-offset-canvas`, so the offset gap matches that surface in both themes. This covers `CardItem`'s keyboard focus ring as well as its open-card (`current`) ring.
- **An outside-click dismisser decides explicitly which classes of target it consumes.** Board space and empty cells are consumed; interactive controls elsewhere in the app pass through. Document the split wherever such a dismisser is added.

The card detail sheet sits next to the card it describes instead of at the far right of a wide monitor. It is the documented exception to the anchored-popover rule (#1455/#1457, § Modals and dialogs).

- **The anchor rule** lives in `computeCardSheetPlacement` (`src/hooks/useCardSheetPlacement.ts`), unit-tested on its own. Width 540px, gap 6px from the column (14px from the card), 16px from the viewport's right edge. At `<= 640px` the sheet is full-screen. From 641px to 1279px it docks to the right edge. At `>= 1280px` it goes right of the card's column if it fits, else left of it, else it docks. It never covers the sticky lane labels and never runs off screen. Anchored: top = the bottom of the board chrome + 8px (the board toolbar, or the filter row or a banner below it when shown: the lower of the toolbar's bottom and `data-board-scroll`'s top), bottom 12px above the viewport edge, `rounded-lg border border-primary-emphasis shadow-2xl` (the 1px brand-blue outline is the cue's other half; docked and full-screen keep `border-line`). Docked: full height, square, `border-l border-line shadow-2xl`.
- **The DOM markers it reads are a contract:** `data-board-scroll` on BoardView's scroll container (the root every lookup is scoped to, so the drag overlay copy never matches), `data-card-id` on the `CardItem` face (not on the `overlay` copy), `data-board-cell` on `BoardCell`, `data-lane-label` on SwimlaneRow's sticky label panel, and `nav[aria-label="Board toolbar"]`. Renaming any of them breaks placement silently; `boardCell`, `swimlaneRow` and `cardItemCompact` tests pin them.
- **No element, no anchor.** A card with no measurable face (a deep link before render, the command palette, a filtered-out card, a collapsed lane or column) docks with no ring or notch, never centered. It switches to anchored if the face appears later (a few frames of retry at open, then a `ResizeObserver` on the board content). After an in-sheet Move the sheet re-anchors to the new column, or docks if the face is gone.
- **Re-placed** on open, swap and Move (layout effect, before paint), and on window resize and board scroll-end after 150ms. The notch alone follows vertical board scroll every frame. The sheet never follows the card vertically and never scrolls the board. Only a scroll of the board container or the document counts: the sheet's own body scrolling is ignored.
- **Notch (#1937, reshaped #1946):** a speech-bubble tail on the sheet's outer edge, `aria-hidden`, `fill-primary-emphasis` (the open card's blue), on a 1px `border-primary-emphasis` sheet border that exists in anchored mode only. Tracking: an isosceles triangle, 12px base on the edge, 5px deep (`M7 6L1 12L7 18Z` in a 7x24 SVG). Clamped (card scrolled out of the 28px-from-corner range): a right-triangle wedge with the same base and depth whose apex is the end nearest the card (`down: M7 6L1 18H7Z`, `up: M7 6H1L7 18Z`, `data-direction`), so the shape alone shows direction and color is never the only signal. Always on the edge facing the card, same SVG both sides: `side=right` (sheet right of the column) puts it on the sheet's left edge with `-left-[7px]`; `side=left` puts it on the right edge with `-right-[7px] -scale-x-100`, no shadow, no stroke, no connector lines, hidden when docked, full-screen or the card is not rendered. **The sheet root must stay `overflow-visible`** (the tail sits outside it): clipping lives on an inner wrapper `flex h-full flex-col overflow-hidden`, with `rounded-[7px]` only when anchored (a docked or full-screen panel is flush and square). Never use `mask`/`clip-path` on the root: it clips `shadow-2xl`. Anything inside the wrapper that shows a focus ring at its edge must use `focus:ring-inset` (the tabs do), or the clip eats it, and an absolutely positioned overlay inside the sheet whose containing block is a positioned element within the wrapper is clipped too, so it must be `fixed` or fit inside the sheet (the wrapper itself is not `relative`, so an overlay positioned against the root is not clipped). The wrapper's `rounded-[7px]` is the root's `rounded-lg` (8px) minus the 1px border: change one, change the other. The tail's `top` is `notch.y - 13` (the SVG's 12px half-height plus 1px, because absolute offsets start at the padding edge and `y` at the border edge); `-left-[7px]` leaves the base 1px over the border and the apex 5px beyond it. **A decorative pointer that links two surfaces must hold >= 3:1 (WCAG 1.4.11) against both of them in light and dark, computed from the token values, never judged by eye.** `primary-emphasis` clears it against the panel (`surface`) and the board (`sunken`, `canvas`): dark 3.98/4.85/5.48, light 3.68/3.52/3.36. `border-line` (1.4-2.0:1 in dark, 1.4-1.5:1 in light) and `border-line-strong` (1.9-2.7:1 in dark, 2.3-2.6:1 in light) do not, so never use them for a pointer or notch.
- **The open card** gets `current` on `CardItem`: `border-2 -m-px border-primary-emphasis ring-2 ring-primary-emphasis ring-offset-2 ring-offset-sunken` and `aria-current="true"` (the 2px border and `aria-current` mean color is never the only signal). BoardView passes `currentCardId` down through SwimlaneRow and BoardCell, so the ring moves with the swap and clears on close.
- **Backdrop:** none when anchored or full-screen. Docked: `bg-backdrop/20` that is `pointer-events-none` and `aria-hidden`. It is visual only, never a click target.
- **Click-catcher, not an overlay.** A document-level listener closes the sheet on a primary click outside it. It classifies at `pointerdown` and acts at the following `click`, so a touch swipe, a scrollbar drag or a selection drag that started inside never closes it. What happens to the dismissing click is decided per target class. On board space (anything inside `data-board-scroll`, including an empty cell's "+ Add card") and on anything that is not a control, the click is consumed and the press does not move focus (its `mousedown` is default-prevented), so it never also activates what was under it and focus can return to the card. A control elsewhere in the app (a toolbar, sidebar or navbar button or link, `OUTSIDE_CONTROL_SELECTOR` in `CardDetail`) closes the sheet and still works in the same click, taking focus as usual. Before closing, the handler blurs the focused field inside the sheet, so a save-on-blur edit (the title) is saved. "Inside" is the React tree: the sheet's `onPointerDownCapture` marks the native event, so a dropdown or tooltip portaled from inside `CardDetail` counts as inside. A click on a board card (`[data-card-id]`) is left to the card's own `onClick`, which swaps the open card; a press on the open card's own face is default-prevented so focus stays in the sheet. While a sheet is open `BoardCell` renders no selection checkbox (it would take focus out of the `aria-modal` sheet and raise the bulk toolbar behind it). A popover a card face portals to `document.body` (the custom-field quick edit and its dropdowns, the hover peek, which cannot be open while the sheet is, #1918) is part of the board: BoardView's scroll container marks pointer events from its React tree (`markBoardTreePointerEvent`), and the click-catcher leaves a marked event that lands outside the board's DOM alone. A new body-level portal opened from a board card needs no attribute; one opened from elsewhere that must keep the sheet open uses `data-keeps-card-sheet-open`. The shared toast region carries `data-keeps-card-sheet-open`, so every toast stays usable while the sheet is open (§ Toasts). A click inside another dialog opened over the sheet (`[aria-modal="true"]` or `[role="dialog"]`, such as the command palette) is left to that dialog, neither closing the sheet nor being consumed. Swap-by-click is a pointer convenience: keyboard and screen-reader users press Escape, then pick a card.
- **Drag-and-drop is suspended** while the sheet is open (BoardView passes no sensors to the board `DndContext`). The old full-screen backdrop blocked drags too, so this is no regression.
- **Focus:** on open, focus goes to Close. `aria-modal` and `useFocusTrap` stay. On close, focus returns to the opener (the clicked card, or the most recently clicked one after a swap). If the opener is gone (command palette, relation link) it goes to the card's face, and if that is gone too (archived, deleted or removed remotely in the same commit that closes the sheet), to the board region (`data-board-scroll`, `tabIndex={-1}`), never to the top of the page. The fallback is resolved when the sheet closes, not at render: `useFocusTrap` accepts a function `restoreTarget` for exactly this, because at render time the card's face is still connected.
- **Full-screen (<= 640px):** a "‹ Board" back control (accessible name "Back to board", distinct from the toolbar's Board view tab) on the left and Close top-right, both 44px tall. Back, Close and Escape all close and return focus.
- **Transitions** (`cardSheetMotion.ts`, Web Animations API, feature-detected, so jsdom never animates). Open: 180ms ease-out, fade plus a 12px move toward the card. Same-side swap or re-place: a 220ms `cubic-bezier(.2,.8,.2,1)` slide while the old body cross-fades out (80ms) and the new one in (120ms). Side flip, mode change or Move: fade out 90ms, reposition, fade in 140ms. Close: 140ms ease-in, fade plus 8px away. Under `prefers-reduced-motion` every one of these becomes a 120ms opacity change (a swap is 120ms out, then 120ms in). **Transitions never delay a state change.** The departing sheet leaves a "ghost", an inert, `aria-hidden`, id- and role-stripped clone on `document.body` that removes itself, so close and swap unmount the real dialog at once. A Playwright spec that measures the sheet's box must first wait for `el.getAnimations({ subtree: true })` to finish. Desktop Chrome's 1280px viewport is exactly the anchored/docked boundary, so card specs pin a viewport per mode.
- **Long content (frame 5):** the header (title, breadcrumb, tabs) and the footer stay fixed while the body scrolls. A top and a bottom fade show only while there is more content on that side.
- **Shared demo (frame 7):** the anchored sheet starts below the demo bar and app bar. A `DEMO_CARD_DETAIL_NOTE` line sits at the top of the body. Comments, Relations and the PR/MR link (`CardExternalRefSection`'s `demoReason`: Add `aria-disabled` with the reason, no Edit or Remove) are each refused in place with their own reason. The footer replaces Archive and Delete with a `DEMO_CARD_FOOTER_NOTE` line, so the sheet offers no destructive or removing action. This is a UI restriction: the demo fence would allow the archive itself.

## Card detail sheet: Dock to edge toggle (#1915)

- **A user pin that outranks an automatic placement goes into the pure placement function.** `computeCardSheetPlacement` takes `docked` and ranks it below full-screen (`<= 640px`, toggle hidden) and above the 1280px anchor rule, so the precedence is unit-tested without the DOM. Toggling re-places with `PlacementReason` `"dock-toggle"`, which `classifyTransition` maps to the swap `slide` (reduced motion: the 120ms fades), or `none` when the frame did not move.
- **Storage:** `user:prefs:card-sheet-docked` (`useCardSheetDockedPref`, the `useCardLayoutPref` pattern; absent = `false`, no `User` field). It is read once per sheet mount, so a swap keeps the mode.
- **Header order is DOM order = visual order: Dock, then Close** (A-Q8). Focus on open stays on Close; Shift+Tab reaches Dock. The labels are `Dock to edge` (off) / `Open next to card` (on) as both `aria-label` and `title`, with `aria-pressed`.
- **The pressed state is more than a tint (WCAG 1.4.1):** the button renders `ActiveUnderline` (default `inset`, host is `relative`) while docked, per the #2188 icon-toggle rule, with the icon swap and border (`bg-surface-hover border-line-strong`) as reinforcement. `aria-pressed` stays the state.
- **Test pins:** Playwright specs for placement pin a viewport per mode (1600 anchored, 1024 docked, 640 full-screen); the dock toggle is exercised at 1600 where it changes the mode.

## Externally-sourced URLs (#1087)

- **Every externally-sourced URL is gated by `isSafeHttpUrl()` (`utils/safeUrl.ts`) before it reaches an `href` or `window.open`.** "Externally sourced" means anything a third party influenced: lens/provider JSON, imports, integration payloads, other API-sourced links. `href` is an XSS sink, so `javascript:`, `data:`, `vbscript:`, relative and garbage values must never be bound; the helper allows `http:`/`https:` only and rejects surrounding whitespace and control characters.
- **Fail closed, render text.** When the check fails, render the value as plain text (no `<a>`, no `href`) or make the handler a no-op. Never fall back to the raw value, and never show an error for it.
- **Precedence:** use `isHttpUrl` (`externalRef.ts`: also a length cap, no credentials) where it already applies (card external refs, URL custom fields, extension panel links); `isSafeHttpUrl` is the minimum for everything else. Both enforce http(s) only and no embedded credentials; `isHttpUrl` adds a length cap. Either satisfies this rule.
- **Exempt:** server-constructed same-origin URLs (attachments, export endpoints, `oauthUrl`) are not externally sourced.
- A new `<a href={...}>` or `window.open(...)` bound to API data without one of these guards is a review finding.

## Long URL display fields

- Truncate long URLs in read-only display inputs: `truncate overflow-hidden text-ellipsis whitespace-nowrap`
- The full value must remain in the clipboard on copy — only the display is truncated
- Add a `title` attribute (or tooltip on hover) showing the full URL

## Click handlers need keyboard parity (#1376, Sonar S1082)

A click handler never goes on a non-interactive element (`div`, `span`, `p`, `h1`) without keyboard parity. Pick the first option that fits:

1. **Make it a `<button type="button">`** — inline-rename text (column/swimlane/board/group names), file dropzones, and "click the text to edit" regions. Tailwind preflight already resets button chrome; add `text-left`, `block`, or `max-w-full truncate` as the original element needed. A truncating button inside a `<p>` needs `block`. Add the standard `focus:outline-none focus:ring-2 focus:ring-primary-emphasis rounded`. For a heading, put the button *inside* the `<h1>` so heading semantics survive. Do not nest block elements (`div`/`p`) or inputs inside the button — use `<span className="block">` and render hidden file inputs as siblings.
2. **Put the handler on the focusable control** — an overlay `<input type="date">` owns its own `onClick` (picker fallback) instead of a wrapper `div`; a `<label htmlFor>` forwards clicks on label text to its switch (see `ToggleField`).
3. **Decorative pointer-only backdrops** — `aria-hidden="true"` when the backdrop is a sibling of the panel (drawers, tour spotlight, bulk-add overlay), `role="presentation"` when it is the panel's parent (`ModalWrapper`). Both require that Escape (via `useEscapeStack`) and a visible Close button already exist.
4. **A `stopPropagation`-only wrapper is a smell** — delete the wrapper handler and move the guard into the parent handler (e.g. `closest("[data-column-drag-handle]")` in the collapsed `ColumnHeader`).

**Pencil policy:** when a text button is paired with a hover-reveal `✎` pencil that starts the same edit, the text button is the single keyboard tab stop and the pencil is `tabIndex={-1}` (keep its `aria-label`, `focus:opacity-100`, and ring). Never two tab stops for one field.

**Allowed shapes that are not a real button:**
- `CardItem` forwards the dnd-kit `listeners.onKeyDown` explicitly. This is the one sanctioned bare `onKeyDown` on a `div`: the div already has `role="button"` and `tabIndex` from dnd-kit `attributes`, and the restatement only makes the handler visible to static analysis.
- `SelectDropdown` options use the `aria-activedescendant` shape: the combobox trigger owns keyboard selection, and each `role="option"` gets `tabIndex={-1}` plus an Enter/Space handler for parity.

Otherwise never add a bare `onKeyDown` to a `div` just to silence the rule; if the element is operable it should be a real control.

## Focus ring consistency

The permitted focus-ring form is `focus:ring-2 focus:ring-primary-emphasis` (or `focus:ring-danger-emphasis` for destructive actions, `focus:ring-warning-emphasis` for amber confirms). `focus-visible:` is **not permitted** on standalone buttons, dropdown triggers, or tab controls — Firefox and desktop Safari treat `:focus-visible` as more restrictive than `:focus` and skip the ring on pointer-driven focus, leaving keyboard users with no indicator after a click+keyboard-handoff sequence.

`focus-visible:` is permitted only on elements that receive programmatic focus from drag-and-drop libraries (e.g. `CardItem` while a drag is mid-flight) where suppressing the ring during pointer drag is intentional.

**Gate for any PR that touches interactive elements:** run `grep -r "focus-visible:ring" frontend/src/`. If any new instances appear outside the documented DnD exception, switch them to `focus:`. Issues #933, #949, and #983 all closed the same regression — keep this rule visible so it does not recur.

**Confirmation-dialog Cancel buttons need a ring too.** The secondary / "Cancel" button in a confirm or destructive-action dialog is a real tab stop and the keyboard user's safe escape path — it must carry `focus:outline-none focus:ring-2 focus:ring-primary-emphasis rounded`, not only the primary or danger action button. A bare-text Cancel with no ring leaves keyboard users with no focus indicator on the very control they are most likely to reach for.

## Hover-reveal controls

When an action button is hidden until hover (`opacity-0 group-hover:opacity-100`), it **must** also include `focus:opacity-100` and a focus ring so keyboard users can reach and activate it. Without `focus:opacity-100`, the button is unreachable by keyboard. This applies to all hover-reveal controls (comment delete, swimlane edit, RTE pencil icon, etc.).

**A sortable list's drag handle is a real `<button type="button">` (#1739).** Spread dnd-kit's `attributes` and `listeners` onto the button, name it `Drag to reorder <name>`, give it the standard focus ring plus `focus:opacity-100` when hover-revealed, and wrap the glyph (`⋮⋮`) in an `aria-hidden` span. A `<span>` with `focus:opacity-100` and no ring is not a keyboard control. The `<name>` is the item's visible text, or its 1-based position when that is empty, never dnd-kit's internal id. A handle may be hover-revealed (the field lists) or always visible (the choice rows, which are short, dense and touch-reached, so no `opacity-0` and no `focus:opacity-100`); either way the glyph is `text-fg-tertiary hover:text-fg`, not `text-fg-faint`, which is too faint for the row's only drag affordance. The keyboard reorder path is dnd-kit's `KeyboardSensor` (`useDragSensors({ keyboard: true })`: Space or Enter lifts, arrows move, Space drops, Escape cancels), so the handle must be a named, visible tab stop. Reference: the Board Settings card-field and swimlane-field lists, and their editors' **choice rows** (#2190, `SortableChoiceList`), which also show three details a sortable list inside a modal needs: (1) **Announce by name, never by dnd-kit's internal id**: pass `accessibility.announcements` (and `screenReaderInstructions`) that read the item's name and `position N of M`; the default reads the sortable id. (2) **Focus follows the moved item.** Rows keyed by index leave the focused handle in place after a keyboard drop, on a different item; move focus to the handle at the new index once the reordered list renders. (3) **Escape that cancels a drag must not also close the host modal**: register an escape-stack entry above the modal (`CHOICE_DRAG_ESCAPE_PRIORITY` / `FIELD_DRAG_ESCAPE_PRIORITY`, 40.7) that consumes Escape only while a drag is in progress and returns `false` otherwise. It works because the escape stack's document listener is registered before dnd-kit's (added at pick-up), so it still sees the drag; any such entry needs a test that Escape mid-drag cancels the drag and leaves the modal open, and that the next Escape closes the modal (`e2e/choice-keyboard-reorder.spec.ts`). The field lists share the same wiring through `useSortableFieldDrag` (#2190); focus-follow does not apply to them because field ids are stable. Known exception: the board grid's column and swimlane handles have no keyboard dragging by design (cards move through the card-detail Move popover). Do not copy that pattern into a new list.

## Card-face quick-edit chip affordance (custom fields, #371)

Pinned custom-field chips on the card face (`CardItem`, checkbox/dropdown types only) are a deliberate exception to the hover-reveal-by-default convention above and to § Inline description fields' hover-only pencil-icon pattern: the dotted-underline edit affordance (`border-b border-dotted border-fg-tertiary` on the value text) is **always visible at rest**, not gated behind `group-hover:opacity-100`.

**Why:** VoC input flagged that Sam (occasional, non-daily user) gets no opportunity to learn a hover-only affordance exists on a card face he visits only a few times a week — by the time he'd hover to discover it, he's already concluded there's no way to edit the value inline. A persistent, low-contrast dotted underline is discoverable without training and adds no visual weight to the chip beyond the browser's own `<abbr title>` convention for "this text is a control."

This exception is scoped narrowly to the pinned-chip quick-edit affordance itself. It does **not** relax the hover-reveal convention for anything else on the card — the card's other hover-only controls (the selection checkbox, etc.) keep their existing `opacity-0 group-hover:opacity-100 focus:opacity-100` treatment. Do not generalize "always visible" to other inline-edit affordances without a matching VoC finding; hover-reveal stays the default.

## Number custom-field formatting (#1391)

1. **One formatter.** Every read-only rendering of a number value (card-face chip, row chip, card detail, peek, filter chip) goes through `formatCustomFieldValue` → `formatNumberValue` in `utils/customFieldValue.ts`. Never format a number value inline at a call site. With all three options at their defaults it returns the raw string untouched — no grouping is added to an unformatted field. A non-numeric stored value renders raw. The minus sign goes before the prefix (`-$5.00`). **Chips do not character-slice a formatted number:** the card-face (16) and row (20) character caps cut the suffix — the unit — first, so `chipValueText` leaves a number with any format option whole and lets the chip's CSS `truncate` clip it only when it truly does not fit (the `title` carries the full text). Unformatted numbers and every other type keep the character cap.
2. **The settings Format block** (`components/Board/NumberFormatFields.tsx`, shared by the Card fields and Swimlane fields tabs) renders **only** while the selected type is `number`: a `Format` section label, then `grid grid-cols-2 sm:grid-cols-3 gap-3` (Decimals wraps to a second row on phones) of three `<label>`-wrapped inputs — Prefix and Suffix (`maxLength={10}`, placeholders `e.g. $` / `e.g. h`, never trimmed), Decimals (`type="number" min=0 max=10`, empty = as typed). Below it, one reserved `text-xs min-h-4` slot holds `Preview: …` (`text-fg-muted`, the formatter applied to `1234.5`; `min-h-4` because a long prefix/suffix can wrap it) or, for invalid decimals, `Enter 0 to 10.` (`text-danger`, `role="alert"` on that error span only — never on the preview or the slot, which would announce every keystroke — with `aria-invalid` + `border-danger focus:ring-danger-emphasis` on the input) — never both. Invalid decimals block Save. A non-number type sends none of the three keys; the server clears them on a retype.
3. **The adorned number input.** In `CustomFieldValueInput`, a prefix and/or suffix wraps the raw `type="number"` input in `inline-flex w-auto min-w-28 max-w-full items-center bg-surface border border-line rounded focus-within:ring-2 focus-within:ring-primary-emphasis focus-within:border-transparent` — never a fixed width, so two 10-character adornments cannot squeeze the number — the input itself is borderless `bg-transparent` and keeps the plain input's width (`w-20` sm / `w-32` md) with a `min-w-[4rem]` floor, and each adornment is a `shrink-0 select-none whitespace-pre text-fg-muted` span with `aria-hidden="true"`. Because the adornments are hidden from assistive tech, the input's `aria-label` carries the unit: `{field name} ({prefix suffix})`, e.g. `Budget ($ USD)`. With neither set, render the original plain input — no wrapper.

## Custom-field choice color picker (#1391)

- **One control per choice row, in both field-editor tabs.** `components/Board/ChoiceColorPicker.tsx` renders in front of each choice input, only while the type is `dropdown` or `multi_select`: a `p-0.5` (24px hit area) button around a `w-5 h-5 rounded-full` swatch: filled with the explicit color's `base` (`border border-line-strong`), or — for *Automatic* — a **hollow `border-dashed border-line-strong` circle with no fill**. Never fill Automatic with the hash `choiceColor`: a filled circle reads as a picked color, and a multi-select entry with no color shows no dot at all. `aria-label="Color for {choice}: {Name|Automatic}"` (also the `title`), `aria-haspopup="dialog"`, `aria-expanded`; disabled while the choice is blank. Clicking it opens a `role="dialog"` popover (`bg-surface border border-line-strong rounded-lg shadow-xl p-2`, labelled `Color for {choice}`) holding the current name, a `role="radiogroup" aria-label="Palette"` `grid grid-cols-4 gap-2` of the 8 swatches (`role="radio"`, `aria-label` = the capitalized key, `aria-checked`), and a `Reset to automatic` text button. The selected swatch shows a check icon **and** `ring-2 ring-fg ring-offset-1 ring-offset-surface` — the `text-on-primary` check alone is under 3:1 on amber, green and teal, so selection never rests on it.
- **Keyboard:** one roving tab stop; arrow keys move focus (Up/Down by a row of 4) without selecting, Space/Enter select and close, focus returns to the swatch button. Escape closes **only** the popover — `useEscapeStack` at 47 (see the modal allocation list); a click outside, or focus leaving the picker (Tab out — a `focusout` whose `relatedTarget` is outside the wrapper), closes it without a change and without pulling focus back. No focus trap.
- **Edge handling inside the scrolling settings panel.** On open, the picker measures the viewport space below its trigger and opens upward (`bottom-full mb-1` instead of `top-full mt-1`) when under 200px is left, and the popover calls `scrollIntoView({ block: "nearest" })` on mount, so one opened on the last choice row is never half out of view.
- **Draft-only, saved with the field.** A pick changes the editor draft; the existing **Save field** sends `choice_colors` (only for choice types — a non-choice type sends no key and the server clears the map on retype). Never a save per pick.
- **Renaming resets the color, and the editor says so.** Colors are keyed by the choice text: editing or removing a choice drops its entry from the draft (`withoutChoiceColor`), and `choiceColorsPayload` sends only trimmed, current choices with known keys. The note `Renaming a choice resets its color.` (`text-xs text-fg-muted`) sits directly under the choice list, shown only while at least one choice in the draft has an explicit color — with nothing to lose it is noise. Choice names are user text used as object keys, so draft and payload maps are built with `Object.fromEntries` / read with `Object.hasOwn` (`withChoiceColor`, `draftChoiceColor`, `choiceColorsPayload`): a plain `obj[choice] = …` silently drops a choice named `__proto__`.

## Column policy editor and confirm-on-entry checklist (#1101)

- **Editor.** `EditColumnModal` has two tabs, **Settings** and **Policy**, as a full tablist (`role="tablist"`/`"tab"`/`"tabpanel"`, `aria-selected`, roving `tabIndex`, Left/Right/Home/End). It is a fixed-height tabbed modal (`h-[85vh] max-h-[640px]`) whose body is one focusable `role="region"` with the bottom fade, and whose Save/Cancel footer is shared by both tabs. **Add policy…** and **Edit policy** pass `initialTab="policy"` and focus that tab per § Deep-link into a tabbed modal. The Policy tab (`ColumnPolicyEditor`) holds two reorderable lists (**Entry criteria**, **Definition of done**), each at most 8 one-line inputs (`maxLength` 200) with ↑ / ↓ / × icon buttons, and **+ Add criterion** below, whose accessible name begins with its visible text and names the list (`Add criterion to Entry criteria`, WCAG 2.5.3); an empty list reads `No criteria yet.` Reorder is by buttons, never drag, so it works inside the modal's focus trap. The ↑ on the first row and ↓ on the last are `aria-disabled`, not `disabled`, so a moved row's button keeps focus at the ends; **+ Add criterion** at the cap is `aria-disabled` with a visible `Up to 8 criteria per list.` reason tied by `aria-describedby`. Reorders and removals are announced in an `sr-only` `role="status"`. The mode is a § Radio groups card pair: **Show the criteria** (advisory, default) and **Ask the mover to confirm**. The helper under Definition of done is fixed copy: "Definition of done shows as a read-only list in card detail. It never blocks moving out." (read-only list, not "checklist": card detail renders it as display-only bullets). Drafts on both tabs persist while switching tabs; the Name field auto-focuses only on the first open, never when the user arrows back to Settings. Save trims criteria, drops blank rows, sends `policy: null` when both lists are empty, and sends `policy` **only when the drafts differ from the value the modal opened with**, so a rename never overwrites a policy another admin changed meanwhile. With a blank name, Save is `disabled` and the footer's always-mounted status line reads "Name is required." (tied to Save by `aria-describedby`), because on the Policy tab the Name field is out of sight. The anonymous share-link payload omits `policy` (`PublicColumnSerializer`; `PublicColumn` on the client), so the editor carries no share-link warning.
- **Board settings policies section (#2156).** **Board settings → Rules → Column policies** (`BoardSettingsPoliciesSection`) lists every column in `board.columns` order, hidden ones included (a `Hidden` badge only when the viewer hid it), with a one-line summary: `No policy`, or `{counts} · Advisory|Confirm on entry`. Non-admins get a read-only list with the criteria in labeled ordered lists and no buttons; admins get an inline accordion, and demo mode inherits the Rules tab's `DemoInert`. Rules the accordion introduces:
  - **One editor open at a time.** Each row is a disclosure button (`aria-expanded`, `aria-controls` only while open, `min-h-11`); the open row mounts `ColumnPolicyEditor` in a `role="group"` labeled by that button, with `useColumnPolicyDraft` seeding the draft at open and never reseeding it from live props. If the open column disappears the row collapses silently.
  - **Switching away from a dirty row uses the inline Confirm/Cancel pattern** (§ Modals and dialogs): a `role="status" aria-live="polite" aria-atomic="true"` wrapper reading `Discard changes to “X” and open “Y”?`, `Confirm` (`text-danger font-medium`) discards and opens, `Cancel` keeps editing; `useConfirmFocusReturn` returns focus to the clicked row and Escape cancels the prompt at priority `40.6`. A clean row switches without a prompt; clicking the open dirty row collapses and discards like the editor's own Cancel button.
  - **A dirty draft is discarded silently on tab switch or modal close.** No prompt: the section unmounts with the tab.
  - **Focus returns to the row's disclosure button after Save or Cancel**, and a successful save announces `Policy saved for {name}.` in an always-mounted `sr-only` `role="status"` region.
  - **Saves are pessimistic.** Save is disabled until the draft differs from the seeded policy; while saving the editor and Cancel are disabled and the other rows are `aria-disabled`. A failure keeps the row open and the draft, and shows the server message (else `Couldn't save the policy. Try again.`) inline in the row's status region, cleared on the next edit. The request is `PATCH` with `{policy}` only (the column `PUT` requires `name`), then the returned column goes to `onColumnUpdated`.
- **During a drag.** While a card from another column is over a column with entry criteria and no limit blocks the drop, the cell shows an `aria-hidden` **Entry: a · b** pill (same position and opaque surface as the blocked-drop label, `text-fg-secondary`), and the drag live region appends `Entry criteria for '{column}': a; b.` Like a limit verdict, a cell with criteria always announces past the hover throttle. A blocked cell shows only its verdict. The pill is one line and truncates; the live region carries the full list, so the truncation is a known, accepted limit of the visual cue.
- **Confirm on entry.** In `confirm` mode a drop opens `PolicyConfirmModal` (`ModalWrapper`) titled `Move "{card title}" to {column}?` (the card's title, since cards have no short key), with the entry criteria as native `accent-primary` checkboxes. **Move to {column}** is `aria-disabled` with `aria-describedby` on an always-mounted `X of N still to check.` status line until every box is checked; **Keep in {source column}** is the secondary cancel. Escape and the backdrop cancel too. Focus returns to the moved card (`[data-card-id]`) either way, retried for a few animation frames because a confirmed move re-renders the card in its new cell. The modal is keyed by card, column **and the criteria text**, so a policy edit arriving over the socket remounts it with every box unchecked (a new criterion can never be skipped); when the card or column disappears, or the column stops asking for confirmation, an effect cancels the held move, announces "Move canceled, the column or its policy changed" and returns focus to the card. **A limit block wins**: when `placementVerdict` predicts a block, the move is sent as is so the server's refusal raises `MoveBlockedToast`, and no checklist opens; this matches the server's order. The checklist covers drag-and-drop moves (pointer and keyboard) only; the card-detail Move popover, bulk moves, the card context menu's Move to… and the palette's Move card to… (#2059), an admin's "Move anyway (admin override)" from `MoveBlockedToast` after a limit block (`forceMoveCard`), API, MCP and import do not prompt. It is a client-side acknowledgment, never enforced by the server, and the docs say so.
- **Card detail.** The current column's **Definition of done** is a display-only list in the Details tab, after Description: small `bg-fg-tertiary` bullet dots (`aria-hidden`), the same glyph as the popover, never empty boxes, which read as checkboxes that do nothing; `text-sm text-fg-secondary` items; and a `text-xs text-fg-tertiary` "For reference. It never blocks moving the card on." line. Its label keeps the sibling sections' `text-xs font-semibold uppercase tracking-wide text-fg-muted` styling but is an `h3` naming the `section` (a deliberate departure from the sibling `<p>` labels, so screen readers get a named region). Omitted when the column has no done criteria (board-config-gated).

## Toasts (#2060, #1438)

One shared toast region for the whole app, from the 1.3 UI spec Part 9 (a design proposal, not user research). `ToastProvider` (`src/contexts/ToastContext.tsx`) is mounted once in `App`; anything raises a toast with `useToast().notify({...})` (`src/contexts/useToast.ts`; a no-op outside the provider, so isolated component tests need no wrapper unless they assert on the toast). The queue rules are pure functions in `src/utils/toastQueue.ts`, unit-tested on their own. **Never add another independently positioned toast**: today's adopters are the three bridges `MoveBlockedToast`, `DemoWriteBlockedToast` and `ImportSkippedToast` (they raise toasts and render nothing), `BoardView`'s "Card archived" toast (`success`, key `archive`, 5 s, action "View in Archived panel", which opens the panel and dismisses the toast), and `CardBlockSection`'s unblock outcome, because components at one anchor could not know about each other and the later one hid the earlier. Since #2107 the rest have moved too, and **there are no exceptions left**: the board preset notice (`usePresetNoticeToast`, `info`, key `preset-notice`, Undo action without the `undo` guard because the Preset menu offers the same Undo, 8 s or 20 s for a `?preset=` link, focus returns to the picker), `BoardView`'s card-not-found notice (`warning`, key `card-not-found`, 4 s, a deep link to a card not on the board or an open card that left it, #1615), the single-card action results of #2059 (key `card-action`: a failure is `danger` and persists, a result whose effect is not visible on the board such as Copy link or a delete is `success`; a result the board shows itself, such as Move, Assign or Priority, raises no toast and is announced through an `sr-only` status only), and the "You've joined …" confirmation on the group page and the board (`useJoinedToast`, `success`, key `joined`, persists until dismissed). `ImportSkippedToast`'s warnings list is a focusable `ul` inside the region, reached by Tab and tested (`importExport.test.tsx`). `BulkActionToolbar`'s own result slot is **not** a toast (§ Bulk operations rule 3) and stays where it is.

- **Variants: shape + word, hue only reinforces (WCAG 1.4.1).** `success` (`✓`, `bg-surface border-line-strong`), `info` (`ℹ`, the shipped `bg-primary/15 border-primary-emphasis/40` shell over an opaque `bg-surface`), `warning` (`⚠`, `bg-surface border-warning`), `blocked` (same amber shell, `⚠`, or `⛔` only for a hard limit via `hardGlyph`, still reserved per § Move-blocked toast), `danger` (`bg-surface border-danger`, an inline-SVG octagon with an ×, `currentColor`; never `⛔`, which is the hard WIP block, and never `⊘`, the card blocked signal). `blocked` and `danger` stay separate so a move refusal stays amber: `blocked` = "the system refused, here is why", `danger` = "something failed". The title is `font-medium` in the variant's `*-on-tint` token, even on the untinted shell (the on-tint tokens out-contrast the plain ones on the tinted shells; ratios in § Color tokens); the body is `text-sm text-fg`, a secondary line `text-xs text-fg-tertiary`.
- **One action at most**, a `text-xs underline` text button in the variant's on-tint token, a sibling of the message, never inside a live region (#1772): `Undo` on `warning`/`info`, `Move anyway (admin override)` on `blocked`. An action never dismisses its own toast; its owner replaces it (same `key`) or dismisses it by key once the outcome is known. An `onAction` that returns a promise shows `pendingLabel` ("Moving anyway…", "Undoing…"), is `aria-disabled` (never `disabled`) and freezes the timer until it settles.
- **Priority:** `danger` = `blocked` > `warning` with action > `warning` > `info` with action > `info` > `success`. **Cap 3 visible.** The stack grows upward; the highest priority sits at the bottom, nearest the anchor, ties newest nearest the bottom. The DOM order is bottom first (`flex-col-reverse`), so Tab follows the visual order bottom to top.
- **Coalescing by `key`** (`move`, `import-summary`, `demo-write-blocked`, `preset-already-active`, `unblock:<card id>`): a toast with the same key replaces the old one in place (same position, timer restarted) and is re-announced only if its text changed. Pick a key for any toast that can repeat.
- **Past the cap** a newcomer evicts the lowest-priority, oldest visible toast, **unless** that toast is `blocked`/`danger` or inside its Undo guard, **or outranks the newcomer** (a lower-priority toast never displaces a higher one; it waits). Otherwise the newcomer waits, and a `+N waiting` line (`text-xs text-fg-tertiary`, plain text rather than the spec's inert button: a button that does nothing is noise in the Tab order) sits at the top of the stack. Because that line is not live, the polite region says "N more notifications waiting." whenever the queue grows; each waiting toast is announced in full when it enters (slots free up highest priority first). An evicted toast was already announced when it appeared.
- **Timing:** `success` 4 s, `info` 6 s, `warning` 8 s, `warning`/`info` with an `undo` action 12 s; `blocked` and `danger` persist until dismissed or replaced. `durationMs` overrides it: the preset "already applied" notice keeps 4 s (#2124); the import notice keeps 8 s (and persists when it lists warnings), "Card archived" its shipped 5 s, and the demo refusal its shipped 5 s until #2109 decides, the preset notice 8 s (20 s for a `?preset=` link), and card-not-found 4 s. The join confirmation (`durationMs: null`) and a card-action failure (`danger`) persist. Every timer pauses while the region is hovered, while focus is inside it, and while `document.hidden`; on resume the **full** delay restarts. The pause is region-wide (one hovered toast holds them all). **Hover means a mouse:** the region listens to `pointerenter`/`pointerleave` and ignores `pointerType` touch and pen, because an emulated hover from a tap never ends and would freeze the stack.
- **Undo guard.** A toast whose action has `undo: true` cannot be dismissed by the timer, `×`, Escape or eviction for its first 5 s: `×` is `aria-disabled` with `aria-describedby` "Available in a few seconds" (focus is never dropped), and Escape is consumed without effect.
- **Announcements** go through two always-mounted `sr-only` regions in the provider, cleared and refilled one tick later so a repeat is announced: `role="status" aria-live="polite"` for `success`/`info`/`warning`, `role="alert"` for `blocked`/`danger`. They carry the title, the message and "`<action>` available." (or the owner's `announceText`), **never a list, a URL, a secret or a host**. The visible toasts are not live. Because the assertive region is always mounted, an e2e `page.getByRole('alert')` must be scoped to its surface.
- **Inside a modal, inline first, announce once (#1732, #1437).** An `aria-modal` surface traps focus, so a toast behind it is out of keyboard reach and its live regions may be suppressed. A surface inside a modal reports the outcome in its own live region and raises the toast with `announce: false` (visible mirror only). **The raiser names the specific live region that owns the outcome; never infer `announce: false` from a document-wide `[aria-modal]` check** (another open modal has no inline report, so the refusal would go unannounced). The default is announced. Adopters, each with its owning region: `CardBlockSection`'s unblock outcome (its `card-block-status` region), and a move refusal raised while the card sheet's Move popover is open (`data-move-popover`, whose inline `role="alert"` reports every failure; the popover stays open on failure), which replaces the double announcement § Move-blocked toast used to sanction. A new adopter adds itself here with its region.
- **Dismissal.** `×` on every toast, `aria-label="Dismiss notification"`, a 24 px target on desktop and 44 px at `<= 1024px`. Escape dismisses the **focused** toast only, at `useEscapeStack` priority **61** (`TOAST_ESCAPE_PRIORITY`), returning `false` unless focus is inside the region: harmless otherwise, and above the command palette (60), modals (40) and the card sheet (30) in the one case where it matters. Dismissing a focused toast returns focus to the element focused before the user entered the region (recorded on `focusin`), else the board grid (`data-board-scroll`). **Every removal path** (×, Escape, timeout, eviction, an owner's `dismiss(key)`) restores focus the same way when the removed toast held it, and the focus-within pause is recomputed from `document.activeElement` after every change to the stack: a removed node fires no `blur`, so never rely on one.
- **Placement.** `fixed`, `z-[55]`: above `BulkActionToolbar` (40), the grid legend (30) and modals and the card sheet (50), so a `danger` toast is visible over the modal that produced it. **Recorded departure: the region sits above the command palette, not below it.** The spec asks for both "below the command palette" and `z-[55]`, but the palette is `z-50` like every other modal layer, so the two cannot hold together; `z-[55]` wins because a `danger` toast must stay visible over the modal that raised it. While the palette is open, toasts remain visible and clickable at the bottom (the palette panel sits at the top, `pt-[12vh]`), and a click on a toast is not a palette backdrop click. Putting the region below the palette means raising the palette's own layer, a separate change. `BulkActionToolbar` publishes its height as `--bulk-toolbar-h` (`usePublishedHeight`) and the region sits at `bottom: calc(1.5rem + var(--bulk-toolbar-h, -0.5rem))`: 1rem from the bottom alone, 8 px above the toolbar when it shows. Desktop: bottom-center, `w-max max-w-[min(24rem,calc(100%-2rem))]` (the #1439 width rule: never `max-w-sm` alone on a `left-1/2` box). `<= 820px`: `left-4 right-4`, `max-w-[28rem]` centered, safe-area padding. `<= 640px`: full width between the gutters, the message clamps to three lines with the full text in `title`. The action sits on its own line and is a 44 px target at `<= 820px` (`inline-flex items-center` so the label is centered in it), next to the `×` rule at 1024. **A menu that opens out of a toolbar the region floats above outranks the region while open:** `BulkActionToolbar` goes from `z-40` to `z-[56]` while its Move/Assign/Priority menu is open, because those menus open upward into the space the region occupies.
- **One landmark, outside the chrome.** `<section role="region" aria-label="Notifications">`, mounted only while a toast is visible. It is a plain region, not a chrome landmark, so it is a recorded departure from the four-landmark cap (Part 1). On the board it renders through `ToastRegionSlot`, which BoardView mounts as the next DOM sibling after the grid and before `BulkActionToolbar`, so one Tab from the focused card reaches the first toast action; elsewhere it renders at the end of the provider. The region carries `data-keeps-card-sheet-open`, so a click on any toast never closes the card sheet (#1914).
- **A successful block raises no toast (decided).** Blocking replaces the header's **Block…** with the red banner, and focus moves to that banner (§ System signals), so the outcome is already visible where the user is and announced by `CardBlockSection`'s status region. Unblock gets a toast because the banner that said "blocked" disappears.
- **Motion.** Entry uses the shared `animate-fade-in` (120 ms, opacity plus a slight scale) with `motion-reduce:animate-none`; exit is instant. Both are recorded departures from the spec's 100 ms opacity-only fade in and out. No progress bar or countdown ring. Moving between a board (`ToastRegionSlot`) and another page re-parents the region, so its toasts remount and replay the entry fade; accepted as rare and cosmetic.

## Move-blocked toast (MoveBlockedToast)

Content rules for the move refusal. Placement, timing, stacking and announcement follow § Toasts: it is a `blocked` toast (key `move`), persists, and is announced assertively.

- **Use `MoveBlockedToast` for all card move constraint violations** — WIP limit, weight limit, or any future column constraint. Never add a second toast for them, in `App.tsx` or anywhere else.
- **A failure shown by both this toast and an inline error states its cause once per surface and never contradicts the other (#1761, #1772).** `CardDetail`'s Move popover keeps its inline `role="alert"` message alongside the toast; both derive their reason from the same failure (`utils/moveFailure.ts`, `moveBlockedMessages.ts`). Because the open panel's focus trap (#1732) makes the toast unreachable from the keyboard, the popover is a limit variant in its own right (re-confirmed in #1914: the sheet no longer has a backdrop when anchored, and the toast carries `data-keeps-card-sheet-open` so a pointer click on it does not close the sheet, but the trap still keeps Tab inside the sheet): for the six limit codes (the three column codes and, since #1150, their `swimlane_*` counterparts) it adds a `text-fg-tertiary` numbers line (`limitCounts`, never repeating the column name; a swimlane code names the swimlane instead), and for the soft codes `wip_limit_exceeded` / `weight_limit_exceeded` / `swimlane_wip_limit_exceeded` / `swimlane_weight_limit_exceeded` it renders the admin override link "Move anyway (admin override)" (board `admin` or `site_admin` only) under the message, outside the live region, wired to `useBoard.forceMoveCardTo` with the popover's own target, never the shared `pendingMove`. No override for `wip_hard_blocked`, `swimlane_wip_hard_blocked`, `move_failed`, 403 or conflicts. In flight the link is `aria-disabled` and reads "Moving anyway…", the dropdowns, Cancel and Move are disabled, Escape is swallowed, and the header Move trigger ignores activation: every control that could dismiss a popover is inert while an action inside it is pending, so its outcome is never hidden. Changing either dropdown clears the message and the link. A successful forced move clears the toast, closes the popover silently and returns focus to the Move trigger; a failed one shows its cause inline and, if the link is gone, focuses the popover's Move button. The toast is not suppressed: it keeps its own override for when the panel is closed. Since #2060 it is raised with `announce: false` only while the sheet's Move popover is open (`data-move-popover`; the popover stays open on failure), so the popover's inline `role="alert"` is the one announcement; with the popover closed the toast announces (§ Toasts, modal rule); the double announcement this rule used to sanction as an exception (#1773) is gone.
- **Always amber** — border `border-warning`, glyph `text-warning`, title and action `text-warning-on-tint` (§ Toasts). Do not introduce a second color for a different limit type; severity is communicated via icon, not color.
- **`move_failed` is the catch-all variant (#1761).** Any move failure with no more specific code (network error, 5xx, 404, unrecognized 409/503) raises a `move_failed` toast: title "Move failed", body is the capitalized cause (`A network error occurred.`), amber ⚠ (never ⛔), no admin override. Drag-and-drop announces success in the sr-only status region only after the request resolves, never before; on failure that region is blanked because the toast's assertive announcement already carries the cause (one live region per action). A later successful move clears a stale toast.
- **Limit variants always show three things**: what was blocked (column name), why (with numbers), and an admin override link when `isAdmin` is true.
- **Admin override link**: a text action, `text-xs underline` in `text-warning-on-tint` (§ Toasts action slot; the popover's own link is unchanged) — never a button with background fill. While the forced move is in flight it reads "Moving anyway…" and is `aria-disabled`.
- **Hard-block variant** (`error.code === "wip_hard_blocked"`): use `⛔` as the toast icon instead of `⚠`. This is the only permitted way to signal severity difference between soft and hard constraint blocks — do not change the amber color. Omit the admin override link for all roles. Add a `text-xs text-fg-tertiary` resolution line: "To unblock, move a card out of [column], or ask an admin to raise the WIP limit."
- **Systemic vs. personal block reasons need a fixed, non-admin-authored title** (#1127). When a block is operator-imposed and instance-wide rather than caused by the current user's own state (e.g. `maintenance_mode`), the toast title must be a fixed sentence — not the generic hard-block title, and never left to the operator's free-text `detail` field alone to convey. A terse or generic operator message must not read as "you personally did something wrong." This mirrors the fixed-lead + operator-message pattern in `MaintenanceBanner` (see § Degraded-state site banners): `maintenance_mode`'s title is "The instance is temporarily read-only", with `error.detail` following after the em dash exactly as any other variant's body. This is a deliberate exception to the terse noun-phrase title vocabulary used elsewhere in this table ("WIP limit reached", "Cannot move this card") — do not shorten it to fit that pattern.

## Post-import notice (ImportSkippedToast)

An `info` toast in the shared region (key `import-summary`; placement, pausing and announcement per § Toasts). Informational tone (`bg-primary/15`, `ℹ`), never amber, because the import succeeded and there is nothing to act on, even when it reports dropped values (`import_summary.warnings`). A notice that lists warnings persists until dismissed (`durationMs: null`). The skipped-counts-only notice auto-dismisses after 8s (`durationMs`, not the 6 s `info` default). It now stacks with a move refusal instead of yielding the slot to it (#1438). The headline is the toast's title, so it renders `font-medium` in `text-info-on-tint` (a deliberate change from the old body-weight `text-fg`, per § Toasts). Any scrollable list inside a toast carries `tabIndex={0}`, an `aria-label` and the standard focus ring. The list is capped at 20 plus a server-worded "…and N more". The live region announces the headline and a count only, never the list.

## Collapsed sidebar rail

The collapsed rail (48px, `w-12`) is for **fixed destinations only** — Dashboard, admin utility links, and a small number of curated shortcuts. Never render an unbounded list of items directly in the rail.

**Rule:** When a section has a variable number of items (boards, groups, favorites), represent it as a **single trigger icon** that opens a positioned flyout panel. Trigger icons sit in the rail like any other icon; the flyout panel appears to the right of the sidebar.

**Flyout panel spec:**
- Rendered via `createPortal(panel, document.body)` to escape the sidebar's `overflow-hidden`
- Positioned with coordinates captured at click time via `getBoundingClientRect()` on the trigger — store as `{ top, left }` state, never a ref
- `position: fixed; top: anchor.top; left: anchor.left + 4` (4px gap from sidebar edge)
- Panel: `w-56 bg-surface border border-line rounded-lg shadow-xl py-1 max-h-80 overflow-y-auto z-50`
- Header row: `px-3 py-1.5 text-xs font-semibold text-fg-muted uppercase tracking-wider border-b border-line mb-1`
- Items: `px-3 py-1.5 text-sm text-fg-secondary hover:text-fg hover:bg-surface-hover transition truncate`
- Active item: `bg-primary/20 text-info font-medium`

**Toggle behaviour:**
- Click to open, click again to close
- Add `onMouseDown={(e) => { if (open) e.stopPropagation(); }}` to the trigger so the flyout's outside-click (`document mousedown`) handler doesn't close it before the `onClick` toggle fires
