- **A native card may carry one nested link — the MR/PR badge — and it follows the lens nested-link rules with drag-safety added.** The badge in `CardItem` is a real `<a href target="_blank" rel="noopener noreferrer">` inside the clickable, draggable card root. It stops propagation on `onClick`, `onPointerDown` (so dnd-kit never starts a drag from it) and `onKeyDown` (so Enter never reaches the card's key handling), and never calls `preventDefault` (cmd/middle-click must still work). Style: `text-info`, `text-xs`, the shared `ExternalRefGlyph`, `max-w-[10rem]` + `truncate font-mono` ref text, full ref in the accessible name (`"{ref}, {Provider}, opens in new tab"`) and in `title` together with the **real host** (`"{Provider} {ref} — {host} (opens in new tab)"`) — provider and ref are free text an editor chooses, so the tooltip is where a reader verifies where the link actually goes. It is the **first** item inside the `!compact` branch of the metadata row (the row clips right-to-left); at `comfortable` density it renders the glyph only, and the peek popover lists `{Provider} {ref}` as plain text — never a link, the peek is a hover tooltip. A glyph-only badge must never be the sole way to read the reference.
- **Every user-controlled `href` passes `isHttpUrl()` (`utils/externalRef.ts`) at the render site, even when the server already validated it.** When the guard fails, render the same content as a non-interactive `<span>` (`role="img"` + `aria-label` on the card face) — never an anchor with the raw value. An `href` is an XSS sink, and data can arrive from older rows or imports.
- **Icon-only informational glyphs on the card face are at least `w-3.5 h-3.5`.** `w-3 h-3` is for decorative glyphs that sit beside text; when the glyph is the only visible indicator (and the click target), step it up.
- **Immediate destructive text buttons get a real hit area.** A bare-text action that commits with no confirm (e.g. the link section's Remove) carries at least `px-1.5 py-0.5` and sits `gap-3` from adjacent actions, so reaching for its neighbor does not mis-hit it.
- **No brand logos for external providers.** One generic PR glyph for GitHub, GitLab and Other; the provider is named in text (detail panel) and in the accessible name. Logos would not track the theme color and add trademark/asset burden.

## Multi-step import wizards and file uploads (#456)

Established by the Trello import wizard (`components/Board/TrelloImportModal.tsx`, `components/Board/trello/`). Reuse these for any future import/creation wizard.

- **Wizard shell.** A multi-step flow inside `ModalWrapper` uses `maxWidth="max-w-2xl" noPadding headerBorder`, with `subtitle="Step X of N · {step name}"` as the only step indicator — no stepper graphic. Each step renders an `h3` (`text-sm font-medium text-fg-tertiary uppercase tracking-wide`, `tabIndex={-1}`) that receives focus on every step change, because the button that triggered the change unmounts; skip it on first open (`ModalWrapper` focuses the panel). Any step with variable-height content, **and the in-flight state that follows it**, uses the fixed panel height `h-[85vh] max-h-[640px] min-h-0` so the panel never collapses and re-expands mid-flow. Footer: bare-text Back on the left (`justify-between`), Cancel + primary grouped right with `gap-3`, `flex-wrap` for narrow widths. While a non-cancelable write is in flight the footer is not rendered and the modal is `dismissable={false}`.
- **One live region per wizard.** Exactly one `role="status" aria-live="polite" aria-atomic="true"` element, placed between the scrolling body and the footer (so errors stay next to the buttons when the body is scrolled). It holds an `sr-only` announcement span plus the visible error callout. Show an error **only** in the callout — set the sr-only text to `""` — or screen readers read it twice and RTL `getByText` finds duplicates. Callout tones: danger `bg-danger/10 border-danger/30 text-danger`; rate limits (429) use warning `bg-warning/10 border-warning/30 text-warning`.
- **A blocked primary action explains itself.** When the primary button is blocked by a field that may be scrolled out of view, use `aria-disabled` (with `aria-disabled:opacity-40 aria-disabled:cursor-not-allowed`) instead of `disabled`, point `aria-describedby` at an `sr-only` reason, and on click focus the offending field instead of submitting.
- **Upload progress bar.** Track `h-2 rounded-full bg-sunken overflow-hidden`; fill `h-full bg-button-primary transition-[width] motion-reduce:transition-none`; the track carries `role="progressbar"`, `aria-label`, `aria-valuemin/max`, `aria-valuenow`. At 100% the bytes are sent but the server is still working, so switch to indeterminate — omit `aria-valuenow`, full-width fill with `animate-pulse` (under `motion-reduce`, a static one-third fill — never full; see § Motion and reduced motion above and the Reduced motion bullet in § Import Board sample gallery) — and change the paired text (and the busy button label) from "Uploading… N%" to the server-phase copy. Announce progress through the live region in 25% buckets only. An in-button spinner on a primary fill uses `border-on-primary border-t-transparent` (the `border-primary` spinner in § Loading assumes a neutral background).
- **Dropzone-as-button.** A file dropzone is one full-width `<button type="button">` (native Enter/Space) wrapping nothing but text, with a sibling `<input type="file" className="hidden">`. The field title is a `<p id>` referenced by the button's `aria-labelledby` — never a `<label htmlFor>` pointing at the hidden input. Style: `w-full border border-dashed rounded-lg p-6 text-center`; idle `border-line-strong hover:border-line-emphasis`, drag-over `border-primary-emphasis bg-primary-emphasis/10`; inner text spans are `pointer-events-none` so `dragleave` does not flicker. This is the **one sanctioned exception** to the `rounded`-only button rule. The button's `aria-labelledby` lists the title id **and its own id**, so the chosen file name stays in the accessible name ("File board.json"); locate it in tests with a regex, not an exact string (#1440). While busy use `aria-disabled` (not `disabled` — a disabled button stops receiving drop events) and guard the handlers. The dropzone **and the enclosing wizard body** both `preventDefault` on `dragover`/`drop`, so a stray drop never navigates the browser to the file. Reset `e.target.value = ""` after a pick so re-picking the same file fires `onChange`. Validate the file **type** on pick, show the message in the live region, and disable Continue rather than rejecting the pick. Never hard-code a server-configurable size limit as a blocking client check: at most show it as advisory copy ("25 MB by default"), and on a `413` show the server's JSON `detail` when present, falling back to generic "too large for this server" copy for a non-JSON proxy `413`.
- **Review-summary tiles.** `grid grid-cols-2 sm:grid-cols-4 gap-2`; tile `bg-sunken border border-line rounded-lg px-3 py-2`; value `font-mono text-lg` (`text-fg-secondary`, or `text-fg-muted` when zero — render `0`, never an em-dash, which reads as "unknown"); caption `text-xs text-fg-muted`, singular when the value is 1 (no "(s)").
- **Matched-user privacy in import previews.** An import preview shows matched existing Visiban users **only as a count** — never names, usernames, emails, or avatars — and lists only the unmatched external identities by the name in the uploaded file. The copy states that matching is limited to people the importer can already see. This is a security requirement (user-enumeration), not a style preference.
- **Focusable scroll regions.** Any inner `overflow-y-auto` region given `tabIndex={0}` so keyboard users can scroll it needs an accessible name (`<ul>`/`<table>` with `aria-label`, or a `div` with `role="group"` + `aria-label` — not `role="region"`, see the landmark cap) and a focus ring: `focus:outline-none focus:ring-2 focus:ring-inset focus:ring-primary-emphasis`.
- **Import options (#119).** Choosing what an import includes is a `<fieldset className="min-w-0">` with a `<legend>` styled as a field label (`block text-xs font-medium text-fg-tertiary uppercase tracking-wide mb-1.5`), a `text-xs text-fg-muted` line naming what is always imported (the fieldset's `aria-describedby`), and native checkboxes (the `EditColumnModal` idiom) in one `space-y-2 bg-sunken border border-line rounded-lg px-3 py-2.5` box. Mount it only once a supported file is chosen, and reset every choice on each new pick. An option that depends on another is indented `ml-6 border-l-2 border-line pl-4` and uses native `disabled` (so it leaves the tab order) while its parent is off; turning the parent off unchecks it, turning it back on restores the value from just before (memoize it — never reset a deliberate choice to the default). Each row has an always-rendered `text-xs text-fg-muted mt-0.5 ml-6 min-h-4` consequence slot wired with `aria-describedby` (rest copy, the short "… are skipped" consequence, or "Requires {parent}"), so a row keeps its height as long as its copy fits one line; the slots are not live regions — one polite `sr-only` status region announces only the parent cascade. Consequence-slot copy must fit one line at `max-w-md` (about 45 characters for a dependent row); if it cannot, reserve `min-h-8`. Under the fieldset an "Importing: …" summary line (`text-xs text-fg-muted min-h-8`) reads "Importing: everything" or lists what is selected, and is the primary button's `aria-describedby` (dropped while submitting). Never send contradictory options (a dependent `true` with its parent `false`), and send nothing at all when every option is at its default. Report what was skipped afterwards in an informational-tone notice (`bg-primary/15`, never amber — skipping was a choice, not a failure): only non-zero counts (cards, comments, checklist items, card labels, and movements plus activity entries as "history entries"), singular for one, at most three then "and N more". Its live region is an sr-only `role="status"` child, mounted empty with the text set one tick later (see § Post-import notice). The notice mounts bottom-center like `MoveBlockedToast` and must not render while a move error is showing (the warning wins). Give it an explicit width (`w-max max-w-[min(24rem,calc(100%-2rem))]`) because an absolutely positioned `left-1/2` element otherwise collapses to half its container at narrow widths. It auto-dismisses after 8s and pauses while hovered or focused, except that a notice carrying `import_summary.warnings` persists until dismissed (see § Post-import notice). This is a sanctioned exception to § Subordinate settings blocks' single-toggle limit and its `ariaDisabled` guidance: the dependents are a fixed, tiny set whose existence is the thing being advertised, the "Requires {parent}" reason is also in the always-rendered slot, and the parent's cascade announcement tells screen-reader users why they are gone from the tab order. Dim an unavailable dependent's label text with `text-fg-muted` on the label `<span>` only; do not put `opacity-*` on the `<label>` (it double-dims the native disabled control).
- **Neutral state pill.** A neutral status pill (e.g. "Archived", "Skipped") is `px-2 py-0.5 text-xs rounded-full border border-line text-fg-tertiary`. Never `text-fg-muted` on `bg-surface-hover` (see § De-emphasized controls). The state is always spelled as a word, never carried by tone alone.

## URL custom-field links (#1390)

Established by the `url` custom field type. `components/Card/CustomFieldLink.tsx` is the **one** renderer for a URL value — card detail (`full`), the card-face and swimlane-row chips (`host`), and the editor's `Open link ↗` helper (`action`). Do not hand-roll a second anchor for a custom field value.

1. **A link inside a clickable or draggable surface stops propagation and never calls `preventDefault`.** A link nested in a card (dnd-kit drag + click-to-open) or a swimlane label panel (double-click-to-edit) stops `onClick`, `onDoubleClick`, `onPointerDown`, `onMouseDown`, `onTouchStart` and `onKeyDown` from bubbling, so it neither opens the card, starts a drag, nor edits the row. It must not `preventDefault`: the link has to open, including cmd/middle-click. `CustomFieldLink`'s `stopPropagation` prop does exactly this; the MR/PR badge (§ Card-face external links) is the same rule applied by hand.
2. **External links use `target="_blank" rel="noopener noreferrer"`, an `aria-label="Open {host} in new tab"` (chip), `"Open {url} in new tab"` (full URL) or `"Open link in new tab"` (the `action` helper, whose visible text is "Open link ↗" — the accessible name must contain the visible text, WCAG 2.5.3), and only an `http:`/`https:` `href`.** Anything else — a legacy `javascript:` value, an import, a value from a newer server — renders as plain `text-fg-secondary` text with no `href`, never an anchor holding the raw value. The guard runs at render time (`normalizeUrl` + `isHttpUrl`) even though the server already validated the value. A pinned chip shows the hostname only (leading `www.` stripped) with the full URL in the chip's `title`. The link focus ring is `focus:ring-2`, not `focus-visible:` (§ Focus ring consistency).
3. **URL inputs normalize bare domains to `https://`, validate on blur and Enter only, and keep invalid text in the input without saving it.** `normalizeUrl` (`utils/customFieldValue.ts`) trims, prepends `https://` when there is no scheme (`localhost:8080` is a host and port, not a scheme), and **rejects** — never rewrites — any non-http(s) scheme. An invalid value shows its error in the always-rendered `min-h-4` slot under the input (`aria-invalid`, `aria-describedby`, `border-danger`), `onCommit` is not called, and Escape restores the last saved value. A server `400` is mapped to the same copy (`urlErrorFromServer`) in the same slot. The error is `role="alert"` and is the input's `aria-describedby` only while shown — the `Open link ↗` helper is never the input's description, and it is hidden while the input holds unsaved text (it links the saved value). A legacy value that fails the type check is edited through the same blur/Enter editor, never the per-keystroke fallback. No per-keystroke or debounced commit for this type, whatever `debounceMs` says. **A Save-button surface must listen to `onInvalidChange`**: clicking Save blurs the input first and the invalid text is refused, so a form that does not track it saves without the edit and closes as if it had succeeded. `EditSwimlaneModal` is the reference — invalid text blocks Save, keeps the modal open and refocuses the field; a server `400` naming the field (`'{name}': …`) is routed back into that field's slot.
4. **An invalid input keeps a danger indicator while focused.** The standard input focus classes (`focus:ring-primary-emphasis focus:border-transparent`) erase a `border-danger` exactly while the user is fixing the value. When an input is invalid, swap them for `focus:ring-danger-emphasis` and keep the border — build the class list conditionally, never by string-replacing a token out of a shared class string.

## Import Board sample gallery (#1452)

`SampleGallery` ("Start from a sample") sits above the dropzone in `ImportBoardModal`. It is named *sample*, not *template* (`BoardTemplate` is a different concept) and owns no import logic: it takes `onSelect`, so user-defined templates (#442) can add an entry point beside it.

- **Modal shell.** `max-w-[640px]` (was `max-w-md`) so two cards fit side by side; the panel is `max-h-[calc(100dvh-2rem)]` and the *body* scrolls (`flex-1 min-h-0 overflow-y-auto`, `px-4 sm:px-6`) so the header and footer stay fixed. Below 560px the grid is one column and the collapsed view shows 3 samples, not 4. Below ~672px of viewport the panel is the viewport minus the backdrop's `p-4`, so no separate "sheet" shell exists.
- **Fold budget.** At 1280x768 the collapsed gallery must leave the dropzone and footer in view (`e2e/import-samples.spec.ts` pins it). Cards are ~133px; any chrome added to a card or above the gallery spends that budget.
- **Cards.** A `<ul>` of `<li>`; the only focusable element is the card's button, whose `::after` stretches over the card (`relative` card, `after:absolute after:inset-0`), so a click anywhere selects it without a handler on a `div` (§ Click handlers need keyboard parity). The card shows the focus ring (`focus-within`), never the button. Text comes from the manifest; secondary text is `text-fg-tertiary` (`text-fg-muted` fails AA on the dark surface). Accessible names contain the visible label (WCAG 2.5.3): `Use this sample: {Title}`, and `Try again, {Title} sample` after a failure.
- **Includes badges render once when every sample is identical** ("Every sample includes" under the helper text) and per card as soon as any sample differs — derived from the data, never hand-toggled.
- **One Tab stop.** Roving `tabindex` (the focused card is `0`, the rest `-1`); the last-focused card is remembered. Arrow keys move in two dimensions without selecting, Home/End jump, Enter/Space select. "Show all" moves focus to the first revealed card (card 5, or card 4 in one column); "Show fewer" leaves focus on the toggle, and the tab stop falls back to the last visible card while the remembered one is hidden.
- **Focus is never stolen.** On open, focus goes to the first card (or **Retry** when the list is unavailable) only if it is still on the dialog or lost. A failed card moves focus to its **Try again**. An Esc-canceled load and **Change** return focus to that sample's card, expanding the list first if it sits behind "Show all".
- **Announce each message once.** The modal's one persistent polite live region (mounted empty) announces "Loading the {Title} sample." and "Loading canceled."; the card's "Loading sample…" is `aria-hidden` visual text. A per-card failure is its own `role="alert"` only and never reaches the submit error banner. Do not add a second `role="status"` for the gallery.
- **Other samples while one loads** are `aria-disabled` (not `disabled`, so the roving focus survives), and the dropzone stays usable: choosing a file aborts the request.
- **Abort for real.** A new pick, Escape, a file choice and unmount `abort()` the in-flight `AbortController`; a late response from an aborted request is ignored. Escape runs at `useEscapeStack` **48** (see the modal allocation list) and returns `false` when nothing is loading; the board-name input has no Escape handler of its own (#1973), or it would close the modal first.
- **Reduced motion.** The indeterminate bar pulses; at `motion-reduce` it is a static one-third bar, never a full one that reads as "done".
- **Unavailable list.** The grid is replaced by the neutral notice banner with **Retry**, the divider reads "Upload your own file" (no "or"), and an empty list counts as unavailable — the section never disappears silently.
- **Upload notes follow the dropzone.** The size-limit notice and the Trello link moved below the dropzone to pay for the fold budget; both are hidden while a sample is the source.
- **Next step.** A fetched sample replaces the dropzone with a "From sample: {Title} · ~{n} cards" row and **Change** (no footer Back); the import sends `shift_dates_from` so the board is not mostly overdue, and never a `name`, so "Imported: <title>" numbering still applies.
- **Dates (#1949).** A **Dates** fieldset follows the Include summary only when the import would shift: a sample, or a chosen `.json` whose top-level `date_anchor` the modal reads client-side (display only; the server reads the same key and shifts by default). It holds one checkbox, **Move dates to today** (on; unticking sends `shift_dates: false` and never `shift_dates_from`), with an always-rendered `min-h-4` hint slot, and an **Adjust dates** disclosure (`aria-expanded`/`aria-controls`, closed by default so occasional users never meet the date field) revealing a `type="date"` input labelled "Date the board around", defaulting to the browser's today with `max` today. The day is sent as `shift_dates_to` only when it differs from today; an unchanged or cleared field sends nothing and the server uses today (UTC). A raw anchored file with every default sends no options at all, keeping the two-argument `onImport` call. A new file or sample resets all three (checked, closed, today).
  - **The checkbox label stays static** ("Move dates to today") so its accessible name never changes mid-interaction; the hint slot is the one element that restates a custom day, formatted with the user's `date_format` (`formatDateStr`, passed in as the `dateFormat` prop), never raw ISO. The disclosure keeps the name **Adjust dates** (the wording the owner approved for #1949). The default path sends no day and the server dates it to today in UTC, so near local midnight "today" in the hint can be a day off from the server's; that is accepted because sending the browser's local today would be refused as "in the future" for zones ahead of UTC.
  - **A disclosure's `aria-controls` is set only while its panel is mounted**, never pointing at an absent id.
  - **A `type="date"` input's `max`/`min` is not self-validating.** `max` only limits the native picker; a typed out-of-range day is treated as invalid in the payload logic (the Adjust dates field shows `Pick today or an earlier day.` in an always-mounted `role="alert"` `min-h-4` slot, `aria-invalid`, `aria-describedby` only while shown, `border-danger` + `focus:ring-danger-emphasis`, and blocks submit with focus moved to the field). A cleared value falls back to the default, and the hint says so.
  - **An always-rendered hint slot whose copy swaps reserves the height of its longest variant** at the narrowest modal width: `min-h-8` unless every variant is verified single-line (the Dates hint is `min-h-8`, like `#import-summary`).
  - **The Import button describes the date choice too:** with Dates shown its `aria-describedby` is `import-summary import-dates-hint`.
  - **A chosen file over the 10 MB upload limit is never read client-side** for its anchor; submit refuses it.

## Threshold and status encoding

- **A color-tinted element with no visible text label of its own must also carry a glyph and text (WCAG 1.4.1).** Status pills and chips that already show a visible label (e.g. `GroupDetail` priority chips, invite status pills) comply as-is. The analytics heatmap (`AnalyticsView`) gives every cell a distinct-shaped `aria-hidden` glyph (`▲` High, `◆` Warning, `●` OK, each followed by the text variation selector `U+FE0E` so it never renders as emoji) plus `sr-only` level text, and renders a `Heatmap legend` list (`text-xs text-fg-muted`, outside the scroll wrapper, above the footnote). Do not rely on hue or alpha alone for a threshold, health, or status display that has no visible label. The heatmap glyphs are `text-[10px]` on purpose (verified #1892). The glyph is the sighted user's non-color cue for the level, so it is informative, not decorative; it is exempt from the `text-xs` floor only because it is a single `aria-hidden` shape (not text), sits on a `w-5 h-5` chip in the legend beside the visible level label that defines it, and is backed in each cell by the `sr-only` level text and the `title`. The exemption covers exactly that: one `aria-hidden` shape glyph with a visible legend. Any glyph that is letters or digits, or that has no legend, keeps the `text-xs` floor.
- **Health status uses inline SVG shapes, not glyphs (#1133).** The glyph allowance above (`▲` / `◆` / `●` with `U+FE0E`) is for the analytics heatmap's cells. Board and group health supersedes it for its own display: the shape comes only from `HealthStatusShape` (`components/Common/HealthStatusShape.tsx`, `viewBox="0 0 12 12"`, `w-3 h-3`, `aria-hidden`, `focusable="false"`, on `currentColor`): filled circle On track, triangle At risk, diamond Off track, hollow circle No status. A font glyph's advance width and baseline shift with the font and can render as emoji; the SVG keeps one box in every pill and radio card. Do not re-inline the paths.
- **Reconciling with #969 ("none of them a font glyph").** That rule governs magnitude ramps, where glyph advance width and baseline would distort step size. This is a threshold/health display, not a magnitude ramp, so text-variation-selector glyphs paired with text are acceptable here.

## Threshold marks on custom fields (#1122)

- **Shape and weight, never hue, on the card face.** A number or date card-field value at or beyond its threshold gets `ThresholdMark` (`components/Card/ThresholdMark.tsx`): a 12px inline SVG on `currentColor`, `aria-hidden`, `focusable="false"`, one chevron at warning and two at alert, mirrored for a "below" threshold. The value turns `font-semibold text-fg`. No `text-danger`/`text-warning`, no tint, no border change: § System signals reserves red and amber for urgency and priority. A font glyph (`›`/`»`) is not used, for the same baseline reason as `HealthStatusShape`.
- **Words always travel with the chevron.** The chip's `title` reads `{name}: {value} ({phrase})` and an `sr-only` suffix carries `, {phrase}`. The hover peek shows the phrase visibly (pinned fields on their own `card-peek-thresholds` line; non-pinned ones in parentheses on the existing fields line), and `CustomFieldEditRow` shows it under both the input and the read-only display. Phrases come only from `thresholdPhrase()` (`Alert: at or above $6,000`, `Warning: 3 days from today or earlier`): inclusive boundaries are worded "at or above/below", never "over".
- **The peek's `card-peek-thresholds` line is a deliberate, bounded second line.** It is an exception to the peek single-line rule (§ Card density, the hover peek paragraph, and #1308's one-line fields exception): the words are the accessible-to-sighted half of the chevron, so it wraps rather than truncates, and it is capped to the pinned marked fields only.
- **The card detail Key facts strip marks a pinned field the same way.** `buildKeyFacts` (`utils/cardKeyFacts.ts`) computes the level with `thresholdLevel()` and puts the phrase in the fact's accessible name (`{name} {value}, {phrase}. Go to {name}`), its static label and its `title` (`{name}: {value} ({phrase})`, as on the chip); `CardKeyFacts` draws the same `ThresholdMark` and weight. A new surface that shows a pinned number or date value takes the mark and the words together, or neither.
- **A quick-edit `aria-label` must carry the phrase.** An `aria-label` replaces the chip's text, sr-only suffix included, so an editable chip spells `{name}: {value}, {phrase}. Press Enter to …`. Number and date are not quick-edit types today, so this guards a future change.
- **A marked chip gains no `cursor-pointer` or other affordance.** The chevron is not navigation; only the existing quick-edit types are clickable.
- **One evaluation rule.** `thresholdLevel(def, raw, now, timeZone)` in `utils/customFieldValue.ts` is the only place that decides the level: inclusive, alert beats warning, numbers by the server's finite rule, dates by day offset from today in the viewer's timezone (blank = UTC, the same `todayInTimezone` fallback as due dates), a non-canonical server date (written by an old pod during a rolling deploy) gets no threshold mark until its next edit, as the upgrade notes say, anything unparseable unmarked. Never re-implement it in a component.
- **Card fields only (1.3).** Swimlane row chips, filter chips and the Summary view show no marks in this release; swimlane-field thresholds need their own decision on admin-only disclosure (#2217), and filter chips and Summary tables are tracked on #2221.
- **The settings block shows the legend** (`› warning, » alert, shown in bold on cards`) in its reserved help slot, replaced by the error when the draft is invalid; an invalid draft blocks Save.

## Bulk operations (#1733)

1. **A bulk operation reports partial failure and keeps the selection.** Every handler in `BulkActionToolbar` (Move, Assign, Priority, Archive, Delete) applies the fulfilled results to state, and when any card failed it shows `{failed} of {total} cards could not be {verb} — {reason}.` and leaves the selection intact so the user can retry (cards that succeeded stay selected too). It calls `onClearSelection()` only when every card succeeded. Never `Promise.allSettled` and then clear the selection unconditionally. (Staged lists such as the Board Settings "To be added" list follow the opposite, per-item pattern; see #1299 in § Inline error messages.)
2. **Name the real cause, in plain and number-neutral words.** Derive the reason from the response with `failureReason` and blame a limit only for the codes in `LIMIT_CODES`: "a swimlane limit was reached" for the `swimlane_*` codes in `SWIMLANE_LIMIT_CODES` (#1150), "a column limit was reached" for the column codes; otherwise it says permission, network, not found, changed by someone else or "something went wrong". A reason follows both "1 of 1 card" and "2 of 5 cards", so it uses no plural pronouns. Archive and Delete pass a 403-specific override ("you can only archive cards you created"); no handler hardcodes a cause. Mixed causes read "for more than one reason".
3. **The toolbar's message slot is a polite live region and the one place bulk results are reported.** It is a persistent `role="status" aria-live="polite" aria-atomic="true"` wrapper, always mounted, with the message swapped inside (conditionally mounting the region is unreliable for announcements). The message wraps to two lines (`line-clamp-2`) with the full text in `title`; "Working..." stays outside the region and is `aria-hidden`.
4. **Every bottom-centered `fixed` or `absolute` box sets an explicit width (#2071, #1439, #119).** A box centered with `left-1/2 -translate-x-1/2` has only half its containing block to shrink-fit into, so without a width it collapses (a toast into a narrow tall column) or overflows (a row of controls). Toasts use `w-max max-w-[min(24rem,calc(100%-2rem))]` (`ToastRegion.tsx`), never `max-w-sm` alone. `BulkActionToolbar` is a row of controls, so it uses `w-max max-w-[calc(100%-2rem)] flex-wrap justify-center`: natural width on a wide screen, a second row at phone width. Measured at 320px before the fix: 495px wide at x=-88, off both edges (right edge 407.6) with Move to... unreachable; after: 288px at x=16 (16px gutters), 86px tall. Never make it `overflow-x-auto`: the Move, Assign and Priority menus are absolutely positioned children and would be clipped. The menu anchoring switches at 540px, independent of wrapping (the bar still wraps up to about 563px, and wraps at any width while a partial-failure message is showing): at 540px and below the trigger wrappers are `static` and each menu is `inset-x-0`, so it anchors to the bar and spans its width inside the gutters; a `left-0` menu anchored to a mid-row trigger ran 39px off-screen at 320 (`max-w` caps width, not position). From 541px up the menus are anchored to their triggers and were measured inside the viewport. Group separators are `hidden sm:block`, so below 640px a wrapped row never starts or ends on a stray tick; from 640px up they show and the bar is one row unless a message wraps it. The wrapped height is what `--bulk-toolbar-h` publishes, so the toast region clears both rows. Pinned in `bulk-toolbar-viewport.spec.ts` (320, 375 and 1024: bar width at least the viewport minus the gutters on phones, one row at 1024, button containment, and each menu's box inside the viewport gutters) and `bulkActionToolbar.test.tsx`. Design debt, not fixed here: the close button's position when the bar wraps and 44px coarse-pointer targets (#2134).

5. **The toolbar gates on the client with the context menu's rules and names the cause (#2173).** `BulkActionToolbar` takes `cardActionCtx` from BoardView (one `CardActionContext`, never derived again in the toolbar) and reads availability from `cardActionEntries()`, so Move, Assign, Priority, Archive and Delete are disabled for exactly the selections the context menu disables them for. **Refused for every selected card:** the button is `aria-disabled` (never native `disabled`, which is for `busy`), stays focusable, carries the reason in `title` and an `sr-only` `aria-describedby` span, and its handler guard makes activation do nothing but write the reason into the status slot (an ownership refusal in the rule-2 wording: `2 of 2 cards could not be assigned — you can only assign cards you created.`; a role, maintenance or demo gate as its own sentence). The reason is not visible on keyboard focus by design (a native `title` shows on hover only; the `sr-only` description serves screen readers and activation shows it to everyone). Activation clears the slot and sets it on the next tick so a repeat is announced again, and when a role or maintenance gate and ownership both apply the slot names the gate the button's `title` names (the first one `cardActionEntries` reports). **Delete is gated the same way** (and keeps its demo-mode behavior, which wins): `CardDeleteConfirm` takes the `CardActionContext` as `actionContext` and, for a mixed set, does not send the cards `cardRefusal(card, 'delete', ctx)` refuses, counting them failed with `REFUSAL_CLAUSE.delete`. Without a context it sends every card and the server's 403 decides (it never skips on its own). There is no visible per-button reason line: that is the menu-item pattern, and a wrapping toolbar row has no room for it. **Refused for some:** the executors skip the cards `cardRefusal()` says the server refuses, count them failed, and add the ownership reason (`for more than one reason` when a server failure adds another); the client check only spares requests known to fail, and the server stays the enforcement point. **Lenient:** an unknown creator, assignee or current user is left to the server. The reasons are one number-neutral table, `REFUSAL_CLAUSE` in `utils/cardActions.ts`, shared by the toolbar and the context-menu and palette executors (`moveCardsToColumn`, `assignCards`, `setCardsPriority`, `archiveCards` take the context); never re-word a refusal per surface, and never copy an executor loop into a surface. Assign's clause says "you can only assign cards you created": the server lets a card's creator assign their own card, so "needs a moderator or admin" is wrong.

   | Action | Refused when (per card) | Reason clause |
   |---|---|---|
   | Move | assigned to someone else, not created by or assigned to the user | a card assigned to someone else can only be moved by its creator, its assignee or a board admin |
   | Assign | created by someone else | you can only assign cards you created |
   | Priority | created by someone else | you can only edit cards you created |
   | Archive | created by someone else | you can only archive cards you created |
   | Delete | created by someone else (the confirm skips them: `CardDeleteConfirm` takes the same context and does not send them) | you can only delete cards you created |

   Moderators and admins are never refused; the hosted demo visitor is widened for everything but Delete.
6. **The status slot's text is controlled by BoardView.** The toolbar takes `message` / `onMessageChange`; BoardView clears it when the selection gains a card it was not written for, or empties, and keeps it when the selection only shrinks (a refusal describes the selection it was written for, but after a partial archive or delete the succeeded cards leave the board and are pruned from the selection, and clearing then would wipe the failure the user needs to read) and routes a context-menu or palette failure into it when the action's target set is the whole selection and the toolbar is up (`reportCardAction(…, selectionResult)`). No new context or store.

## Card context menu (#2059)

The 1.3 UI spec Part 9 § 2 menu for acting on a card without opening it. `CardContextMenu` renders it; `BoardView` owns the state, the target set and the executor, which it shares with the ⌘K palette's card commands (§ Command palette ownership). Availability comes from `cardActionEntries()` in `src/utils/cardActions.ts`, which mirrors the role gating of `BulkActionToolbar` and `CardDetail`; never re-derive it per surface.

- **A control that exists only at `pointer: coarse` must not depend on a state a tap cannot reach.** The `⋯` button shows on the selected or focused card; a tap on a card opens the sheet, so the touch route is selection (where a checkbox is reachable) or the card focus returns to when the sheet closes. That is documented in `docs/features/board.md`; a device pass (spec open conflict 4, tracked in #2104) decides whether `⋯` must show on every card at coarse pointer. The title takes `[@media(pointer:coarse)]:pr-14` exactly while the button can show, so it never runs under it. The context-target outline drops its `bg-info/10` fill under an aging tint, as the selection does (#1841).
- **Opening paths.** Right-click a card; `Menu` or `Shift+F10` on a focused card (anchored to the card's bottom-left); at `pointer: coarse` only, a 44px `⋯ More actions` button on the selected or focused card (never a hover reveal). **No long-press path**: a touch long-press is the drag start (#1287) and release-without-movement is unverified on device (device pass tracked in #2104), so `CardItem` ignores a `contextmenu` whose pointer type is touch. A keyboard-generated `contextmenu` (no pointer position) anchors to the card, and the one the `Menu` key fires right after its keydown does not open the menu twice.
- **The card owns right-click.** `CardItem` stops the event, and `BoardCell`'s `onContextMenu` also ignores any `contextmenu` from inside a card face, so the add-card shortcut fires only from empty cell space, in a populated cell too.
- **Target set.** A card inside the selection acts on the whole selection; a card outside it acts on that one card and leaves the selection unchanged, with a transient `ring-1 ring-info bg-info/10` outline while the menu is open (transient-open is info; persistent selection stays `primary-emphasis`).
- **Not over an open sheet.** No menu while the card detail sheet is open (`onOpenCardMenu` is withheld, so a right-click there gets the native menu): the portaled menu would take focus out of the `aria-modal` sheet, the same reason the selection checkbox is withheld (#1914). The palette acts on the open card instead.
- **Items and groups**, one engraved separator between groups: Open card (`Enter` hint) / Copy link to card · Move to… ▸ / Assign to… ▸ / Set priority ▸ · Block card… or Unblock card (state-aware) · Archive card / Delete card… (`text-danger`, `MENU_ITEM_FOCUS_RING_DANGER`, last). Several cards: a `text-xs` header ("3 cards") and Open, Copy link and Block are disabled with "Select one card". The submenus list the same choices as the bulk toolbar's menus (columns; Unassign plus every non-viewer member; the four priorities), from one source: `moveChoices` / `assignChoices` / `priorityChoices` in `utils/cardActions.ts`, used by the context menu, the palette and `BulkActionToolbar`; the toolbar's delete confirm is the same `CardDeleteConfirm` (#2103). Do not re-derive these lists in a surface. **Pointer grace (#2106):** moving the pointer from an open submenu's trigger row onto another row closes the submenu only after `SUBMENU_CLOSE_GRACE_MS` (200 ms); entering the submenu or the trigger again cancels the close, opening a submenu or closing the menu clears it. An open trigger carries `aria-controls` naming the submenu (a `useId()` id; omitted while closed, as it would point at nothing). Tests drive this with fake timers restored in `afterEach`.
- **Disabled with a reason, never hidden.** A disabled item is `aria-disabled`, stays in the roving order, and shows its reason on a visible second line (`text-xs text-fg-tertiary`, also its `aria-describedby`): "Viewers can't block cards", "Select one card", "Only the card's creator or a board admin" (for Assign, Set priority, Archive and Delete only when every creator is known and none is the user, otherwise it stays enabled and a refusal reports through the bulk failure wording; for Block / Unblock whenever the user is not known to be the creator and is not a moderator or admin, because those open the sheet's form and must match `CardDetail`'s `canBlock` exactly, or the sheet would open with no form), "Assigned to someone else: only its creator, its assignee or a board admin" for Move (only when every target card is known to be assigned to someone else and created by someone else, the server's `move_card` rule). Every PATCH takes the server's own-content gate, which is why Set priority is ownership-gated like Assign; no write the menu or palette offers as enabled is one the server is known to refuse, the maintenance line, or the demo copy (`DEMO_BLOCK_REASON`; Archive and Delete take `DEMO_CARD_FOOTER_NOTE`, following the demo card sheet, which withholds them; the bulk toolbar agrees since #2105: Archive and Delete stay visible but `aria-disabled` with the same note in `title` and `aria-label`, and the handlers no-op). Activating one does nothing and re-announces the reason through the menu's always-mounted `sr-only` status region. **The visible reason line is `aria-hidden`**, so it is read once, as the description, and not also inside the item's name; the palette's disabled rows do the same.
- **Keyboard.** `role="menu"` / `role="menuitem"` buttons with `tabIndex={-1}` and roving focus: Up/Down wrap, Home/End, a letter jumps to the next item starting with it (only while the menu has focus). `ArrowRight` opens a submenu and focuses its first item; `ArrowLeft` closes only the submenu. Tab closes the menu (focus to the card, then the browser moves on). An outside press, focus moving outside, a window resize or an outside scroll closes it (§ Modals and dialogs, anchored `fixed` popovers: `useAnchoredPlacement`, hidden until placed, focus after placement).
- **Layers.** Menu `z-[45]`, submenu `z-[46]`: above the bulk toolbar (40) and every board dropdown, below `ModalWrapper` and the palette (`z-50`). **Escape 59** (one entry: submenu first, then the menu), recorded in the modal list; it outranks the delete confirm's 40 only because the menu always closes before the confirm opens. Escape, Tab and choosing an item return focus to the target card, so a surface the item opens (the sheet, the delete confirm) captures the card as its opener (#1775).
- **Block / Unblock** closes the menu, opens the card sheet and sets `blockFormRequest`, so `CardBlockSection` focuses Reason (or Resolution). Cancel lands on the header's **Block…** inside the sheet, not on the board card the spec named: the sheet is `aria-modal`, and the board card is outside its trap. Closing the sheet returns focus to the card.
- **Delete card…** opens `CardDeleteConfirm` (same copy and ownership note as the bulk toolbar's confirm, `aria-disabled` "Deleting…" in flight, Escape swallowed); it never runs from one click.
- **Results.** Partial failure uses the § Bulk operations wording and keeps the selection; a full success on the whole selection clears it. Single-card results go through the shared toast region (§ Toasts, key `card-action`, #2107). **Every action leaves a result both a sighted and a screen-reader user can perceive:** an action whose effect is not visible on the board (Copy link, a delete that removes the card, "Deleted “title”.") is a `success` toast (4 s); announce-only success is allowed only when the board itself changes in view (Move, Assign, Priority, Archive). **A failure persists until dismissed or replaced** (a `danger` toast, #1772); only successes auto-clear. A blocked clipboard is a failure. BoardView keeps an `sr-only` status (`card-action-status`) for the announce-only case. A failure or partial result over the whole selection goes to the toolbar's status slot while the toolbar is up, not the toast (§ Bulk operations rule 5); a full success clears the selection, which unmounts the toolbar, so it keeps the announcer or toast, and a card outside the selection keeps the toast.

## Focus containment for modal surfaces (#1732)

1. **Any element with `aria-modal="true"` must use `ModalWrapper` or `useFocusTrap`, and restore focus on close.** `aria-modal` tells screen readers the background is inert; without a trap Tab walks out into the board behind it. Slide-over panels (`CardDetail`, `ArchivedCardsPanel`), the bulk-add overlay, the AppSidebar mobile drawer and the command palette call `useFocusTrap(ref, active)` on the `role="dialog"` element (`tabIndex={-1}`). Centered confirm/prompt dialogs use `ModalWrapper`. Never hand-roll a Tab handler (`AdminPage` `ConfirmDialog` did, and it drifted).
2. **`useFocusTrap` restores focus.** It captures the opener while rendering (a child's `autoFocus` has already moved focus by the time effects run) and returns focus there on deactivate or unmount, but only if focus would otherwise be lost (on `body`, or still inside a container that stays mounted but is hidden, like the mobile drawer's aside), so it never steals focus the user moved. Do not add a second restore in the component. When the opener has unmounted, restore falls back to `restoreTarget` and otherwise is skipped, so `OverflowMenu` moves focus to its own trigger before calling an item's `onSelect` (#1775): the surface that item opens captures the surviving trigger as its opener, and both `useFocusTrap` and `ModalWrapper` callers get focus back with no extra argument. `UserMenu` (a separate menu component) does the same in its Keyboard shortcuts handler before dispatching `visiban:open-shortcuts`; any new menu whose item opens a surface elsewhere must do likewise. Do not add a per-caller restore for an `OverflowMenu` item. `useFocusTrap` callers can still pass a surviving element as the third argument, `restoreTarget`, for any other opener that unmounts (a different menu component, or an item whose own handler removes the opener); `ModalWrapper` passes its `restoreFocusTo` prop through as that argument (#2204). A `ModalWrapper` whose confirm removes its own opener (a delete confirm opened from the row being deleted, or from an editor the delete closes) must pass `restoreFocusTo`, preferably as a function, which resolves at close after the same commit removed the opener: the custom-field delete modals (`BoardSettingsFieldsTab`, `BoardSettingsSwimlaneFieldsTab`) resolve the enabled `+ Add field` button (`data-add-field`, list footer or empty state) and fall back to the tab heading (`tabIndex={-1}`). Cancel still returns to the real opener, because the fallback is used only when the opener is disconnected. Pin it with a `document.activeElement` test.
3. **Nested traps are stack-ordered.** Only the most recently activated trap handles Tab, so a `ModalWrapper` confirm opened inside `CardDetail` does not fight the panel's trap.
4. **Every `role="dialog"` has an accessible name** (`aria-label` or `aria-labelledby`). The command palette is a portaled `aria-modal` dialog that keeps its inner `combobox`; non-modal popovers (`SwimlaneFieldsPopover`, `ChoiceColorPicker`, `ConnectionStatus`) are outside rule 1.

## Local input buffers and timers (#1319)

1. **Never seed a local input buffer from fetched or prop data in a `useEffect`.** An effect runs only after the commit that first shows the input, and it runs once on mount too. An edit that lands in that window is queued ahead of the late seed and silently overwritten by it. A loaded CI runner hits the window reliably; a user can hit it too. Seed in the fetch's `.then` together with the data (`EmailSettingsSection`'s `load`), or during render with the "adjust state when a prop changes" pattern: keep the last synced value in state and, when the prop differs, update both in the render (`AdminPage` `SettingsTab` maintenance notice, `CustomFieldValueInput`, `LensFilterBar`). Key the sync on the one field the buffer mirrors, never on the whole object, so an unrelated save does not discard an in-progress edit. A buffer component gets a regression test that types inside the window (see `docs/development/testing.md` § Flake-resistance checklist (Vitest unit tests)).
2. **A timer with a delay holds its id in a ref and is cleared on unmount.** A discarded id cannot be cleared, so the callback can fire after the component is gone (a ProfileTab save once navigated during a later test). ESLint `no-restricted-syntax` (in `src/components`, `src/pages`, `src/hooks`, `src/context`, `src/contexts` and `src/App.tsx`) rejects a `setTimeout`/`setInterval` call, bare or on `window`/`globalThis`, with a delay other than a literal `0`, when its return value is visibly discarded: the call is a whole statement, the expression body of an arrow (`.then(() => setTimeout(...))`), or the operand of `void`. Zero-delay hops (focus after render, resetting a ref) are allowed. It does **not** catch an id that is stored but never cleared (`const t = setTimeout(...)` with no `clearTimeout(t)`, or a ref whose unmount cleanup is missing, the #1304/#1305 shape), nor a call discarded another way (`cond && setTimeout(...)`). So review checks for the unmount cleanup, and the component gets a test that unmounts with the timer pending.

<!-- BEGIN #1150 placement limits -->
## Placement limits on the board (#1150)

The "Lane over limit" row of § System signals reserves the signal; this section defines it.

- **Swimlane limit state line.** A swimlane with a `wip_limit` or `weight_limit` renders at most one limit line under its name in the label panel (`SwimlaneLimitStat` in `SwimlaneRow`), worst offender first: Over WIP > Over weight > At > Under, the same priority as § Column headers. Under: `WIP {n}/{limit}` in `text-fg-tertiary`, `bg-line-strong` meter. At: `WIP {n}/{limit} · at limit` in `text-fg-secondary`, `bg-warning-emphasis` meter, shown only when the board opts in with `Board.show_wip_at_limit`, exactly as on the column header; off (the default), an at-limit row reads like Under. Over WIP: `⚠ Over WIP · {n}/{limit}` in `text-danger font-medium` (`⛔` when the row's WIP mode is hard), plus an inset `h-0.5 bg-danger-emphasis` top accent on the label panel, an absolutely positioned line so the panel does not grow. Over weight: `Over weight · {n}/{limit}` in `text-danger font-medium` with a `bg-danger-emphasis` meter. Both over-limit states use the fixed danger tokens § System signals reserves for "Lane over limit" (deliberately not the column header's `text-warning` for over weight); the words, not the hue, tell WIP from weight, and both always show, whatever the opt-in. The line always names the metric that produced the state (`laneLimitState` returns it): a weight-only row, Over weight and a weight exactly at its limit show the weight numbers. The text carries the state and never truncates (`shrink-0 whitespace-nowrap`); the meter is `aria-hidden`, `h-1`, `flex-1 min-w-0 max-w-12 lg:max-w-16`, and shrinks, to nothing if need be, before the text does. A collapsed row keeps only the two over-limit lines.
- **Blocked drop target.** While a card drag is over a cell whose drop would trip a block or hard limit (`utils/placementLimits.ts` `placementVerdict`, the client mirror of the server rule), the cell gets `ring-2 ring-inset ring-danger-emphasis` and the ordinary drop tint and the cell's card-count badge stand down. The ring is never color only: an `aria-hidden` label, `text-xs font-medium text-danger` on an opaque `bg-canvas rounded px-1.5 py-0.5 shadow` pill, names the strictest limit numbers first (`⚠ WIP limit 5/5 · Payouts`, `⛔` for hard), so a long name is what truncates. The ring only predicts; a refusal still raises `MoveBlockedToast`. Screen-reader and keyboard users get the same verdict from the drag live region (`Over column 'X', swimlane 'Y'. Swimlane "Y" is at its WIP limit (5/5), admins can override.`), and the drag-over throttle must never drop an announcement that carries a verdict.
- **Swimlane hard block in the toast.** The § Move-blocked toast hard-block rules (`⛔`, no override link, the resolution line) apply to `swimlane_wip_hard_blocked` exactly as to `wip_hard_blocked`; the resolution line names the swimlane ("To unblock, move a card out of {swimlane}, or ask an admin to raise the WIP limit."). Swimlane variants name the swimlane in the body (`Swimlane "{name}" is at its limit of …`). When one move trips several limits, the server's body names the strictest and the toast names the rest on a `text-xs text-fg-tertiary` "Also: …" line in the same toast, and in its assertive announcement (§ Toasts). Weight limits have no hard mode, for rows as for columns.
- **Edit Swimlane → Limits.** The Limits section sits after the Swimlane fields section and always renders. It holds the paired WIP / Weight fields per § Paired numeric settings fields, each helper slot reading `Now {n} · 0 = no limit` and switching to a `text-warning` "Already over: …" note when the typed limit is below the current count, worded for the enforcement currently selected (not enforced / admins can override / everyone), never promising a block that will not happen; a "Columns that count" fieldset of native `accent-primary` checkboxes (default: every column not marked done; an empty selection is refused in the helper slot, with `aria-invalid` on the first box and focus moved to it); and the enforcement radio cards per § Radio groups: **Follow board settings** (the default, `null`, with a "Board now: …" line stating both the WIP and the weight behavior), **Block, admins can override**, **Block for everyone**. There is no warn-only option (#2048). The modal body is one scroll region per § Modals and dialogs (#1455: `role="region"`, `aria-label`, `tabIndex={0}`, bottom fade) and the footer stays fixed.
- **Scroll regions in a dialog use `role="region"`.** Inside a dialog or popover, § Modals and dialogs rule (a) (#1455) governs: `role="region"` with an `aria-label`. The `role="group"` preference in "Focusable scroll regions" (§ Multi-step import wizards) applies to in-page scroll containers, not to a dialog's body.
<!-- END #1150 placement limits -->

## Charts (#448, #2028)

Every chart is hand-rolled SVG on the shared primitive, `components/Common/Chart.tsx` with `utils/chartScale.ts`: `ChartFrame`, `ChartPanel`, `ChartReferenceLines`, `ChartPointHit`/`ChartBandHit`, `ChartTable`. No chart library, and no second set of axes. #1774's `series` panel renders through it too, so keep it free of flow-metric knowledge.

- **One hue with alpha steps.** Strokes and fills are `CHART_HUE` and `chartTint(alpha)` (the `primary-emphasis` token). No categorical palette, and no status colors: red, amber and green stay reserved for system signals.
- **Color is never the only carrier.** Bands get 1px separators and a direct label at their right edge. Overlapping lines get a dash pattern from one vocabulary: 50th percentile dashed, 85th solid and heavier, 95th dotted, medians dashed. The bottleneck band is hatched (`<pattern>`). A blocked card is a square, a card past P85 is ringed, a reopened sample is hollow. When a shape means something, say so in a `ChartPanel` note, and only when that shape is on screen.
- **Direct labels, in the margin.** Reference lines are labelled in the right margin (`REFERENCE_LABEL_MARGIN`), outside the plot, so a label cannot overprint a mark; labels of nearly equal lines spread with `spreadLabels`.
- **Text stays 12px.** `ChartFrame` measures its width and keeps the `viewBox` 1:1 with pixels. A fixed `viewBox` scaled into a narrow panel would shrink axis text below the `text-xs` floor. Axis text is `fill-fg-tertiary`, labels `fill-fg-secondary`, never `fg-muted`.
- **The server owns percentiles.** Draw what the API returns. A `null` draws no line and a note that names no number ("Too few cards to draw percentile lines."). Never compute a percentile in the browser.
- **Two layers.** The `role="img"` SVG holds read-only marks and is named by the panel's heading and summary. Interactive marks live in a sibling SVG, because `img` has presentational children and assistive technology may not reach a button inside it.
- **Hit targets are 24px whatever the mark's size,** with a ring on hover and focus. Marks are `tabIndex={-1}` by default: a chart can hold hundreds, so the table is the complete keyboard path. Only marks a design names as tab stops pass `tabbable` (the aging dots).
- **Every chart has a table.** Pinned first column, `font-mono` numerics, `—` for null and zero per § Summary and analytics table layout, scrolling in a focusable `role="region"`. Each drill-down mark has a button in its row.
- **Layout.** Two columns from `lg` (1024px), one below; a frame never grows past 760px.

The Analytics tab scrolls as one page since #448: it no longer pins the heatmap, so § Analytics / split-panel scroll layout does not apply to it. Dropdowns on that page pass `portalMenu`, because the page is a scrolling ancestor. `AnalyticsView` has one return, with the heatmap's states inside one slot: a child that fetches on mount must not change position when a sibling changes state, or React remounts it. Here a remount was four history replays and lost every filter.

## Locked member rows and assignee pickers (#2164, #1905)

- **Locked member rows.** When the server refuses to change a membership (a site admin's membership, viewed by an admin who is not a site admin), the board and group Members panels drop both the role picker and Remove from the DOM and show the read-only role or Full access chip. `LOCKED_MEMBERSHIP_NOTE` (`src/constants/membership.ts`) is the one shared explanation. The group panel shows it as visible text under the name on every locked row, and as the `title` of the plain role text. Never write a second copy of the string.
- **Assignee pickers** filter the roster with `isAssignableMember` (`src/utils/assignableMembers.ts`), which mirrors the server's `_get_assignable_member_ids`. Never filter with an inline `role !== "viewer"`, because that offers out-of-enum rows the server refuses. Filter and history dropdowns (FilterBar, MovementHistoryView) are exempt: they list existing data, not new assignments.

## Card detail tabs and the History type filter (#967)

- **Tabs are Details · Comments · History**, a full tablist (roving `tabIndex`, Left/Right/Home/End with wrap, `focus:ring-inset` for the sheet's clip). A tab with a count shows it in a `ml-1 font-normal text-fg-tertiary` span and gets an explicit `aria-label` of `"<Label>, <count>"`: a visually hidden comma span is padded with spaces by name computation (Chrome and jsdom both give "Comments , 4"). Details has no count. **While a count is unknown, the tab shows its label only, never `0`.**
- **Counts come from data the sheet already fetches**, never a new `CardSerializer` field: Comments from the loaded comment list, History from the first timeline page CardDetail fetches on open with `include_counts=true` (summed `counts`). That page is refetched on `refreshSignal`, on `card.version` change and after this session posts a comment, and is passed to `ActivityTabPanel` as `initialPage` so History opens with no second request.
- **Which tab opens is session memory** (`cardDetailTabMemory.ts`, module scope): last chosen, kept across swaps and closes, never written to storage. A deep link `?card=…&tab=details|comments|history` overrides it (BoardView consumes and removes `tab`); an unknown value is ignored silently. A programmatic switch (Block… selecting Details) does not change the memory.
- **Details ends with the Last activity line**: one `text-xs text-fg-tertiary` button, "Last activity: {who} {what} · {relative time}", whose name ends with a visually hidden ". Go to History". It selects History and focuses the newest entry (`li` with `tabIndex={-1}` and a focus ring). No line when the card has no history or it has not loaded.
- **The History type filter is an independent `aria-pressed` group** (`role="group" aria-label="Show"`), not a tablist and not a menu: one text button per timeline group with `ActiveMark` and its count from `counts` (no number while unknown). All start on. The last button that is on is `aria-disabled`, with `title` and `aria-describedby` "At least one type stays shown", and its handler ignores activation. The group is data-driven (`EVENT_TYPE_OPTIONS`) so a later group (Readiness, #1922) is one entry.
- **The comment composer is pinned to the bottom of the Comments tab** (`sticky`, `bg-surface`, `border-t border-line`), so a note can be written without scrolling the thread. **A control pinned to the edge of a scroll body that has an edge fade must suppress that fade** (or sit above it with a divider): the body's bottom fade is not drawn on Comments when the composer is shown. Posting scrolls the new comment into view; switching tabs resets the body scroll to the top.
- **A filter that changes a list announces the new total** through an always-mounted polite status ("12 events"), and compact toggle chips use at least `py-1`. A toggle chip with a count gets `aria-label` "<Label>, <count>", like the tabs.
- **A text-only link-like button in the sheet** (the Last activity line) has a persistent non-color cue (`underline decoration-dotted underline-offset-2`) and `py-1`, and wraps rather than truncating, so its end (the time) stays visible.

## Destructive action in an editor footer; form error slot; locked attributes (#2194)

- **A destructive action offered from an editor goes in the editor footer, far left.** Use `mr-auto` and the danger button style (`bg-danger-bg hover:bg-danger-bg-hover text-on-danger font-medium rounded focus:ring-danger-emphasis`). DOM order is Delete, Cancel, Save so Tab follows the visual order. It renders only for an existing record (hidden, not disabled, for non-admins and for a new one; disabled only while a save is in flight, a transient state and not a permission), never takes default focus, and opens the same confirmation as the row-level delete: one canonical delete confirmation per resource. Reference: custom field editors (`BoardSettingsFieldsTab`, `BoardSettingsSwimlaneFieldsTab`).
- **A form's reserved error slot is `min-h-4` with `role="status" aria-live="polite" aria-atomic="true"`, never a fixed `h-4`,** because refusal copy can wrap.
- **A locked attribute explains itself in visible text next to the control, never only in a `title`.** The reason is a visible `text-xs text-fg-muted` paragraph directly under the control, referenced by `aria-describedby` on a `role="group"` (or on the control). It names the reason and the way out and states the consequence. The string lives in one shared constant per resource noun (`typeLockedMessage`, `src/constants/customFields.ts`). `title` is a supplement, never the only carrier, because disabled controls don't fire hover or focus.

## Card detail key-facts row and section counts (#1916)

- **The key-facts row is header row 3**, between the title/breadcrumb row and the tabs, outside the scrolling body so it stays put. It is one `<ul aria-label="Key facts">` of `border border-line rounded px-1.5 py-0.5 text-xs min-h-6` buttons (the custom-field chip family, not a new pill exception). The facts are data (`buildKeyFacts`, `utils/cardKeyFacts.ts`): an ordered list of `{key, target, text, label}` where a later fact (Readiness, #1922, between due/urgency and the pinned fields) is one more entry and not a renderer change.
- **A fact that does not apply is not produced**, so nothing is reserved for it. Board-specific facts (deal value, stage) come only from pinned `show_on_card` fields that have a value; never from unpinned fields.
- **Every fact's accessible name ends "Go to <field>"** ("Assignee: Alex Brown. Go to Assignee"). The visible text is the value only; the glyph (▸, ⚑) is `aria-hidden` and never the only carrier of urgency (the text says "Due …").
- **A jump is switch-tab, open, scroll, focus, in that order**: select Details if needed, open a collapsed section, then (after that render) `scrollIntoView({block: "start"})` on the section marked `data-fact-section="<target>"` and focus its first control. The scroll is `behavior: "auto"` (instant) under `prefers-reduced-motion`, smooth otherwise. A new jumpable section needs only a `data-fact-section` attribute and a `KeyFactTarget`.
- **Read-only viewers keep the row.** A fact whose only action is an edit surface (the Column fact opens the Move popover) renders as a non-interactive chip, not a dead button, when the viewer cannot do it (`inertTargets`).
- **Overflow is measured, not truncated**: at most two lines, then a trailing `+N` button (`aria-expanded`, visible text `+N`, with an `sr-only` " more facts") that expands the row in place and becomes "Show fewer". The `+N` itself must land on line two or earlier. Re-measure on width change.
- **Section counts use `Label N`**, in the label's own span (`normal-case font-normal`), with no parentheses (except Custom fields, which keeps its existing `(N)`). Zero is shown ("Relations 0"). An unknown count (still loading, or the fetch failed) shows no number, never `0`. Counts come from the state the section already renders (a refetch keeps the last known count). The Checklist heading is owned by #445 and is not changed here.
- **Label-in-name and inert chips.** A fact's accessible name contains its visible text, first where it is the whole label (abbreviated or truncated text like "Due 11/20/2020" or "Deal value $4…" is never replaced by a different phrase); a truncated chip carries the full value in `title` and the name. A chip that cannot be activated is `role="group"` with the name, never a bare `aria-label` on a `span`. Fact chips stay 24px (`min-h-6`) on a phone too; they are a dense secondary row, and every jump target remains reachable by scrolling.

## Checklist item trailing controls (#445)

- **Always visible.** The checklist row's assignee and due buttons (`ChecklistItemControls`) are never hover-revealed: a second scoped exception to hover-reveal, justified like #371 (an occasional user must find them). Both are `type="button"`, carry the design § 5 accessible names ("Assign {item}" / "Assigned to {name}. Change assignee for {item}"; "Set due date for {item}" / "Due {date}. Change due date for {item}") and the standard focus ring.
- **Face suffix.** At most one suffix after `✓ 3/7`: `· ⚑ N` (overdue items, `text-danger`; outside Classic the flag is `UrgencyGlyph` (#871), under Classic the `⚑` text character, byte for byte), else `· mine N` (`text-fg-tertiary`). Derive both client-side with `checklistFaceSuffix` from `checklist_open_due_dates` / `checklist_open_assignee_ids`; never feed a checklist due date into `classifyCardUrgency` or the card border (A-Q9).
- **Assignee pickers** in the row use `isAssignableMember`, like every other assignee picker. The picker is a plain list of `aria-pressed` buttons (never `role="listbox"` with a button inside an `option`), marks the current value with `Common/SelectedCheck`, focuses the current value on open, and moves with ArrowUp/ArrowDown. A former member keeps the assignment and the button says "(no longer a board member)".
- **Read-only rows show values, not dimmed controls.** A user who cannot edit the item (viewer, or the creator-only gate) gets no `disabled` button: an empty assignee or due date renders nothing, a set one renders as plain text (avatar or date) with a `title` and an `sr-only` sentence. Never render a dimmed control the user cannot use.
- **Minimum target.** Trailing row controls are `h-6 min-w-6` (24×24px), so the glyph-only state is never narrower than it is tall.
- **One text tone per element.** Never stack two `text-*` / `hover:text-*` utilities on one element (CSS source order, not class order, decides the winner); build the tone string conditionally (`NEUTRAL_TONE` / `DANGER_TONE`).
- **Text-only face indicators need `sr-only` text.** The face suffix glyphs are `aria-hidden` and a sibling `sr-only` span carries the sentence ("1 overdue checklist item"); a `title` alone is not an accessible name.
- **Native date input commit/cancel.** Enter commits (by blurring), Escape abandons the edit; a cancel ref guards the blur that some browsers fire on unmount.


## Transient state cues (#2251)

A cue that appears in response to a user filter or setting change (for example "doesn't match lane filters" in the focus banner) renders inside a persistent `role="status"` element with only its text conditional; a live region mounted together with its content is not reliably announced. Keep the cue at `text-xs` or larger, mark separator glyphs `aria-hidden`, and add `shrink-0 whitespace-nowrap` so a truncating neighbor shrinks instead.
